Nach der Regel für wiederkehrende Fehlerklassen ersetzt ein Test gegen das echte Artefakt das Flicken weiterer Einzelfälle. Die Abhängigkeit kommt aus dem bestehenden Lock (gleiche Version, keine neue Quelle). |
| M1-08/15 | 2026-10-11 | Der Orakel-Test über die getrackten Markdown-Dateien verbietet nur Namen in Code, die der Parser findet und `code_parts` nicht (Orakel minus Werkzeug). Mehr zu finden ist erlaubt: Zellen jenseits der Kopfzeile, die ein Parser nach GitHub-Tabellen verwirft, und Code-Spans in HTML-Blöcken, HTML-Kommentaren und eingerückten Code-Blöcken. Die Fälle der Prüfer bleiben Vergleiche auf Gleichheit. `test_code_in_a_cell_beyond_the_header_is_allowed_extra_detection` hält den Auslöser als Fixture, `test_oracle_mismatches_report_a_span_code_parts_loses`, dass ein verlorener Span weiter auffällt. Die Dateiliste liest der Test mit `git ls-files -z`. | Red Team zu `1ac9333`, Befund 1: Die Zeile D-W-71 in `docs/DECISIONS.md` von `plan/spezifikation` und `publish/v1` hat einen senkrechten Strich in einem Code-Span. Ein Parser nach GitHub-Tabellen teilt die Zeile dort in eine Zelle mehr als die Kopfzeile und verwirft diese Zelle, Python-Markdown (Docs-Site) teilt sie nicht, und `code_parts` liest sie. Mit Gleichheit wurde der Test rot, sobald diese Datei mit M1-08 zusammenkommt, obwohl D7 dort richtig liegt. Die Fehlerklasse der zweiten Prüfrunde sind Spans, die das Werkzeug verliert; nur diese Richtung kann einen abgelösten Namen vor D7 verstecken. Mit `.split()` zerfiel ein Dateiname mit Leerzeichen oder Umlaut in der Liste (Minor 1 des Red Teams). |

## M1-09 Secret-Provider und `bothy secrets`

| Nr | Datum | Festlegung | Grund |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-09/1 | 2026-10-11 | Der Datei-Treiber entfernt genau ein `\n` am Ende und ein `\r` direkt davor; sonst bleibt der Inhalt unverändert. | `echo wert > datei` ist der übliche Weg, ein Geheimnis abzulegen, und ein Zeilenende gehört nie zu einem Passwort; erzeugte Geheimnisse sind ASCII ohne Zeilenende, für sie ändert sich nichts. |
| M1-09/2 | 2026-10-11 | Eine Datei, die nach dieser Regel leer ist, gilt wie eine leere Umgebungsvariable als nicht gefunden (`not_found`). Inhalt über 1 MiB (1048576 Byte) ist ein Lesefehler (`read_failed`). | Ein leeres Passwort oder ein leerer Schlüssel ist nie gültig; der Start bricht so mit klarer Meldung ab statt später im Treiber der Datenbank. Die Grenze hält eine versehentlich falsch verwiesene große Datei aus dem Speicher. |
| M1-09/3 | 2026-10-11 | Der Treiber `env` liest aus der Umgebung, die `cmd/bothy` einmal gelesen hat: `Options` hat dafür zusätzlich das Feld `Env`, `NewEnvDriver(environ)` schlägt darin nach, der erste Eintrag eines Namens gilt wie bei `os.Getenv`. Das ist die einzige Ergänzung der im Brief festgelegten Schnittstelle außer Hilfsfunktionen. | Die forbidigo-Regel (M1-07/10) und 02 "Konfigurationsladen" Punkt 5 erlauben das Lesen der Umgebung nur in `cmd/bothy`. Die Umgebung eines Prozesses ändert sich nicht von außen; jedes ungecachte `Get` liefert darum dasselbe wie `os.LookupEnv`. |
| M1-09/4 | 2026-10-11 | Der Platzhalter `<secrets.dir>` wird auch in gesetzten Werten (Datei, Umgebung) von `*_ref`-Schlüsseln durch den wirksamen Wert von `secrets.dir` ersetzt, andere Platzhalter dort nicht. Das ersetzt den letzten Satz von M1-07/14. | Der Brief verlangt die Ersetzung in Standard- und gesetzten Werten; Compose und Betreiber können so `file:<secrets.dir>/db_owner_password` schreiben. Mehr Ersetzung bliebe eine Schablonensprache ohne Nutzer. |
| M1-09/5 | 2026-10-11 | Ein Verweis `file:` braucht einen absoluten Pfad in sauberer Form (`path.Clean` ändert ihn nicht: kein `.` oder `..`, kein doppelter oder abschließender Schrägstrich) ohne NUL-Zeichen, im Lader wie in `secrets.ParseRef`. | `bothy config check` und der Provider akzeptieren so dieselbe Grammatik; ein Verweis, den die Prüfung durchlässt, scheitert nicht erst beim Start. |
| M1-09/6 | 2026-10-11 | Fehler zu einem ungültigen Verweis zitieren den Text nie; sie nennen nur ein bekanntes Schema (`file`, `env`, `azkv`, `awssm`) und die verletzte Regel. Gültige Verweise erscheinen vollständig. | Wie M1-07: Ein Geheimnis kann im falschen Schlüssel oder in einer falschen Spalte stehen. Ein gültiger Verweis ist Konfiguration und steht auch in `bothy config print`. |
| M1-09/7 | 2026-10-11 | Der Datei-Treiber prüft mit `os.Stat` (folgt Symlinks) vorab, dass eine reguläre Datei vorliegt, öffnet sie dann und prüft Art, Modus und Besitzer erneut an der geöffneten Datei (`File.Stat`, `os.SameFile`), bevor er liest. | Zwischen Prüfung und Lesen kann die Datei nicht ausgetauscht werden; eine FIFO würde beim Öffnen blockieren und wird vorher abgewiesen. |
| M1-09/8 | 2026-10-11 | Scheitert das erneute Lesen und gibt es einen Wert im Cache, liefert der Provider den alten Wert, schreibt eine Logzeile, erhöht den Zähler einmal und setzt die Lesezeit auf jetzt; der nächste Versuch kommt nach `secrets.cache_ttl`. Eine Uhr, die vor der Lesezeit steht, gilt als abgelaufen. | Eine Zeile je TTL und Verweis statt einer je Aufruf; der Alarm `BothySecretRefreshFailing` braucht nur einen Anstieg. Nach einem Rücksprung der Uhr (NTP) bliebe ein Wert sonst bis zum Einholen der Uhr gültig. |
| M1-09/9 | 2026-10-11 | `bothy secrets export` sichert genau ein Verzeichnis (`--from`, in Compose `_store`). Die Dateien von `secrets.signing_key_dir` kommen mit M1-32 ins Bündel, das die Signierschlüssel erzeugt (Brief M1-32, Abschnitt 2). Format: POSIX-tar (PAX) mit relativen Namen, Modus-Bits, uid und gid 0 ohne Namen, mtime 0, nach Namen sortiert, mit Verzeichnissen; verschlüsselt mit age an jeden X25519-Empfänger, optional ASCII-Armor. | Brief D8; vor M1-32 gibt es keine Signierschlüssel. Ein festes, nach Namen sortiertes Format ohne Besitzernamen und Zeiten verrät nichts über den Host und ist reproduzierbar. |
| M1-09/10 | 2026-10-11 | `bothy secrets export` lehnt fehlende und ungültige Empfänger (ein ungültiger nur mit seiner Nummer genannt, nie mit seinem Text) und Binärausgabe auf ein Terminal ohne `--armor` mit Exitcode 2 ab; eine bestehende Datei bei `--output` mit Exitcode 1, sie bleibt unberührt. Scheitert der Export, löscht er die Ausgabedatei, die er angelegt hat. Er schreibt nie Klartext, auch keine Temp-Datei. | Brief D8; ein halbes Bündel sähe aus wie eine gültige Sicherung. |
| M1-09/11 | 2026-10-11 | `totp_key` ist ein Katalogeintrag mit Erzeuger `pending`, bis M1-39 ihn baut (Brief M1-39); die Konfigurationsreferenz nennt ihn bei den Erzeugern späterer Etappen. | Jede Zeile der Tabelle "Geheimnisse" ist ein Katalogeintrag (Brief D6); das Format des Schlüssels legt M1-39 fest. |
| M1-09/12 | 2026-10-11 | In `--source` sind nur reguläre Dateien erlaubt; ein Symlink, ein Gerät oder eine FIFO bricht mit Exitcode 2 ab und nennt den Pfad. Dateien mit dem Präfix `.bothy-tmp-` in Ziel-Verzeichnissen sind Reste eines unterbrochenen Laufs und werden entfernt. | `secrets init` läuft als root über ein Verzeichnis des Owners und folgt dort keinem Link. Ein abgebrochener Lauf soll beim nächsten Lauf ohne Handgriff zum selben Stand kommen. |
| M1-09/13 | 2026-10-11 | `bothy secrets init` prüft vor dem ersten Schreiben: unbekannte Dateien der Quelle, ungültige oder fehlende `--owner` (Exitcode 2), ein `--owner` ungleich der eigenen uid:gid ohne root (Exitcode 1) und Konflikte erzeugter Einträge (Exitcode 1, alle Einträge genannt). Scheitert eine dieser Prüfungen, schreibt es nichts. Jedes Dienstverzeichnis des Katalogs entsteht, auch ohne Datei. | Ein halb abgeglichenes Volume wäre schwerer zu reparieren als ein abgelehnter Lauf. Compose bricht mit einem `subpath` ab, der nicht existiert. |
| M1-09/14 | 2026-10-11 | W3: Ruling L1 umfasst zusätzlich `secrets`, `healthcheck` und `restore`; `bothy admin ...` in 08 heißt `bothy setup bootstrap-owner` und `bothy setup issue-enrollment-code`. 08 ändert M1-37. | Empfehlung des Plans (PLAN W3); 02 "Befehle des Binarys" führt die Befehle schon. |
| M1-09/15 | 2026-10-11 | `secrets.dir` ist ein absoluter Pfad in sauberer Form, nicht `/`, ohne NUL-Zeichen und ohne `<`. Der Lader prüft die Regel bei jedem gelieferten Wert und beim Standardwert (`config.checkSecretsDir`); ein falscher Wert ist ein Problem von `secrets.dir` allein, Verweise mit `<secrets.dir>` melden dann kein zweites. `secrets.NewProvider` verlangt dasselbe für `Options.Dir`. Die zuerst gebaute Querprüfung `secrets_dir_clean_absolute` in `internal/wire` entfällt. | `bothy config check` soll ablehnen, was der Start ablehnt: Ein leeres `secrets.dir` machte aus `file:<secrets.dir>/x` den gültigen Verweis `file:/x`, `/` den ungültigen `file://x`, und ein `<` im Wert brächte nach dem Ersetzen einen Platzhalter zurück, den `Resolve` ein zweites Mal ersetzt. Als Querprüfung lief die Regel erst nach dem Ersetzen und erzeugte je Verweis ein irreführendes Folgeproblem (Befunde der Reviews von Task C und des Abschluss-Reviews). |
| M1-09/16 | 2026-10-11 | `bothy secrets init` hält für den ganzen Lauf eine exklusive, nicht blockierende Sperre (`flock`) auf dem Zielverzeichnis; ein zweiter Lauf auf demselben Ziel scheitert mit Exitcode 1. Plattformen ohne `flock` lehnen `init` ab. | Zwei gleichzeitige Läufe (etwa `docker compose run secrets-init ...` während `up`) löschten sich gegenseitig die Temp-Dateien und könnten einen erzeugten Wert durch einen zweiten ersetzen, was der Brief verbietet (Befund des Reviews von Task B). |
| M1-09/17 | 2026-10-11 | Der Fingerabdruck von `secrets init` (zwölf Hex-Zeichen eines ungesalzenen SHA-256) gilt nicht als geheimnisfrei: Er bestätigt einen erratenen schwachen gelieferten Wert. Die Ausgabe, die das Log von `secrets-init` behält, gehört nicht an öffentliche Orte; das README von `stack` sagt das. | Format und Länge legt der Brief fest (16 5.3 Punkt 5); erzeugte Werte haben 256 Bit Zufall und sind davon nicht betroffen. |
| M1-09/18 | 2026-10-11 | `bothy secrets init` mit leerem `--target` oder `--source` und `bothy secrets export` mit leerem `--from` oder `--output` sind Aufruffehler (Exitcode 2). | Wie M1-07/21: Ein leerer Pfad aus einer leeren Variable hieße still "keine Quelle" (ein Restore mit `--adopt-source` erzeugte neue Geheimnisse, statt das Bündel zu übernehmen) oder Standardausgabe statt einer Datei. Aus demselben Grund ist `--adopt-source` ohne `--source` ein Aufruffehler: Ohne Quelle bewirkt das Flag nichts (Befund des Abschluss-Reviews). |
| M1-09/19 | 2026-10-11 | Ein zweites `--owner` für denselben Dienst ist ein Aufruffehler (Exitcode 2). | Welcher von zwei Besitzern gemeint ist, rät der Befehl nicht. |
| M1-09/20 | 2026-10-11 | Aufruffehler, die erst `stack` erkennt (jeder Fehler mit `stack.ErrUsage`: unbekannte oder nicht reguläre Datei der Quelle, Pfad von `--source` oder `--from`, der fehlt oder kein Verzeichnis ist, `--owner` für einen unbekannten Dienst, fehlender `--owner` für `server` oder `migrate`, fehlender oder ungültiger Empfänger, Binärausgabe auf ein Terminal ohne `--armor`), geben Exitcode 2 mit genau einer Zeile `bothy secrets <unterbefehl>: <fehler>` ohne Hilfetext; Fehler der Befehlszeile selbst (unbekanntes Flag, ungültiger Flag-Wert, fehlendes `--target` oder `--from`, leerer Pfad, unerwartetes Argument, fehlender oder unbekannter Unterbefehl) zeigen zusätzlich die Hilfe. | Die Zeile nennt Datei, Dienst oder Empfänger; die Hilfe würde sie verdecken und hilft nicht, weil die Aufrufform stimmte (Abweichung vom Plan, der die Hilfe immer vorsah). |
| M1-09/21 | 2026-10-11 | `bothy secrets` ohne Unterbefehl ist wie `bothy` und `bothy config` ohne Befehl ein Aufruffehler (Exitcode 2, Hilfe auf der Standardfehlerausgabe); `help`, `-h` und `--help` geben die Hilfe mit Exitcode 0 aus. Außer den Pfaden von `--target`, `--source`, `--from` und `--output`, die Meldungen nennen, wiederholt keine Meldung von `bothy secrets` Text der Befehlszeile: ein unerwartetes Argument, ein unbekanntes Flag oder ein ungültiger Flag-Wert erscheinen nur mit ihrer Position (`argument 3 ...`), ein unbekannter Unterbefehl ohne Namen, und `--owner` nimmt nur Dienstnamen aus Kleinbuchstaben, Ziffern und `-` an, sodass ein unbekannter Dienst nie anderer Text ist. | Ein Aufruf, der nichts tut, darf in einem Skript mit leerer Variable nicht als Erfolg gelten. Eine ohne `--age-recipient` eingefügte age-Identität (`AGE-SECRET-KEY-1...`) darf so wenig auf das Terminal oder in ein Log wie ein abgelehnter Empfänger, den `stack.Export` nur mit seiner Nummer nennt (M1-09/10). |
| M1-09/22 | 2026-10-11 | `bothy config check` warnt mit und ohne `--secrets` auf der Standardfehlerausgabe vor jedem Schlüssel `*_ref`, dessen wirksamer Verweis `env:` nutzt; der Exitcode bleibt davon unberührt. | Brief D2: `env:` ist erlaubt, aber nicht empfohlen (16 5.2); die Prüfung ohne Auflösung ist der übliche Aufruf und soll den Hinweis ebenso zeigen (Befund des Reviews von Task D). |
| M1-09/23 | 2026-10-11 | Die letzte Zeile von `secrets init` ist der aufrufbare Export-Befehl mit dem `_store` des Laufs: `back up the secrets: bothy secrets export --from <target>/_store --age-recipient <age public key>`; ein Pfad, der kein einfaches Shell-Wort ist, steht in einfachen Anführungszeichen. Das weicht vom Wortlaut des Briefs D4 ab. | D8 macht `--from` zur Pflicht; die Zeile des Briefs ergab beim Kopieren Exitcode 2 und verleitete dazu, ein falsches Verzeichnis zu exportieren (Befund des Abschluss-Reviews). |
| M1-09/24 | 2026-10-11 | `secrets.cache_ttl` ist mindestens `1s`; `Options.CacheTTL` darf für Tests `0` sein. | Mit `0` läse jeder Aufruf das Geheimnis, und während ein erneutes Lesen scheitert, schriebe jeder Aufruf eine Logzeile und erhöhte den Zähler, statt einmal je TTL (Befund des Abschluss-Reviews). |
| M1-09/25 | 2026-10-11 | `secrets init` lehnt gelieferte Dateien von Datei- und Mustereinträgen ohne Wert (leer oder nur ein Zeilenende) mit Exitcode 2 ab und nennt sie alle; ein solcher Wert in `_store` bricht mit Exitcode 1 ab. Dateien unter einem Verzeichniseintrag (`offsite/`) dürfen leer sein. | M1-09/2 gilt auch für die Dienste, die diese Dateien selbst lesen (Datenbank, Sicherung, Grafana); ein leerer Wert in `_store` bliebe sonst als `kept` für immer (Befund des Abschluss-Reviews). |
| M1-09/26 | 2026-10-11 | `secrets export` bündelt nur Dateien mit Katalogeintrag; eine andere Datei in `--from` ist ein Aufruffehler (Exitcode 2), der alle nennt, bevor die Ausgabe entsteht. | Das Bündel ist die Quelle der Wiederherstellung, und `secrets init --source` lehnt jede andere Datei ab; ein Export des Volume-Stamms oder eines `_store` mit fremder Datei scheiterte sonst erst im Notfall (Befund des Abschluss-Reviews). |
| M1-09/27 | 2026-10-11 | Die Logzeile eines gescheiterten erneuten Lesens nennt nur `error.type`, nicht den Verweis; welcher Verweis scheitert, zeigt `bothy config check --secrets` (Runbook `bothysecretrefreshfailing` in `docs/observability/alerting.md`). Wartende Aufrufer desselben Verweises warten auf das laufende Lesen; der Kontext wird beim Aufruf geprüft. | Log-Attribute stammen nur aus der Liste in `docs/observability/signals.md` 5.3, die keinen Verweis kennt, und der Brief D3 nennt nur `error.type`. Die Treiber in M1 lesen lokal; ein Lesen, das Wartende nicht aufhält, bringen die Netz-Treiber einer späteren Etappe mit (Befunde des Abschluss-Reviews). |
| M1-09/28 | 2026-10-11 | `secrets init` gibt dem Volume-Stamm den laufenden Benutzer als Besitzer, nicht nur den Modus `0700`; `.gitignore` nimmt `infra/compose/secrets/` aus, und Fall B in `disaster-recovery.md` sagt, die entschlüsselten erzeugten Dateien nach der Übernahme zu löschen. | Ein fremder Besitzer des Stamms könnte die Kopien der Dienste ohne `--owner` lesen und den Modus zurückstellen; ein entschlüsseltes Bündel im Arbeitsbaum darf nie in einen Commit geraten (Befunde des Abschluss-Reviews). |
| M1-09/29 | 2026-10-11 | `secrets init` nimmt als vorhandenes Ziel nur ein leeres Verzeichnis (bis auf `lost+found`) oder ein Verzeichnis mit `_store`; sonst ist es ein Aufruffehler (Exitcode 2), bevor sich etwas ändert. | Ein vertipptes `--target` (etwa `/var/lib`) gäbe das Verzeichnis sonst dem laufenden Benutzer, setzte `0700` und leerte jedes Unterverzeichnis mit dem Namen eines Dienstes (`postgres`, `grafana`, ...); nachgestellt im Red Team. `lost+found` bringt ein eigenes Dateisystem für das Volume mit. |

## M1-12 PostgreSQL access, migrations and schema version

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-12/1 | 2026-10-11 | Role passwords reach PostgreSQL only as SCRAM-SHA-256 verifiers computed in Go: 4096 iterations, 16 random bytes of salt, sent with the simple protocol so pgx never caches the statement. | 4096 is the default of `scram_iterations` and the minimum of RFC 7677, so a login costs what it costs with a password PostgreSQL hashed itself; 16 bytes is the salt PostgreSQL generates. |
| M1-12/2 | 2026-10-11 | Role passwords must be non-empty printable ASCII (0x20 to 0x7e); the error names the role, never the value. | For those characters SASLprep (PostgreSQL, libpq) and OpaqueString (pgx) change nothing, so every client derives the key the verifier was computed from; `secrets init` generates base64url values. |
| M1-12/3 | 2026-10-11 | `pg.Config` renders every libpq setting in the connection string, also empty ones (`passfile`, `sslcert`, `sslkey`, `sslrootcert`, `channel_binding`, `require_auth` and the rest), drops fallbacks, Kerberos names and the parsed password afterwards, and sets pgx's exec mode, cache sizes and the pool lifetimes to their defaults, so a libpq service file (`PGSERVICE`) cannot change them. | pgx reads `PG*` variables, `~/.pgpass`, `~/.pg_service.conf` and `~/.postgresql/`; 02 "Konfigurationsladen" item 5 allows only `cmd/bothy` to read the environment, and a stray `PGSSLMODE` or `root.crt` must not change a connection. |
| M1-12/4 | 2026-10-11 | A zero deadline in `pg.Config` switches it off as in PostgreSQL; the configuration keys have `1ms` as minimum and `24d` as maximum (`database.statement_timeout`, `lock_timeout`, `idle_in_tx_timeout`, `migrate.lock_timeout`). Deadlines are sent in whole milliseconds, rounded up. | `bothy migrate` needs `statement_timeout = 0`; the server never may (02 "Fristen"). PostgreSQL stores the three as int32 milliseconds; rounding up keeps a positive deadline below 1ms from becoming 0. |
| M1-12/5 | 2026-10-11 | Every connection starts with `default_transaction_isolation = 'read committed'` and `TimeZone = 'UTC'`; `Begin` sets READ COMMITTED when the options leave the level empty. | 03 Grundsatz 11 and 16 7.2 fix the level, and goose writes its `tstamp` with `now()` in the session time zone. |
| M1-12/6 | 2026-10-11 | `Acquire`, `Begin` (acquire and `BEGIN` together) and `Ping` wait at most `database.acquire_timeout`. When that deadline passes while every connection is acquired, the cause is `db_pool_exhausted`; when the database does not answer or refuses the login, `dependency_unavailable`; both have code `unavailable`. An expired request context is `deadline_exceeded`, a canceled one comes back as the context error. | 02 "Fehlerarten" names `db_pool_exhausted` for "no connection was free"; a database that is down is a different operator signal. A client that went away is no server error. |
| M1-12/7 | 2026-10-11 | `WaitReady` and `pg.Connect` retry with exponential backoff from 100ms to 2s and full jitter; each failed attempt logs `database not reachable yet, retrying` with `server.address`, `server.port` and `error.type` (SQLSTATE, secret error code, `timeout` or `connect`), never the error text. The final error names host and port and wraps the last real cause; an attempt cut short by the end of the wait does not replace it. | Agreed with M1-10 (log keys of the catalogue, no foreign error text in logs, signals.md "Logs"); the final error goes to the operator on stderr. |
| M1-12/8 | 2026-10-11 | The tracer of the pool wraps otelpgx: it replaces `PASSWORD '...'` literals in the statement text with `PASSWORD '[redacted]'`, and the error of a failed statement, batch, copy or prepare with a PostgreSQL error that keeps severity and SQLSTATE only. | otelpgx writes `err.Error()` into the span status and an exception event, and PostgreSQL messages quote parameter values (`invalid input syntax for type integer: "<value>"`), which the parameter option alone does not keep out; the role statements of the bootstrap carry the SCRAM verifier as a literal, enough for an offline guess. |
| M1-12/9 | 2026-10-11 | otelpgx v0.13.0 has no option for semantic conventions and emits only the current names: `db.system.name`, `db.namespace`, `db.operation.name`, `db.query.text`, `db.collection.name` (COPY spans only), plus `server.address`, `server.port`, `user.name`, `pgx.rows_affected`, `pgx.sql_state` and `pgx.prepare_stmt.name`. | signals.md "Datenbank" asks to check the installed version; the span allowlist of M1-10 drops `user.name` and `pgx.*` before export. |
| M1-12/10 | 2026-10-11 | The migrator uses goose's PostgreSQL session locker with a poll every second for up to five minutes, a fresh locker per migrator, and the global goose registry switched off. | Two `migrate up` apply each migration once; goose keeps the retry state of its polls in the locker. |
| M1-12/11 | 2026-10-11 | The schema version is `max(version_id)` of the applied rows of `goose_db_version`, 0 without the table; `CheckSchema` and `Migrator.Status` read it with their own queries. | goose's `GetDBVersion` and `Status` take the migration lock and create the version table; a status or a start check must only read. |
| M1-12/12 | 2026-10-11 | Against a database newer than the binary, `migrate up` runs neither its steps nor goose; it prints `schema is newer than this binary; nothing to do` and exits 0. | upgrade.md "Rückweg": the older binary must not touch a schema of the newer one, also not through River or the instance row. |
| M1-12/13 | 2026-10-11 | Output of `bothy migrate`: `applied <version> <name>` with the version as a number and the slug of the file name, then `database version <n>, applied <k>`; status lines `<version> <name> <applied\|pending> <applied_at\|->` with the time in RFC 3339 UTC; `bootstrap: roles and grants are in place` after `--bootstrap`; `migrations ok` after `validate`. A configuration that does not load or bind is exit code 2, every runtime failure 1. | Stable lines for scripts on stdout (02 3.3); 02 3.3 counts configuration errors as exit code 2. |
| M1-12/14 | 2026-10-11 | `Validate` parses each file with goose's own parser through a provider over a driver that refuses every statement, requires `-- +goose Up`, and refuses `-- +goose ENVSUB` in any case and after any number of `--`, as goose reads it. | goose v3.28.0 has no public parser; with ENVSUB a migration would read the process environment at migrate time. |
| M1-12/15 | 2026-10-11 | `BootstrapRoles` runs in one transaction under a transaction advisory lock and is retried once when a concurrent bootstrap created a role first (42710, 23505); `BootstrapDatabase` refuses a connection that is not on the named database. | Two `migrate up --bootstrap` must not fail each other; the schema statements act on the current database only. |
| M1-12/16 | 2026-10-11 | `bothy config check --secrets` prints `secret <key>: skipped (read only by bothy migrate up --bootstrap)` for the `migrate.*` references and does not resolve them. | The api and worker containers do not get the superuser and owner passwords; the runbook command would otherwise fail in every server container. |
| M1-12/17 | 2026-10-11 | The module register gains the optional top-level section `migrations: {used_by: [...]}`: the package `migrations` is a component that uses nothing, which `wire`, `cmd` and the listed platform packages may use (02 7.1). | The brief asks for a component for the package; 7.1 allows new entries only, and the section is optional, so registers without it stay valid. |
| M1-12/18 | 2026-10-11 | pgtest names its containers `bothy-pgtest-<hex>` and labels them `org.bothy.test=pgtest`; Ryuk is disabled by `os.Setenv` in `Main` before the first testcontainers call. | A session-specific name would need an environment read in a product package (forbidigo); the label finds leaked containers. testcontainers has no programmatic switch for Ryuk. |
| M1-12/19 | 2026-10-11 | pgtest keeps one template per migration set (hash of names and contents), closes it for connections after migrating, clones it per `New`, and gives tests a pool of 4 with acquire 10s, statement 30s, lock 10s and idle in transaction 60s. Tests that change a role password do not run in parallel and restore it. | Templates make each database cheap; the deadlines leave room on a shared machine under the race detector; roles are cluster-wide. |
| M1-12/20 | 2026-10-11 | `TestPrivilegesAfterAllMigrations` compares the rights of `bothy_app` on every table in `public` with the list `appPrivileges` (all eight table rights including `MAINTAIN`); a table without a row fails. | 16 7.1 rule 3 asks for an expectation list per table; PostgreSQL 17 added `MAINTAIN`. |
| M1-12/21 | 2026-10-11 | `database.tx_retry_max` and `database.listen_reconnect_backoff_cap` are registered now, although M1-13 and M1-16 read them. | The brief asks for all `database.*` keys of 02 and 16 in this ticket. |
| M1-12/22 | 2026-10-11 | `services/server/osv-scanner.toml` overrides the license of `github.com/opencontainers/go-digest` 1.0.0 (pulled in by testcontainers for pgtest) to `Apache-2.0`. | deps.dev reports CC-BY-SA-4.0, which the package's `LICENSE.docs` grants only for its README and CONTRIBUTING; the code is Apache-2.0 (`LICENSE`). docs/quality/licenses.md item 3 asks for exactly this per-package override with reason. |
| M1-12/23 | 2026-10-11 | `golang.org/x/crypto` 0.56.0, `github.com/moby/go-archive` 0.3.0 and `github.com/moby/sys/user` 0.4.1 instead of the versions the new dependencies select. | `make osv` reports GO-2026-6354, GO-2026-6355, GO-2026-6253 and GHSA-mjcv-p78q-w5fw for the selected versions; the fixed versions build and pass the tests. |

## M1-10 Logging and telemetry foundation

| Nr | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-10/1 | 2026-10-11 | D1: with `telemetry.enabled = false`, with `OTEL_LOGS_EXPORTER=none` or with `telemetry.logs.exporter = stdout` the server logger writes JSON to stdout even when `telemetry.logs.exporter = otlp`; there is no configuration in which logs go nowhere. | Brief D1: logs are never silently lost. An operator who turns telemetry off still needs the logs of the process (`TestDisabledTelemetryKeepsStdoutLogs`). |
| M1-10/2 | 2026-10-11 | D3: traces and logs each have one in-flight budget (`OTEL_BSP_MAX_QUEUE_SIZE`, `OTEL_BLRP_MAX_QUEUE_SIZE`, default 2048) across the front queue, the SDK batch processor and the running export. An item takes a slot on entering the front queue and returns it after its export; an item without a slot is counted in `bothy.telemetry.dropped{bothy.signal}`, and every item of a failed export (spans, log records, metric data points) is counted too. The SDK queues are as large as the budget, so neither the blocking span batch processor nor the log batch processor (no blocking option, it drops its oldest record on overflow) ever overflows. Not counted: items still queued when `Shutdown` reaches its deadline; a failed last metric export at `Shutdown` is counted but never exported. | A front queue alone is not enough: the SDK batch processors collect up to one export batch before they export, so with a collector that never answers they would hold that many items more than the front queue before anything was counted, and the log batch processor would then drop silently. One budget per signal bounds memory and makes every loss before the deadline visible. |
| M1-10/3 | 2026-10-11 | `Deps` has a fifth field `Env` (the environment as `cmd/bothy` read it). `Setup` reads from it the protocol variables (`grpc` default, `http/protobuf`; `http/json` has no Go exporter and is a start error), `OTEL_{TRACES,METRICS,LOGS}_EXPORTER` (`otlp` or `none`, anything else a start error) and the two queue sizes (a whole number of at least 1). Endpoint, headers, timeouts, TLS and `OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES` are still read by the OTLP exporters and package `resource` from the process environment. | Like M1-09/3: forbidigo allows reading the environment only in `cmd/bothy`. The vendor packages read their variables themselves; in the server both views are the same environment. |
| M1-10/4 | 2026-10-11 | `Middleware` is a package function as the brief fixes it; it reads per request the state of the last `Setup` (trust of the remote parent, server logger) from an atomic pointer. Before any `Setup` a remote parent is not trusted and the access log goes through `slog.Default()` behind the allowlist filter. `serve` (M1-11) calls `Setup` before it builds the chain. | The brief gives `Middleware(next)` no telemetry argument; a snapshot keeps the request path free of locks. |
| M1-10/5 | 2026-10-11 | The log allowlist filter resolves `LogValuer` values and flattens groups (`slog.Group`, `WithGroup`) into dotted keys; a flattened key must be `attr.Allowed`, and only scalar values (string, number, bool, duration, time, `[]string`) pass, so an error or struct value is dropped even under an allowed key. The span exporter keeps only scalar attribute values as well. | One key form in JSON and OTLP that matches the catalog and the collector allowlist; a group must not smuggle in a key the catalog does not list, and an encoder would write the text of an error, which signals.md "Logs" keeps out. |
| M1-10/6 | 2026-10-11 | Span export filter: `tracetest.SpanStubFromReadOnlySpan(...).Snapshot()` rebuilds every span with only allowed keys on the span, its events and links, clears the status description and, on server spans, removes `server.address` and `server.port`. | The pinned SDK v1.47.0 has no supported span attribute filter (brief D5). otelhttp fills `server.address` and `server.port` of a server span from the Host header of the client, an open and client-controlled set; the status description is free text. |
| M1-10/7 | 2026-10-11 | A method outside the nine standard HTTP methods is `_OTHER` in the access log and on the duration metric and `HTTP` in span names (as the HTTP semantic conventions do). The view of `http.server.request.duration` keeps exactly `http.request.method`, `http.route`, `http.response.status_code` and `error.type` with the boundaries of signals.md 4.7, drops the otelhttp body size histograms and turns exemplars off. `error.type` of a 5xx response is its status code. | Metric attributes only from closed sets (17 rule 9, signals.md "Kardinalität"): otelhttp would add `server.address` from the Host header, and exemplars would carry trace ids and the filtered attributes. The body size histograms are not in the catalog. |
| M1-10/8 | 2026-10-11 | The OpenTelemetry error handler writes the constant message `telemetry export error` with `error.type` set to the Go type of the error, at most once per 10 s and only to stdout. `ServerErrorLog` maps every line of net/http to `http server error` with `error.type` `tls_handshake`, `accept`, `panic` or `other`. | Exporter errors quote endpoints and foreign messages; an OTLP record about a failed export could fail the same way, and a collector that is down would otherwise write a line per export. net/http lines carry client addresses (SEC-LOG-5). |
| M1-10/9 | 2026-10-11 | `NewPseudonymizer(ctx, keys, ref, rotation, clock)` and `(*Pseudonymizer).PseudonymWithPeriod` are exported besides the API of the brief; `KeySource` is the one-method interface `*secrets.Provider` satisfies. A principal id must be a UUID in canonical text form (any letter case); the period index rounds toward negative infinity. | `bothy telemetry pseudonym` needs the pseudonym and its period start without `Setup`, which would start exporters. privacy.md fixes the input as the lowercase UUID; another form would give a pseudonym nobody can look up. |
| M1-10/10 | 2026-10-11 | `bothy telemetry pseudonym <principal_id> [--at TIME] [--config FILE]`: output `pseudonym <hex>` and `period_start <RFC 3339 UTC>` on stdout; exit 0, 1 when the key cannot be read or is shorter than 32 bytes, 2 for a usage error, a principal id that is not a UUID or a configuration that does not load. Flags may follow the id. | 02 "Befehle des Binarys": 2 is a call or configuration error. Unlike `config check`, whose result is the check itself, a configuration that does not load is a wrong call here. |
| M1-10/11 | 2026-10-11 | Commands log through `otel.NewCommandLogger` on stderr: `config check --secrets` and `telemetry pseudonym` at `telemetry.logs.level`, `info` before the configuration is loaded. `secrets init` and `export` do not log. | Brief scope `cmd/bothy`: the same allowlist for every output. |
| M1-10/12 | 2026-10-11 | Registered configuration keys (M1-07 had not): `telemetry.enabled`, `telemetry.traces.sampler_ratio` (0 to 1), `telemetry.traces.trust_remote_parent`, `telemetry.logs.exporter`, `telemetry.logs.level`, `telemetry.pseudonym_key_ref` (chapter 17), `privacy.telemetry_pseudonym_rotation` (chapter 20, at least `1s`) and `instance.environment` (chapter 16), defaults from the chapter tables. `telemetry.dashboard_url` comes with the admin console that links it. | The period index divides by whole seconds. `TestConfigKeysMatchChapterTables` holds the defaults. |
| M1-10/13 | 2026-10-11 | `sloglint` (`static-msg`, `attr-only`, `no-raw-keys`, `context: scope`, verified with `golangci-lint config verify`) checks product code; tests are exempt. `platform/secrets` keeps its own constant for `error.type`, and `kernel/errs` constants for the keys of its `LogValue` group. | Tests log arbitrary keys on purpose to prove the handlers drop them. `platform/otel` depends on `platform/secrets`, and platform cycles are not allowed (`architecture/modules.yaml`), so `secrets` cannot import `otel/attr`. |
| M1-10/14 | 2026-10-11 | `attr.Allowed` is false for the resource key `bothy.server.role` and the metric-only key `bothy.signal`; both are constants of `attr` with their scope. `genai.ConventionRef` names the convention repository and its status as read on 2026-10-10 without a commit. | signals.md lists `bothy.server.role` as a resource attribute and `bothy.signal` only on `bothy.telemetry.dropped`. signals.md records no commit of the conventions, and the brief forbids inventing one. |
| M1-10/15 | 2026-10-11 | The span export filter also clears the trace state of every span, its parent and its links; propagation to outgoing calls keeps it. | The trace state comes from the `tracestate` header of the client, free text outside the header allowlist; a probe of the closing review showed it on exported links (default) and spans (with `trust_remote_parent`). `TestTraceStateNeverExported`. |
| M1-10/16 | 2026-10-11 | After the handler, the access log step writes the route it used back to `r.Pattern` of the request `otelhttp` passed on (`""` for `unmatched`). | otelhttp takes `http.route` of the duration metric from `r.Pattern`, and that value wins over the labeler; below an outer `ServeMux` with a prefix pattern and `http.StripPrefix` the metric showed the prefix while span and log showed the route of `RecordRoute` (`TestRecordRouteWinsOverOuterPattern`). |
| M1-10/17 | 2026-10-11 | A handler panic passes `Middleware` unchanged and is re-raised in the deferred call that caught it; before that the access log and the span record status 500 (or the status already sent) with `error.type`. The duration metric does not count a panicking request. | The recovery of 02 9.1 step 1 sits outside step 2 and answers the request; its stack must still show the panicking frame (`TestPanicKeepsAccessLog` checks both). otelhttp records the metric only when the handler returns; counting it anyway would mean answering inside step 2, which is the job of step 1. |

## M1-11 Lifecycle, listeners, health and shutdown

| Nr | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-11/1 | 2026-10-12 | D4: the runner listens for `SIGTERM` and `SIGINT` with a buffer of two. The first signal starts the orderly shutdown, also during the start: the start context ends, the components started so far are stopped through the plan, and the exit code is `0`. A second signal at any time ends with `3` without further steps. The end of the context of `Run` counts like a first signal. | Brief D4 and 02 "Shutdown". A signal during a long database wait is an operator stopping the process, not a failed start; `1` would make an orchestrator report a crash. |
| M1-11/2 | 2026-10-12 | `wire.Serve(ctx, *config.Values, Role, Deps)`: the typed configuration of M1-07 is `*config.Values`; a type `config.Config` does not exist. Each package binds its own `Config` struct (`httpserver.Config`, `wire.ServeConfig`, `otel.Config`, `pg.Config`). `Deps` carries the environment, stdout, clock, signals, version, the migration set (tests) and the module handlers. | The brief names `*config.Config` with the note "use the typed configuration type M1-07 created". |
| M1-11/3 | 2026-10-12 | The secret provider is built in `Serve` before any component, because `otel.Setup` resolves `telemetry.pseudonym_key_ref` through it. The start component `secrets` then resolves `database.password_ref` once, before the internal listener opens, so a missing or unreadable password file ends the start at once with exit `1` instead of after `startup.dependency_wait`. | 02 "Start" puts telemetry first and secrets second, and 02 "Ausfall einer Abhängigkeit" makes the secret provider fail fast. The provider itself resolves lazily. |
| M1-11/4 | 2026-10-12 | The line `server ready` carries `bothy.server.role` as the brief asks, but the attribute catalog of `platform/otel` keeps that key on the resource only, so the stdout JSON drops it and OTLP carries it on the resource of every record. The listener lines carry `server.address` and `server.port`, which the catalog allows on logs. | signals.md "Resource-Attribute" and `attr.Allowed` are the contract of M1-10; widening the catalog is outside this ticket. The role is still on every exported record. |
| M1-11/5 | 2026-10-12 | Stop step budgets: `0` means the step is bounded only by the rest of `shutdown.total_timeout` (readiness, pool, internal listener). A step that outlives its budget is left running and the plan moves on with the log line `shutdown step timed out`; a step error is logged as `shutdown step failed` and does not change the exit code; a panicking step counts as failed. Exit `1` comes only from a total that is spent. Steps of the same phase run in reverse registration order. The budget sum is checked when `Run` begins, over the steps registered by then; `wire.Serve` registers all its steps before `Run`. | 02 "Shutdown": exit `1` is reserved for a shutdown over the total. A hanging step must not hold the whole plan; the orchestrator's longer grace period remains the backstop. |
| M1-11/6 | 2026-10-12 | `wire.Serve` calls `MountRoutes` as start step 7 now, because M1-17 is on the base; with no enabled operation it mounts nothing. The public listener serves `httpserver.Public(mux)`: a request no route matches gets `404` with an empty body; a method mismatch also gets `404` until M1-18 brings the problem format. The fallback asks `ServeMux.Handler` for the pattern instead of registering a catch-all route, so the Semgrep rule `bothy-go-route-registration` needs no exception on the public side. The internal mux registers `/healthz` and `/readyz` with a `nosemgrep` comment that names R-AZ-22. | M1-17/7 promised that M1-11 calls the step before the public listener opens. A catch-all pattern on the public mux would bypass the route check the rule protects. |
| M1-11/7 | 2026-10-12 | Bounds of the new keys: `http.read_header_timeout`, `http.idle_timeout`, `shutdown.http_grace` and `shutdown.telemetry_flush_timeout` at least `1ms`; `shutdown.drain_delay` at least `0s`; `shutdown.total_timeout` at least `1s`; every `shutdown.*` key at most `1h`; `http.max_header_bytes` from `4096` to `16777216`. | A zero would switch the limit off in net/http or leave a step without its own budget. One hour bounds the sum, so it cannot overflow. Below 4 KiB ordinary requests with bearer token and DPoP proof no longer fit; 16 MiB bounds the memory one connection can bind. |
| M1-11/8 | 2026-10-12 | `bothy serve` writes its JSON log lines to stdout (the stdout exporter of 17, `otel.Deps.Stdout`) and the cause of a failed start, for example the hint to run `bothy migrate up`, as one line `bothy serve: <error>` on stderr. The log handlers keep no error text. | 02 "Befehle des Binarys" puts logs of commands on stderr so scripts can read stdout; `serve` prints nothing for scripts, and 17 defines stdout as the log output of the server. The stderr line is the carrier of the error text that INT-W2/7 left to M1-11. |
| M1-11/9 | 2026-10-12 | `bothy healthcheck` checks the URL itself before any request (scheme `http`, no user info, host `localhost` or a loopback address without zone) and quotes nothing of it in errors; the loopback client of `platform/egress` checks again at connection time. Exit `1` writes one line: `status <code>`, `timeout`, `connection failed` or `refused: <reason>`. Redirects are not followed (a `3xx` is exit `1`). The default `--timeout` of `5s` equals the Compose healthcheck timeout; a test reads both. | Brief D5. Error texts of net/http carry addresses; a class is enough for a probe. |
| M1-11/10 | 2026-10-12 | A listener whose `Serve` ends with an error other than the orderly close reports it through `Runner.Fail`: orderly shutdown, exit `1`. `/healthz` has no liveness check yet; the log tail, hub and River client report through `Fail` in their tickets (02 "Panics"). | 02 "Health": `/healthz` needs no own check because a failed core part ends the process. |
| M1-11/11 | 2026-10-12 | Ticket branch `feat/M1-11-lifecycle-health-shutdown` (name of the launcher instead of the brief) on the head of `int/m1-wave2`; this session does not merge it, the PR lane ports it. | The launcher's instructions take precedence over the boundaries of the brief for branch name and merge. |
| M1-11/12 | 2026-10-12 | Red team (verdict JA, four Minors): the total of the shutdown now counts from the shutdown request instead of the first stop step; the drain step returns at once when the public listener never opened; the telemetry step switches the logger back to stdout before the flush, so the last lines survive a flush that outlives its budget. The English addition in the German command table of 02 stays. | The first three are reproduced by `TestTotalCountsFromTheShutdownRequest` and `TestServeSignalDuringStartSkipsTheDrain` or follow from the code; the table row of `telemetry pseudonym` set the precedent of English text in that table while the spec is being translated. |
| M1-11/13 | 2026-10-12 | Scoped re-review of the fixes (verdict JA, two Minors and a note): a spent shutdown total is now an error of `Runner.Err` naming the total and the phase, so `bothy serve` prints a cause on stderr for that exit `1` too, and `TestTotalCountsFromTheShutdownRequest` checks the log line, the skipped step and that error. The order "switch the logger, then flush" in the telemetry stop step gets no test of its own. | A test would need an OTLP endpoint that accepts and never answers; the order is two lines with a comment, and the effect (lines on stdout after a hanging flush) does not change an exit code. |

Deriving the row from the generated file closes the class: the state cell and the type cell change whenever a ticket moves a key into the schema, and a literal row would break again. |

## M1-13 Unit of work with phase hooks and in-transaction events

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-13/1 | 2026-10-12 | The module of a hook, handler or event type is the path segment after the last `/internal/` of its Go package (`_test` suffix dropped); `wire` and `platform` yield no module, and `kernel` may publish events but owns no hook or handler. The unit-of-work tests register through the stand-in module `internal/platform/pg/internal/fakemodule`, which lies inside the `platform-pg` component of the architecture gate; the tests of `kernel/txn` store registrations through the unexported half of `SetHook` and `Subscribe` and test the derivation on runtime symbol names. | Product packages have exactly one `/internal/`, so the rule equals "segment after /internal/" of the brief; the last occurrence lets a nested fixture act as a module without a test-only switch of the derivation. A fixture below `internal/kernel/txn` would belong to no component, and `make arch` rejects such files. |
| M1-13/2 | 2026-10-12 | A subscription is allowed only when the publisher is `kernel` or is listed directly in `depends_on` of the subscriber; a module subscribing to its own events and a subscriber or publisher missing from the register fail the check. | The subscriber imports the publisher's port for the event type, and go-arch-lint allows exactly the direct `depends_on` edges; an own event needs no bus. |
| M1-13/3 | 2026-10-12 | `CheckTxnDirections` takes `wire.ModuleDeps` (module to `depends_on`); the golden test reads `architecture/modules.yaml` with `go.yaml.in/yaml/v3`. | `wire` has no vendor groups in the register, and `tools/archgen` is a `main` package; test files are outside the architecture gate, and no second YAML library is added. |
| M1-13/4 | 2026-10-12 | `wire.Modules` is the (still empty) container of module objects, and `wire.NewTxnRegistry` the one function every role calls; `bothy serve` (M1-11) wires it. | No role builder exists yet; one function for all roles keeps the golden file the list of every role. |
| M1-13/5 | 2026-10-12 | A retry starts at once, without backoff; the deadlock victim then waits for the winner's locks under `database.lock_timeout`. | 02 names no pause and no key for one; the lock wait is already bounded. |
| M1-13/6 | 2026-10-12 | The rollback uses the request context without its cancellation, bounded by `database.statement_timeout`; its own error is dropped. | A canceled request must still end its transaction cleanly; the attempt already fails with the better error, and pgx closes a connection whose rollback failed. |
| M1-13/7 | 2026-10-12 | The error after the last retry has code `unavailable` without an internal cause and without `Retry-After`; the HTTP mapping (M1-18) adds `http.unavailable_retry_after`. | 14.1 lists no cause for an exhausted retry, and this ticket returns only `kernel/errs` kinds. |
| M1-13/8 | 2026-10-12 | Exported besides the API of the brief: `txn.Options`, `ApplyOptions`, `Options.Covers`, `NewUnit`, `Key.Name`, `Registry.Hook`, `KindHook`, `KindSubscribe`, `ModuleOfSymbol`, `ModuleOfPackage`; `pg.DBTX` and `Exec`, `Query`, `QueryRow` on `pg.Pool`; `otel.SubscriberSpan`. | platform/pg reads options, state and hooks across the package boundary; there is no sqlc code yet, so `pg` defines the sqlc `DBTX` shape, and the pool must satisfy it without exposing pgxpool and its unbounded acquire. |
| M1-13/9 | 2026-10-12 | `Registry.SetTracer` may be called after `Seal`; `Publish` with an unsealed registry is an internal error. | The tracer changes only telemetry, never what runs; delivering before the list is complete would skip subscribers silently. |
| M1-13/10 | 2026-10-12 | `txn.RightsChange` names principals only; targets (space, channel) come with the authz hook of M1-29, which resolves the affected principals. | The brief fixes the signature `RightsChange(affected ...ids.ID)`; 02 "Ablauf" item 1 also allows a target, which needs the authz resolution this ticket does not build. |
| M1-13/11 | 2026-10-12 | `txn.Unit` is a struct of `kernel/txn`, not an interface `platform/pg` implements: `txn.NewUnit(reg, opts)` returns the unit and the only function that changes its phase; `platform/pg` keeps the transaction under its own context key and accepts it only together with the unit it created. `WithUnit` and `UnitFrom` take and return `*txn.Unit`. | The brief asked for an interface, but the deep scan of `make arch` (go-arch-lint) rejects passing a `platform/pg` implementation into `kernel` (`Dependency platform-pg -\-> kernel-ports not allowed`), and `kernel` depends on nothing (02 4.5). The phase setter stays with its creator, so code that reads the unit cannot fake the phase that `instance.Counters` (M1-15) checks. |
| M1-13/12 | 2026-10-12 | Findings of the red team (minors): `CheckTxnDirections` rejects a handler whose path holds more than one `/internal/`; the Semgrep rule `bothy-go-tx-only-in-platform-pg` also flags `Commit`, `Rollback`, `txn.NewUnit` and `txn.WithUnit` outside `internal/platform/pg`; the package documentation says that a module wrapper could still pass a foreign closure off as its own. A deadlock or lock timeout on a context that has already ended comes back as the raw database error, as the brief asks; M1-18 maps it. | The last-segment rule of M1-13/1 would otherwise let `internal/wire/internal/sync` act as sync in the product; `pg.Tx` hands out the whole `pgx.Tx`, and a commit or a unit built outside the unit of work would skip the hooks or fake the phase. |

## M1-15 Sequence counters and instance record

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-15/1 | 2026-10-12 | The ticket is built on `feat/M1-15-instance-counters` from `int/m1-wave3` (`ca30b63`) in its own worktree and is not merged locally; the PR lane ports it. | The launcher's task overrides the merge step of the brief ("Definition of done"). |
| M1-15/2 | 2026-10-12 | The phase rule lives in `instance/domain` over the phase names of `txn.Phase.String` (`rights_begin`, `sync`, `audit`); `app` passes `txn.CurrentPhase(ctx).String()`. Unknown names, `n < 1` and a wrong phase are kind `internal` before any SQL. | The domain layer may import only kernel-core; `kernel/txn` is a kernel port (02 "Schichten im Modul"). |
| M1-15/3 | 2026-10-12 | `State.RecordMigration` never lowers `schema_version` (`UPDATE ... WHERE schema_version <= $1`), and the instance step of `bothy migrate up` reads the goose version inside its own unit of work and records only when it equals the newest migration of the binary. | `Migrator.Up` returns before the steps on a newer database, but the steps run outside goose's lock: a newer binary may migrate between goose and the step of an older one. With both checks the older step never overwrites the newer lock (upgrade.md "Rückweg"). |
| M1-15/4 | 2026-10-12 | `wire.MigrationSteps(pool, fsys)` replaces `MigrationSteps()`; the instance step runs a unit of work on the pgx pool of `bothy migrate` and ignores the `*sql.DB` of `pg.Step.Run`. Its `UnitConfig` is zero (session deadlines of migrate, `lock_timeout = migrate.lock_timeout`, no retry) and its registry is `NewTxnRegistry(&Modules{})`. | The brief asks for a unit of work on the migrate connection, and units run on `pg.Pool`; `OpenSQL` wraps the same pool, so both use the migrate connections. |
| M1-15/5 | 2026-10-12 | `pgtest.Main(m, pgtest.WithSteps(wire.MigrationSteps))` migrates the templates of a test binary with the steps of `bothy migrate up`; `internal/wire` and `cmd/bothy` use it, so their databases have the instance row. Databases of one binary share the `instance_id` of their template. | `pgtest` is in `platform/pg` and may not import `wire` (architecture gate); without the row every serve test would end at the new start step. |

## M1-18 HTTP middleware chain and problem details

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-18/1 | 2026-10-12 | The request validator is `github.com/pb33f/libopenapi-validator` v0.16.1 (MIT, OpenAPI 3.1, JSON Schema 2020-12) with `github.com/pb33f/libopenapi` v0.41.3. The probe tests `TestValidator_Probe_*` pass on the first library: `additionalProperties: false`, `unevaluatedProperties: false` on an `allOf` composition, `format: uuid` (with format assertions on), enums, `minLength`, `maxItems`, `oneOf` with discriminator, header and path parameters; kin-openapi was not needed. The validator keeps only the operations the chain mounts (the model is pruned before the schemas are compiled), and an operation it does not know fails closed with 500. | The brief names the library and its fallback. Compiling every request schema of the 29 000-line contract took about 3 s on the build machine, against 5 s of `perf.startup_ready_max`, for routes that are not served; the unmounted operations answer 404 at the mux anyway. |
| M1-18/2 | 2026-10-12 | `platform/httpserver` has its own `Operation` and `Route` types; `internal/wire` converts `routes.Operation` with `HTTPOperation`. The harness `httpservertest` builds the chain around these operations. The validator takes the contract as bytes; `api/rest.Spec` embeds `api/rest/openapi.gen.yaml`. | The module register gives `platform` no edge to `api` (the generated layer), and the harness lives in the `platform-httpserver` component; a copy of six fields keeps the platform package independent of the generator. A test (`TestHTTPOperationCarriesEveryField`) holds the conversion and the equal enum values. |
| M1-18/3 | 2026-10-12 | FieldError `code` vocabulary of schema validation: `required`, `unknown_member`, `invalid_type`, `invalid_format`, `invalid_enum`, `too_long`, `too_short`, `too_large`, `too_small`, `too_many_items`, `too_few_items`, `pattern_mismatch`, `invalid`; use cases keep `limit_reached` and `not_enabled` of 06. `pointer` names the member itself also for `required` and `unknown_member` (the validator reports those at the enclosing object; the chain appends the member name). `detail` is a fixed English text per code and never repeats the value. | The brief fixes the list; 06 section 5 item 3 already names `limit_reached` and `not_enabled`, so the contract description lists both sets. |
| M1-18/4 | 2026-10-12 | `If-Match: *` on an operation that requires If-Match is `428 precondition_required`, like a missing header. A weak tag, a list or a non-decimal value is not valid for the use case, which answers `412 version_conflict` with `current`. | The wildcard would skip the version check the contract demands; a strong comparison of a weak tag fails (RFC 9110 13.1.1). |
| M1-18/5 | 2026-10-12 | Problem titles come from `x-problem-codes` of the schema `Problem` (`errs.Code.Title`, held by `TestTitlesMatchOpenAPI`), not from the title rule of the brief. | The rule (code with spaces, first letter upper case) disagrees with the contract for `cursor_invalid` ("Invalid cursor"), `use_dpop_nonce`, `invalid_dpop_proof`, `step_up_required`, `channel_read_only`, `title_manual`, `idempotency_key_reuse`, `rate_limited` and `unavailable`; R-API-23 makes `x-problem-codes` the one source of titles. |
| M1-18/6 | 2026-10-12 | Null repair: the response writer buffers `application/json` and `application/problem+json` bodies, removes object members whose value is `null` and logs `response contained JSON null` with `http.route` and `error.type = null_member`; a `null` array element, a top-level `null`, an integer beyond 2^53 - 1 or a body that is not one JSON value replaces the response by `500 internal_error` (`response refused`). Clean bodies go out byte for byte. The harness fails every handler test that triggers the repair. The JSON Pointer of the removed member is not logged. | ADR 0053 and 06 R-API-03. The log allowlist of M1-10 has no key for a JSON Pointer, and adding one needs signals.md and the collector allowlist (M1-10 scope); the route and the harness failure locate the handler. |
| M1-18/7 | 2026-10-12 | Unmatched requests (404, 405) carry no `instance`. | `instance` is optional in RFC 9457 and in the schema `Problem`; without a route the server cannot know which segments are secret (`{token}` of `postIncomingWebhook` is not mounted yet, and a later route could add another), and echoing a raw path could put a webhook token into a response (SEC-LOG-3). Matched routes write the path with every `{token}` segment of the template kept as `{token}`. |
| M1-18/8 | 2026-10-12 | A body on an operation without request body is `415 unsupported_media_type`; a missing required body is `400 validation_failed` with `errors[]` `{pointer: "", code: required}`. Multipart and octet-stream bodies are not read by the chain: their limit (`file.max_upload_bytes`) and transfer deadline apply while the handler streams, and their parameters are validated. | SEC-IN-1 rejects what the contract does not describe; reading an upload into memory would defeat the stream limit of SEC-IN-3. |
| M1-18/9 | 2026-10-12 | The recover step logs `panic recovered` with `error.type = panic`, the correlation ID and `code.function.name` of the function that panicked first; the stack itself is not logged. A panic after the response started becomes `http.ErrAbortHandler`. | 02 says "Stack nur im Log", but the allowlist of M1-10 and the collector drop `exception.stacktrace` on purpose; the function name locates the panic without new keys. |
| M1-18/10 | 2026-10-12 | The proof applies to every success of a route, also to an empty response (implicit 200), to a protocol switch (Hijack is refused without a check) and to a success produced by a stage before `StageProof` (a slot of a later ticket): without a check of the route's action it becomes `500 internal_error`. M1-20 replays a stored response only after recording the check of its action. | Fail closed (B7, R-AR-14): no code path may answer a success that no `authorize` call allowed. |
| M1-18/11 | 2026-10-12 | `server.public_url` enters the schema as a required key of kind `url`, with the cross-check `public_url_origin` (origin form, as 16 asks of `bothy config check`); `api.cors_allowed_origins` gets `cors_origins`. Every command that loads the server configuration (`serve`, `migrate`, `config`, `telemetry`) therefore needs `server.public_url`; Compose sets it for api, worker and migrate (`x-server-env`), and the tests set `https://chat.example.com`. | 16 "Konfigurationswerte" says "keiner, Pflicht"; the DPoP htu of StageAuthenticate is built from it. One schema serves all commands (M1-07). |
| M1-18/12 | 2026-10-12 | W7 closes the 142 object schemas reachable from request bodies of operations under `paths` with `additionalProperties: false`; maps (`additionalProperties` with a schema) and `JsonObject` stay open; webhooks under `webhooks:` are not walked. The generator check `request schemas closed` (`api/routes/gen/closed.go`) fails `make codegen-check` on a new open request schema and names it; a schema composed with `allOf` must carry `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Schemas shared with responses or events are closed too. | The brief's rule; webhooks are requests the server sends, where R-API-04 keeps additivity. A shared schema cannot be both; SEC-IN-1 wins for the request (W7), and clients keep ignoring unknown members in responses by the tolerance rule of 06 (G5). |
| M1-18/13 | 2026-10-12 | `kernel/actor` gets the port this ticket needs: `Actor` (07 section 3 fields without `acting_for` and `view_as`), `Credentials`, `Authenticator` with `Authenticate` and `Revalidate`, `WithActor`, `FromContext` and `AnonymousOnly`. `kernel/authorize` gets only the check log; the `Authorizer` interface comes with M1-28 and repeats the `RecordCheck` contract. | M1-05 left both packages as documentation only. `acting_for` (SR9) and `view_as` (07 9.2) come with the tickets that verify them. |
| M1-18/14 | 2026-10-12 | Correlation ID: the chain reuses `otel.CorrelationID` (M1-10) and stores the value with `reqmeta.WithCorrelationID`; without a span (a unit test without the middleware) it is 16 random bytes. | The brief allows reusing an M1-10 helper with the same purpose; `kernel/reqmeta` stays standard library only. |
| M1-18/15 | 2026-10-12 | The task of this run overrides the brief's merge step: the branch `feat/M1-18-http-chain` is not merged locally; the PR lane ports it. | Instruction of the launcher. |

Debian 13 is the newest line the distroless project lists as supported at build time. | Distroless static brings maintained CA certificates, time zone data and an `/etc/passwd` entry for `nonroot`, which a static Go binary needs for outgoing TLS and local time, without a shell or package manager. | M1-23/3 | 2026-10-12 | The build argument `VERSION` in `release.yml` comes from `needs.resolve.outputs.version`, like the other steps of the job `build` on this base. On a base whose job `build` resolves the tag again (step `info`, D-W-52 on `main`), the port uses `steps.info.outputs.version`, as the brief asks. | The base `int/m1-wave3` has neither the step `info` nor the rule `release-untrusted-output`; a reference to a missing step would be empty and fail the build on the version check. The Dockerfile refuses a version that differs from `services/server/VERSION` either way. |
| M1-23/4 | 2026-10-12 | `TestReadOnlyRunAsNonRoot` passes `BOTHY_DATABASE__HOST=postgres` instead of `BOTHY_SERVER__PUBLIC_URL`. | `bothy config check` needs no variable on this base, and `server.public_url` is not yet a key of the schema: the loader rejects the variable as unknown. One known key still proves that the environment reaches the loader in the hardened container. |
| M1-23/5 | 2026-10-12 | The image contains the empty directory `/var/lib/bothy/signing-keys` (default of `secrets.signing_key_dir`) with owner `65532:65532` and mode `0700`, created by `COPY --from=build --chown --chmod`, not by `RUN` in the final stage; `TestSigningKeyDir` checks it. | 18 "Server-Images" rule 3 requires it, and a named volume takes owner and mode from the image on first mount, so the server can write its keys without an init step. A `RUN` in the final stage would need a shell and emulation. |
| M1-23/6 | 2026-10-12 | The `HEALTHCHECK` of the image carries the timings of `x-server-healthcheck` too (interval `15s`, timeout `5s`, start period `30s`, `4` retries); `TestHealthcheckMatchesCompose` compares command and timings with `infra/compose/compose.yaml`. | A runtime without Compose then probes the same way, and the acceptance criterion "the health check of the image equals the Compose health check" is held by a test instead of by review. |
| M1-23/7 | 2026-10-12 | The pin check treats a `FROM` whose reference is built from a variable as a finding unless the digest is written out; stage names compare case-insensitively; only the leading parser directive block counts for `# syntax=`; a comment inside a continued instruction is skipped. Two repository tests hold the golang tag of the builder equal to the `toolchain` of `services/server/go.mod` and the base images in the server README equal to the Dockerfile. | A variable cannot be verified statically. BuildKit reads directives only before the first comment, blank line or instruction, so a later `# syntax=` is a plain comment. Renovate could bump the golang image without the toolchain, and the README table is a number in documentation that a test must hold. |
| M1-23/8 | 2026-10-12 | The image tests read `BOTHY_TEST_IMAGE` and `BOTHY_TEST_IMAGE_OCI` through one helper with a `//nolint:forbidigo` and run Docker through one helper with a `//nolint:gosec` (G204), each with its reason; `.golangci.yml` is unchanged. Containers start with `--pull=never`. | The suppressions stay at the two places that need them, without widening a path rule. `--pull=never` keeps the tests offline and makes a missing local image fail instead of pulling a namesake from a registry. |
| M1-23/9 | 2026-10-12 | The row `docker/setup-qemu-action` leaves `docs/quality/toolchain.md`, because `release.yml` no longer uses the action. Text added by this ticket to German documents (18-release.md, gates.md, the server README) is English. | A pinned action without a user is documentation drift. The launcher requires English for everything added; M1-11/12 set the precedent of English text in German chapters during the translation. |
| M1-23/10 | 2026-10-12 | Red team (verdict NEIN, one Major, three Minors), all fixed: the cleanup of the image tests removes containers under a context without the cancellation of `t.Context()`, which Go cancels before cleanup functions run, and logs a failed removal; `TestCleanupRemovesContainers` proves that no container outlives its test. The pin check also checks the images of `COPY --from=` and `RUN --mount=...,from=` (a stage name or index passes) and finds Dockerfiles by name in any case (`Dockerfile`, `Containerfile`, with a prefix or suffix after a dot). `TestImageConfig` asserts that the image exposes no port. | The Major was reproduced: every `make image-test` left three containers in state `created`. SEC-SC-4 covers every image that enters a build, not only `FROM`, and a lower-case `build.dockerfile` would have escaped the gate. |
| M1-23/11 | 2026-10-12 | `TestQuerier_AcquireTimeout` (M1-13, `internal/platform/pg/unit_test.go`) retries its first `Acquire` for up to ten seconds before it holds the only connection; the three checks on the exhausted pool are unchanged. This test file is outside the scope of the brief. | The full `make check` of this ticket failed twice in three runs on this test with "the database is not reachable": the first connection is dialed under the 50 ms acquire timeout, and under the load of `make check` the dial takes longer. With `-cpu 1` it failed one run in four before the change and passed six of six after it. The pool keeps a connection whose acquire gave up, so the retry gets it. Leaving it would keep `make check` red for every ticket on this base. |

| M1-27/1 | 2026-10-12 | Branch `feat/M1-27-authz-grant-store` from the head of `int/m1-wave4` (`76b79a5`), in its own worktree; this session does not merge it locally and does not remove the worktree, the PR lane ports it. | The launcher's instructions take precedence over the merge step of the brief. |

| M1-27/3 | 2026-10-12 | `NewGrant` reports capability errors (unknown bit, `terminal` off a host, a host grant without exactly `terminal`) on `/capabilities`, and a host grant to an agent or app on `/subject`; the other fields are those of the brief. `NewGroup(id, name, description, limits, by, at)` takes `GroupLimits` (`group.name_max_chars`, `group.description_max_chars`, bound by M1-48), and names, descriptions and grant reasons pass the SEC-IN-4 text check (`kernel/textcheck`) like display names. | The brief names five pointers but its own shape rules include capability rules; `/capabilities` is the request field that carries them. Configuration only through named keys; a reason is shown in the audit view and in "who has access", so it gets the same text rules as other display text. |
| M1-27/4 | 2026-10-12 | `bothy_app` gets `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` on `groups`, `group_memberships` and `grants`, no `DELETE`. The immutability trigger does not block deletion; a retention job of M7 that must delete grants it in its own migration. | Groups are soft deleted, memberships removed and grants revoked; the rows are history (R-DM-09). Least privilege, as M1-26 did for `principals` and `owners`; the repository has no delete query anyway. |
| M1-27/5 | 2026-10-12 | `authz_grants_immutable` compares `to_jsonb(NEW)` and `to_jsonb(OLD)` without the five bookkeeping columns, then checks the revocation (once, with `revoked_at` set) and each expiry column (NULL to a value, once). An update that changes nothing passes. The adapter maps the trigger to `validation_failed` on `/id`. | Comparing the whole row makes a column that a later migration adds immutable by default instead of silently mutable. A no-op is no change. The repository never reaches the trigger (its revocation filters `revoked_at IS NULL`), so the mapping only covers a programming error, and the brief maps trigger violations to `validation_failed`. |
| M1-27/6 | 2026-10-12 | Repository writes store the times the caller passes (`created_at`, `added_at`, `granted_at`, `deleted_at`, `removed_at`, `revoked_at`) and never fall back to the column default `now()`; a zero time is kind internal. | One clock decides validity (brief "Outgoing port"); a default would mix the database clock with the server clock of `authorize`. |
| M1-27/7 | 2026-10-12 | Outside the file scope of the brief: `architecture/modules.yaml` gives `authz` the adapter vendor `pgx` (`.go-arch-lint.yml` regenerated), and `sqlc generate` regenerates the models of `identity` and `instance`, because every entry of `sqlc.yaml` reads the whole `migrations/` directory; `migrations/privileges_test.go` gets the rows of the three tables. | `make arch`, `make codegen-check` and `TestPrivilegesAfterAllMigrations` fail without them; M1-26 changed the same files for the same reason. |
| M1-27/8 | 2026-10-12 | Names: the checks on `groups` are `groups_source_check`, `groups_provider_shape_check` and `groups_external_ref_shape_check`; the indexes are `groups_live_name_key`, `groups_claim_key`, `group_memberships_active_key`, `group_memberships_active_user_idx`, `group_memberships_expiry_idx`, `grants_active_<column>_idx` and `grants_expiry_idx`. A host grant to an agent violates both `grants_bot_limits_check` and `grants_host_shape_check`; PostgreSQL reports the first, and both map to a client error. | The adapter maps by name, so the names are part of the contract and `TestAuthzGroupsGrantsSchema` holds them. |
| M1-27/9 | 2026-10-12 | Additions beyond the sketched API: `CapsOf`, `Capability.String`, `Grant.Shape` and `MembershipWithGroup` in `domain`. `CheckRevoke` does not apply rule 1 of `CheckCreate`. | They keep the use cases of M1-28, M1-29 and M1-48 from rebuilding sets and shapes by hand. Rule 1 judges a grant that the database check never lets exist, and an owner must be able to revoke anything (brief: "owner always allowed"). |

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| INT-W4/1 | 2026-10-12 | M1-15, M1-18, M1-23 and M1-26 enter as one squash each onto `ca30b63`, in this order; append-only files (DECISIONS, CHANGELOG, `migrations.sha256`, the privileges table, `sqlc.yaml`) hold both sides. The test helpers `column`, `columns` and `constraints` of `migrations/identity_principals_test.go` (M1-26) become `principalColumn`, `principalColumns` and `principalConstraints`, because `instance_test.go` (M1-15) declares the same names with other shapes in the package `migrations_test`. In `cmd/bothy/migrate_test.go` both tickets derived the expected output from the embedded migration set; the helpers of M1-26 (`embeddedMigrations`, `migrationFile`) stay. | Both tickets were built on the same base and passed alone; together the module did not compile (`Kind redeclared`, `column redeclared`). Keeping the declaration of the earlier ticket and renaming test-only helpers of the later one is the smallest change; no exported behaviour changes. |
| INT-W4/2 | 2026-10-12 | M1-15 (`00002_instance_counters.sql`) and M1-26 (`00002_identity_principals.sql`) both added migration version 2. The migration of M1-26, the later ticket of this wave, becomes `00003_identity_principals.sql` with unchanged content; `migrations.sha256`, the identity README and the server CHANGELOG name the new file. `sqlc generate` regenerates the models of both modules, because both entries of `sqlc.yaml` read the whole `migrations/` directory. | goose refuses a set with a duplicate version: `TestEmbeddedMigrationsValidate` failed with `duplicate version 2` and the test templates could not migrate. Neither migration is released, so renumbering the later one is allowed (the lock only forbids changing a merged file's content); the ticket order of the wave decides which one moves. |
| INT-W4/3 | 2026-10-12 | `TestReadOnlyRunAsNonRoot` (M1-23) passes `BOTHY_SERVER__PUBLIC_URL=https://bothy.example.com` instead of `BOTHY_DATABASE__HOST=postgres`; this supersedes M1-23/4 on the integrated base. | M1-18 makes `server.public_url` a required key for every command that loads the configuration (M1-18/11); against the image of the integration head the test failed with `server.public_url: is required`. The required key still proves that the environment reaches the loader in the hardened container. `make image-test` passes on the integration head (9 tests, 0 containers left). |
| INT-W4/4 | 2026-10-12 | `TestCountersOnlyUsedByPhaseHooks` (M1-15) no longer lets `packages.Load` type-check: it loads names, compiled files, imports and export files (`NeedExportFile`), then type-checks every matched package and test variant one after the other with `go/types` and a fresh `go/importer` (`gc`, reading the export files) per package, with the module's Go version and the gc sizes. What the check reports is unchanged (`TestCountersOnlyUsedByPhaseHooks_ReportsFixtures`). | On the integration head `go test -race ./internal/wire/` failed in 2 of 4 runs with a data race inside `go/types` (Go 1.27.2): two parallel checks of `packages.Load` expanded the same imported generic instance (`Named.unpack` writes `fromRHS`, `Checker.isComplete` reads it without the lock). M1-18 adds the packages that make the collision likely; M1-15 alone passed 9 of 9 runs.
