| D-M2-38-15 | 2026-10-12 | The vector files `vectors/cacophony.txt` (repository `haskell-cryptography/cacophony`, the former `centromere/cacophony`, commit `8ee9d41e34a1a596cfa3ab12aa4069ff87dc1247`, Unlicense) and `tests/vector/noise-c-basic.txt` (`rweather/noise-c`, commit `cfe25410979a87391bb9ac8d4d4bef64e9f268c6`, MIT, license file `COPYING`) are stored unchanged but gzip-compressed (`gzip -n -9`) under `services/host/testdata/noise/vectors/`, with their license files and a README table of source, commit, license and SHA-256 of the content that `TestVectorProvenance` holds. Accepted for test data: a license of `quality/licenses/allowed.txt` or a public-domain dedication such as Unlicense. | Compression keeps the upstream content byte for byte below `repo.max_file_kb`, keeps the hygiene hooks from rewriting it and keeps the published test keys out of the secret scanners' text rules; test data is never shipped. |
| D-M2-38-16 | 2026-10-12 | The ticket is built on `feat/M2-38-noise-core` from `int/m1-wave5` (`46d20a0`) in its own worktree; it is not merged locally and the worktree stays, the PR lane ports it. The rulings of this ticket stand in this section in the table format of the base (the brief names a "section 10" with rows `D-M2-38-<n>`, which the base does not have); `docs/RISKS.md` does not exist on the base, so the facts for RK-28 (pinned versions, release dates, advisories, date of the check) stand in 24 5.2 and in the ticket report instead. | The launcher's task takes precedence over the brief's merge step; the brief says to follow the merged code where it differs. |
| D-M2-38-17 | 2026-10-12 | Two guards of M1-01 change outside the brief's file list: `tools/ci/tests/test_make_server_gates.py` passes `PERF_MODULES` with its throwaway module (D-M2-38-3) and passes `DART_PKGS=` in `test_licenses_pass_the_allowlist_to_osv_scanner`, whose expected arguments listed only the Go lock file. | Both tests encoded the absence of a module without performance targets and of a tracked Dart package; the recipes under test are unchanged. |
| D-M2-38-18 | 2026-10-12 | `services/host/.golangci.yml` takes the linters and settings of the server's configuration with the host import prefix; it drops the exclusion rules for server paths and keeps two of its own: `forbidigo` is off for `cmd/bothy-host/` (the one place that reads the environment) and for the two tests that read `UPDATE_RECORDINGS`, the switch of `make -C services/host recordings`. | A verbatim copy would name paths that do not exist in the host module and would forbid the switch that regenerates the test data. |
| D-M2-38-19 | 2026-10-12 | flynn/noise v1.1.0 draws the ephemeral key of a message from `Config.Random` (`Config.EphemeralKeypair` serves pre-messages only), so a fixed ephemeral key, which the device needs because it reports `cek` before the handshake, is passed as a reader that yields exactly its 32 private bytes. `HandshakeHash` comes from `ChannelBinding`, the initiator ephemeral key from `PeerEphemeral`. | The brief allows a deterministic `Config.Random` reader when the pinned version has no fixed ephemeral key for messages. |
| D-M2-38-20 | 2026-10-12 | `packages/bothy_noise/osv-scanner.toml` overrides the license of each package of `pubspec.lock` (package and version, one entry each) with the license of its own LICENSE file: Apache-2.0 for `cryptography`, MIT for `yaml`, MIT and BSD-3-Clause for `node_preamble` (its LICENSE holds both texts), BSD-3-Clause for the others. | deps.dev has no license data for pub packages, so OSV-Scanner reports every one as UNKNOWN and `make licenses` fails; docs/quality/licenses.md item 3 asks for a checked entry with reason per package, never a blanket override. |
| D-M2-38-21 | 2026-10-12 | The Dart lines of `make lint` and `make test` run `flutter pub get --enforce-lockfile` in each package first; a `pubspec.lock` that no longer matches `pubspec.yaml` fails. Every public `List<int>` input of `bothy_noise` rejects a value outside 0 to 255 (`lib/src/bytes.dart`): a received message or frame fails like any other bad input of its step (`handshakeFailed`, `decryptFailed`, `malformed`) and breaks the object, a bad key or ID throws `invalidKey` or `invalidId`, and a bad local payload, plaintext or prologue throws an `ArgumentError` and changes no state; and an overlapping `decrypt` that resolves after a failed one throws `broken`. | Red team M2-38 (findings 2, 4, 5): `flutter analyze` and `flutter test` rewrote a stale lock file silently, so `make osv` and `make licenses` alone could check another lock than the tests ran with; `Uint8List.fromList` truncated such values silently where Go cannot receive them; the broken rule must hold for callers that do not await each call. |

## Integration int/m1-wave6

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| INT-W6/1 | 2026-10-12 | M1-16, M1-28, M3-01, M3-17, M2-23 and M2-38 enter as one squash each onto `46d20a0` (`int/m1-wave5`), in this order, each with only its own commits (`46d20a0..<head>`). Append-only files (DECISIONS, the server CHANGELOG, the config key groups and chapter constants of `internal/wire`, the server struct, start order comment and start steps of `internal/wire/serve.go`) hold both sides: start step `sync` (M1-16) is 6b and `authz` (M1-28) is 6c, both after `audit`. Where two tickets edited the same line, each side keeps its own change: the Dart lint line of the root `Makefile` is M2-38's (`flutter pub get --enforce-lockfile`, `|| exit 1`) next to M2-23's bandit comment; in the component table of `docs/spec/23-qualitaet.md` the Go and Dart rows are M2-38's and the Python and TypeScript rows M2-23's; in `tools/ci/components.py` the gate `apps` has the Noise test data of M2-38 and the SDK gates have `sdk/testdata/` of M2-23; the OSV allowlist test passes `PY_PROJECTS=`, `TS_PROJECTS=` and `DART_PKGS=`. Generated files (`.go-arch-lint.yml`, sqlc models, `config-schema.json`, the configuration reference, the catalogue copies, the SDK clients) equal a fresh generation on the head. | The tickets were built and red teamed separately on the same base; each row of the table and each argument list had been changed by one ticket only for its own component, so the union keeps both behaviours. No ticket behaviour changes and no semantic conflict needed a fix. |

## M1-20 Idempotency keys

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-20/1 | 2026-10-12 | Fingerprint (`request_sha256`): SHA-256 over the upper-case method, a line feed, `r.URL.EscapedPath()` (with `/api/v1`, without query), a line feed and the canonical body: the RFC 8785 form when the body is one JSON value without a duplicate member (`platform/jsoncanon` over `github.com/gowebpki/jcs` v1.0.2, Apache-2.0), the raw bytes otherwise, empty without a body. Recorded in 06 "Idempotenz" point 6. | 04 and 03 name "method, path and canonical body"; RFC 8785 makes member order, whitespace and escapes irrelevant, so a client may re-serialise its outbox entry. The raw bytes keep a malformed body comparable, and StageBody answers it with a final `400` that is stored under the same fingerprint. |
| M1-20/2 | 2026-10-12 | The header has no effect on an operation without the parameter `IdempotencyKey` and for the anonymous actor; the request runs as without it. Where it acts, a value that is not one UUIDv7 is `400 validation_failed` (`#header/Idempotency-Key`, `invalid_format`) and nothing is stored. Recorded in 06 "Idempotenz" point 6. | `idempotency_keys.principal_id` references `principals`, and no public operation declares the parameter; storing per anonymous client would key on nothing stable. |
| M1-20/3 | 2026-10-12 | `jsoncanon.Canonicalize` refuses an integer literal (no fraction, no exponent) that a double cannot hold exactly; the fingerprint then uses the raw bytes. Literals with a fraction or an exponent are rounded as RFC 8785 says. | RFC 8785 rounds every number to a double, so 2^64 - 1 and 2^64 - 2 written out as integers would share one fingerprint and the second request would get the first one's response instead of `422`. The API carries no such integer (ADR 0053), so the rule costs nothing for valid requests. |
| M1-20/4 | 2026-10-12 | The stage runs the rest of the chain in the outermost unit of work without `txn.RightsChange`. A keyed use case that needs `RightsChange` (M1-48 `createGrant`, `revokeGrant`, `addGroupMember`; M1-52 to M1-54) gets the internal error of `platform/pg` for a nested `RightsChange` until the ticket that builds the first such handler resolves it. Recommended there: the route metadata marks rights-changing operations and the stage opens its unit with `RightsChange`, or `platform/pg` lets a nested `RightsChange` run the rights begin hook when the unit has written nothing but the key row (the key row is the first and only lock of `idempotency_keys` in every unit, so taking `permission_version` after it cannot close a cycle). | 02 "Idempotenz im Unit of Work" makes the stage open the unit, and 02 "Ablauf" lets only the outermost unit declare `RightsChange`; the affected principals are known only inside the use case. Changing `kernel/txn` or `platform/pg` (M1-13) is outside the scope of this ticket, and no handler with `RightsChange` exists yet (`api/operations.yaml` enables no operation). |
| M1-20/5 | 2026-10-12 | A deadlock or lock timeout (SQLSTATE `40P01` or `55P03`, read through `SQLState()`) that reaches `WriteProblem` inside an attempt of the stage ends the attempt with that error, so the unit of work of the stage retries it. | Inside the stage the use case's unit of work joins the stage's one and returns the database error to its handler, which writes a problem; without this the retry of 02 "Sperrreihenfolge" would be lost for every keyed operation. Each attempt reads the body again and starts with an empty buffer (`TestRetry_ReinvokesWithFreshBuffer`, `TestRetry_HandlerDeadlockReachesTheUnitOfWork`). |
| M1-20/6 | 2026-10-12 | A keyed operation with a file body (multipart or octet-stream) answers `500 internal_error`; a keyed `2xx` whose body is not JSON rolls back and answers `500`; a final error without a JSON body is sent but not stored. | The generator refuses all three forms for the contract (`api/routes/gen`), so they can only come from a hand-built operation or a handler bug; failing closed never hashes an upload in memory and never stores a body that `jsonb` cannot hold. |
| M1-20/7 | 2026-10-12 | A stored success that carried a one-time secret is recognised by its body `{"one_time_secret": true}` with a `2xx` status; its replay is `409 conflict`, `reason: one_time_secret`, `hint: create a new one`, `Idempotent-Replay: true`. | The brief fixes the placeholder; the table has no column for the mark, and a contract response never consists of exactly this member. |
| M1-20/8 | 2026-10-12 | Generator checks for every keyed operation of `openapi.yaml`, enabled or not: no query parameter, request body absent or `application/json`, every `2xx` with `application/json` or no content, and a `2xx` with `ETag` has a required integer member `version` (also through `allOf`). | Brief "Generator checks"; checking all operations now keeps a later handler ticket from enabling an operation whose replay cannot be rebuilt (`TestRealSpec_KeyedOperationsReplayable`). |
| M1-20/9 | 2026-10-12 | Built on `feat/M1-20-idempotency-keys` from `int/m1-wave6` (`785916c`) in its own worktree; not merged locally, not pushed; the PR lane ports it. The migration takes the next free number of the base, `00007`. | The launcher's task overrides the brief's "merge locally into the base branch"; several tickets run in parallel, and the integration renumbers migrations if another ticket took the number (ADR 0007 rule 4). |
| M1-20/10 | 2026-10-12 | The method of `kernel/idempotency.Store` that claims a key is `Claim`, not `Begin` as in the brief's sketch; the outgoing port of sync names its insert `Insert`. | The gate rule `bothy-go-tx-only-in-platform-pg` (`.semgrep/`) refuses every `$X.Begin(...)` outside `platform/pg`, because a transaction begun elsewhere skips the phases; the claim begins no transaction, and a gate is not lowered for a name. |
| M1-20/11 | 2026-10-12 | A keyed `2xx` body runs through `SanitizeJSON` before `Store.Complete`: the stored body is the one the response writer sends (null members removed), and a body it would refuse (a null element, an integer beyond 2^53 - 1, not JSON) rolls the unit back and answers `500`. | Otherwise the effect would commit while the client gets `500` from the response writer of `StageRoute`, and every replay would answer `500` as well (`TestRefusedSuccess_RollsBack`). |
| M1-20/12 | 2026-10-12 | A keyed `2xx` without a check of the route's action in the check log (the test of `routeWriter.proven`, 02 9.3) rolls the unit back and is not stored; the response writer answers it with `500`. The generator checks of M1-20/8 also cover a `2XX` range response. | Red team, first round, Major 1: the effect committed and the row was stored while the client got `500`, and the retry then received the held-back body as a replay, which 06 "Idempotenz" point 4 allows only for an answer the client was already allowed to get (`TestUnprovenSuccess_RollsBack`). The range case was a Minor of the same round (`TestGenerate_RejectsKeyedOperationWithNonJSONRangeResponse`). |

## M1-29 Rights changes, access.changed and invariants I1 and SR8

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-29/1 | 2026-10-12 | `access.changed` is written only for an affected principal whose difference is non-empty; a change without visible effect still raises the rights version. | Ruling of the brief: later events carry the version in the envelope; an empty event would only make clients reload. |
| M1-29/2 | 2026-10-12 | I1 is checked for all active personal agents on every rights change. | Ruling of the brief: 10 section 5.1 names the minimum per action; the superset cannot miss a case, and 03 section 6.4 already asks the check for shrinking changes. |
| M1-29/3 | 2026-10-12 | The declaration of the brief is `txn.RightsChangeSpec` with the option `txn.DeclareRightsChange`; action and resource kinds are strings and `txn.Ref` names resources and targets. `txn.RightsChange(principals...)` stays and adds up with it; `Options.Covers` also requires the targets and the action of a nested unit. `RightsChangeOf` and `InRightsChange` read the declaration. | `RightsChange` is already the name of the option, and kernel ports never import each other (go-arch-lint: `kernel/txn` may not import `kernel/authorize`). |
| M1-29/4 | 2026-10-12 | The hooks of phase 1 and 3.1 are the method values `Begin` and `BeforeCommit` of `authzapp.RightsHook`, which also implements `authz.RightsChanges.AddAffected` and `VerifyInvariants`. The appender of sync and the recorder of audit are bound after the unit of work exists (`Bind`, start step `authz`); an unbound hook fails every rights change with kind internal. | The recorder writes through the unit of work whose registry holds the hook, so it cannot exist when the registry is sealed; failing closed keeps a rights change from committing without its event and audit. |
| M1-29/5 | 2026-10-12 | The deny entry of a violation goes to `Recorder.RecordDenial`, which aggregates per `audit.deny_aggregate_window` and writes in its own transaction after the rollback. `last_owner` wins over `i1_violation` when both hold. | Brief "Deny audit entry"; without an owner no personal agent can be in order either, and AZ-M26 expects `last_owner`. |
| M1-29/6 | 2026-10-12 | The view of a principal (the input of `access.changed`) counts a person as in a session: the owner role takes effect for an active owner. dm channels are not part of it; a space is visible with read on it or on one of its channels. The input type of `DiffVisible` is `domain.AccessView`. | The view is what the person sees in the app; rights changes never change dm participation; `domain.Snapshot` is the rights snapshot of M1-28 (merged names win). |
| M1-29/7 | 2026-10-12 | For declared targets authz derives the affected principals from the grants in effect on the instance, the target, its space and, for a space, its channels, with group members, the active owners and the effective operators of the channels. Fixed thread operators are not added. | The difference step drops everyone without a change, so the superset is safe; the view of an agent on a channel never depends on a thread it operates. |
| M1-29/8 | 2026-10-12 | `StructureSource.ServedTargets` returns `authz.ChannelRef{ChannelID, SpaceID}`, with a zero `ChannelID` for a space whose default operator is the agent; `StructureSource.SpaceArchived` and `ChannelFacts.Archived` (channel or space archived) are added. `Effective` caps every level at read on an archived channel, owners included, and `Decide` answers a requirement above read there with `channel_archived` (07 section 4.5 point 1). chat fills them from M1-52 to M1-54. | 03 section 6.4 point 3 needs spaces without a channel; test 10a of the brief caps a channel at read through the target path, and the cap is rights logic, which lives only in authz (B7). `grant.create` must refuse archived spaces. |
| M1-29/9 | 2026-10-12 | `AgentPolicySource.PersonalAgents` returns `authz.PersonalAgent{AgentID, CredentialHolderID}`. | `authz.AgentPolicy` already exists (M1-28) with other fields. |
| M1-29/10 | 2026-10-12 | The store refuses writes of memberships and grants and `SoftDeleteGroup` outside a unit declared as a rights change; `InsertGroup` and the new `UpdateGroup` are no rights change. New store reads: `ActiveTriggerGrants`, `ActiveGrantsOnTargets`, `ActiveMembershipsOfGroups`, `MembershipsOfUserInGroup`. | The store doc of M1-27 left the enforcement to M1-29; `group.create` and `group.update` have "Rechte" `nein` in 05. |
| M1-29/11 | 2026-10-12 | Inside a unit declared as a rights change the Authorizer neither reads nor fills the snapshot cache, the version memo or the scope memo. | The unit sees its own uncommitted version; an entry cached under it would survive a rollback and could serve another change that commits the same number. |
| M1-29/12 | 2026-10-12 | `grant.revoke` resolves a revoked grant like the stored grant (visibility and the delegation guard decide); every other action still finds no revoked grant. | 06 section 2 "Ohne Wirkung": revoking a revoked grant succeeds without change, which `not_found` would contradict. |
| M1-29/13 | 2026-10-12 | Admin calls that may be without effect (`DeleteGroup`, `RemoveGroupMember`, `RevokeGrant`) decide that in a first unit without rights change and run the change in a second unit declared as one, which checks everything again under the lock. When that second unit finds the call without effect after all (a concurrent call did the change first), it rolls back, so no rights version is raised, and the first unit runs again to record the call without effect. An `UpdateGroup` without change audits `no_change: true` too. | No rights version for a call without effect (R-API-27), while the change itself still runs fully under the lock of `permission_version`. |
| M1-29/14 | 2026-10-12 | `AddGroupMember` closes a membership that is not removed but expired (removed_at, removed_by) before inserting the new one; a membership in effect is `409 conflict`. Claim groups: rename, add and remove are `409 conflict` with reason `claim_managed_group`; the description may change. | The unique index allows one row without `removed_at` per person and group; 07 section 2.6 names the reason for claim groups. |
| M1-29/15 | 2026-10-12 | `CreateGrant` answers a host target with `404` until M2-39 brings hosts; an archived space or channel is `409 conflict` with reason `channel_archived`, checked after `authorize` so an invisible target stays `404`. An optional revoke or delete reason is checked with SEC-IN-4 and audited as `ReasonText`. | No host can exist before M2-39; the order keeps 02 section 9.2. |
| M1-29/16 | 2026-10-12 | `AdminService` takes the caller as `actor.Actor` and puts it into the context for the recorder and the events; a different principal already in the context is an internal error. Group text limits come through `AdminDeps.Limits`; the keys `group.name_max_chars` and `group.description_max_chars` are bound when M1-48 wires the service with its routes. | The recorder reads the actor from the context only (M1-24); the brief adds no configuration key. |
| M1-29/17 | 2026-10-12 | `errs.ProblemExtender` members are merged by the problem writer in name order after the standard members; a standard name (also an absent one) is never taken, nil members and nested nulls are dropped, an internal error carries none. | 10 section 5.4 and ADR 0053. |
| M1-29/18 | 2026-10-12 | The integration tests use PostgreSQL 18 with the real identity PrincipalSource, the real unit of work, sync and audit, and the fakes of structure, agent policy and credentials. Owner, status and operator changes of later tickets are test use cases that change SQL rows or the fakes inside the transaction and declare the action. A personal token of the owner gets `owner_only` for owner actions (SR5), not `step_up_not_possible`. | The brief's test setup; the requirement step decides before the step-up step (M1-28). |
| M1-29/19 | 2026-10-12 | Base `int/m1-wave6` at `785916c`, branch `feat/M1-29-authz-invariants` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and the worktree stays, the PR lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and the brief's language. |
| M1-29/20 | 2026-10-12 | Red team round 1 (verdict JA, four minors): the raced call without effect no longer raises the rights version (M1-29/13, `TestRevokeGrant_RacedRevocationIsWithoutEffect`); every view of one change is computed at the time of `Begin`, so a grant that expires during the unit is reported by the expiry sweep and not as part of this change, while `VerifyInvariants` reads the clock at the check; the example of `txn.UnitOfWork.Do` declares with `DeclareRightsChange`. The comment of `identity/app.Store.InsertOwner`, which still names `txn.RightsChange`, stays for M1-31, because the brief allows only the adapter method in `internal/identity`. Closing an expired membership with the caller as `removed_by` stays (M1-29/14). | Findings of the red team fixed in the same ticket where they are in scope. |

## M1-33 Devices, device keys and DPoP verification

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-33/1 | 2026-10-12 | Base `int/m1-wave6` at `785916c`, branch `feat/M1-33-identity-devices-dpop` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and the worktree stays, the PR lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also in the module README (its German headings stay). | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and the brief's language. |
| M1-33/2 | 2026-10-12 | `listUserDevices` authorizes `device.list` on the resource kind `device` without an id and with `OwnerID` = the path person, not on the kind `user` of the brief's table. An unknown person is `not_found` before `authorize`; `listMyDevices` keeps the brief's resource `user` = caller and `getDevice` the device itself. | With the kind `user` another person is visible (people see the directory) and the requirement `self oder owner` fails with `403 forbidden`, which breaks the brief's outcome column and 05 rule 2 ("kein Sichtrecht ergibt 404"). Devices are owned resources (M1-28/D4): visible to their person and to effective owners only, so the device kind gives `404` from `authz` itself and no rights logic moves out of `authz`. The pending rule still answers `403 device_pending` for the own list from a pending device. |
| M1-33/3 | 2026-10-12 | A missing device or person and an invisible one answer the same `404 not_found` without a detail (`app.invisible`). | 02 section 9.2 and 05 rule 2: the bodies must not differ (`TestGetDevice_PendingOwnOnly`, `TestListUserDevices_SelfOwnerOther`). |
| M1-33/4 | 2026-10-12 | `identity.Device` and the API response leave out `verification_code`; M1-38 adds it for the pending device itself. | 08 section 5.2 derives the code from the token pepper and `auth.device_verification_code_digits`, and the brief M1-38 owns approval with the code and its response mapping. |
| M1-33/5 | 2026-10-12 | `TestCredentialSource_Devices` checks active, pending, revoked, a pending device older than any pending window (stays `pending`), a foreign principal and an unknown device (`unknown`). Session and token facts stay not active until M1-34 and M1-44, so a request that names a session or token is unauthenticated (fail closed). | Devices have no expiry of their own; treating a pending device past `auth.device_pending_ttl` as revoked belongs to M1-38 (its brief, design notes of authentication). The brief's word "expired" is covered as the old pending device. |
| M1-33/6 | 2026-10-12 | `kernel/jose` bounds JSON nesting with the constant `MaxJSONDepth = 32`, the default of `http.max_json_depth`. | The kernel reads no configuration and `ParseCompactJWS` has a fixed signature; JOSE objects of the profile nest at most three levels, so the bound only stops hostile input. |
| M1-33/7 | 2026-10-12 | The JWS vectors of ADR 0067 run as `TestJWS_RFC7515ExampleES256` (appendix A.3 through `ParseCompactJWS` and `Verify`) and `TestVerify_RFC8037ExampleEdDSA` (appendix A.4 through the internal signature check, because its payload is plain text and the profile accepts JSON objects only). The thumbprint vectors are those of the brief (RFC 9449 and RFC 8037); the RSA vector of RFC 7638 section 3.1 comes with RSA in M1-41. | The profile has no RSA key type yet; every vector the profile can express is tested. The literal segments stand apart in the test source so that the secret scanner (rule `jwt`) does not take the public examples for tokens; the fuzz seed files write the leading `e` of each segment as `\x65` for the same reason. |
| M1-33/8 | 2026-10-12 | `kernel/jose` has a few exported helpers beyond the brief: `JWKFromPublicKey`, `JWK.Canonical`, `JWK.MarshalJSON`, `JWK.PublicKey`, `ES256Signer`, `EdDSASigner`, `Header`, `AlgES256`, `AlgEdDSA`, `ErrInvalidJWK`, `ErrInvalidJWS`, `ErrSignature`. `ParseCompactJWS` with an empty `typ` checks no type; `zip` is refused besides `crit`, `jku`, `x5u`, `x5c`. | The test helpers, the stored canonical JWK and the token issuers of M1-34 need them; ADR 0067 item 2 lists `zip`. An empty `typ` exists for foreign tokens without one (the RFC 7515 example). |
| M1-33/9 | 2026-10-12 | `device_keys.public_jwk` stores the canonical public JWK (only `crv`, `kty`, `x`, `y`); the attestation is stored as received. The repository has `KeyByJKT` beyond the port for the login tickets. | The canonical form is exactly what the thumbprint covers, so no unknown member of a client survives; 08 section 6.1 keeps the attestation unchanged. |
| M1-33/10 | 2026-10-12 | Device registration also applies SEC-IN-4 to `app_version`, rejects an approver on a pending registration, and has no length limit for the name. | Both are display texts of the device list; an approver only belongs to an active device (03 `approved_by`). No configuration key names a device name limit, so none is invented; the body limit bounds the input. |
| M1-33/11 | 2026-10-12 | `bothy_app` gets `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` on `devices` and `device_keys`, no `DELETE`. | Revocation sets state and `revoked_at`; the rows are security history (retention class `security`), and the retention job of that class grants its delete in its own migration. |
| M1-33/12 | 2026-10-12 | The jti store keeps no size cap; an insert looks at up to four entries of its shard and removes the expired ones, and a sweeper removes every expired entry each `auth.dpop_proof_max_age` (stop step `dpop_replay_sweeper`, phase HTTP). | 08 section 7.3 names no limit (the window and the rate limits bound it); a full scan per insert would make inserts linear in the shard size. The window length is the interval after which every entry of a window has expired. |

## M2-01 Worker consumers of the event log

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-01-1 | 2026-10-12 | No migration seeds `event_consumer_cursors`. In the roles `worker` and `all`, `ConsumerService.Ensure` inserts the row of every registered consumer in one unit of work before the runner starts, with `INSERT ... SELECT $1, COALESCE(max(seq), 0) FROM event_log ON CONFLICT (name) DO NOTHING`; unregistered names have no row and no lag series. The column default `0` of 03 stays. Place: `00007_event_consumer_cursors.sql`, `queries/consumers.sql`, 02 11.5 item 5. | A seeded row for a consumer that arrives in a later release (`push`, M3-19) would make it replay every event retained since the migration; a new consumer starts at the head like the first connection of an agent consumer (04 15.1 item 2). Reading `max(seq)` in one statement is safe because a reader that sees `seq = k` sees every smaller number (04 3.2 item 3); two workers starting together run the same statement and one row wins. |
| D-M2-01-2 | 2026-10-12 | `domain.PlanRound`: numbers contiguous from `last_seq + 1` are processed; when `last_seq + 1` lies below `min(seq)` and the first number read equals `min(seq)`, the events in between were removed by retention, the round processes from `min(seq)`, logs `event consumer lost events to retention` once at error level with `bothy.consumer.name` and `bothy.event.seq` and continues; every other jump, hole or repeat is `ErrConsumerGap`, the round rolls back and the consumer stalls. `min(seq)` is read in the round's transaction only when the first number does not follow the cursor. Place: `domain/consumer.go`, `app/consumers.go`, 02 11.5 item 6. | The removed events cannot come back and stalling would not help; a webhook subscription detects its own gap through `last_delivered_seq` (11 6), and the alarm fires long before. 04 3.2 items 3 and 4 rule out other holes, so they are bugs; unlike the tail's `ErrGap` the worker does not exit, the cursor just never passes the hole. The round holds `ACCESS SHARE` on `event_log`, so no partition is detached between the reads. |
| D-M2-01-3 | 2026-10-12 | A handler error, a handler panic (recovered into kind internal, the panic value is not kept), a database error and `ErrConsumerGap` roll back the whole round: no job, no cursor move, the next attempt at the next wake or poll; no event is ever skipped. The runner logs the first failure after a success at error level (`event consumer round failed`, `bothy.consumer.name`, `error.type` as SQLSTATE, `consumer_gap`, `timeout`, `canceled` or the `kernel/errs` kind) and the next success at info level (`event consumer recovered`). Each consumer has its own goroutine, so a stalled one never holds back another. Place: `app/consumer_runner.go`, 02 11.5 item 7. | One failing event must stall only its consumer, visibly through the gauge and the alarm, rather than lose an event; edge-triggered lines keep a database outage, during which the worker pauses (02 14.4), from flooding the log. |
| D-M2-01-4 | 2026-10-12 | Before the cursor moves, the round asks the appender of `sync/app` (its per-unit `txn.Key` buffer, `bufferedEvents`) whether the unit buffered durable events; if so it fails with kind internal and the unit rolls back. Place: `app/appender.go`, `app/consumers.go`, 02 11.5 item 8. | The consumer would read its own output, and the round would take the `event_seq` counter lock and lengthen the serialized commit phase (02 11.7); ADR 0051 lets a round insert jobs only. |
| D-M2-01-5 | 2026-10-12 | Two observable gauges, `bothy.event.consumer.lag` (`{event}`, `head_seq - last_seq`, floored at zero) and `bothy.event.consumer.pending_age` (`s`, now from `kernel/clock` minus `occurred_at` of the oldest event after the cursor, zero at the head), with the only attribute `bothy.consumer.name` from the closed set `sync.ConsumerNames()`; one callback calls `Consumers.States` (one read of all rows with the head, then one read per row, bounded by `database.statement_timeout`), and a failed read drops the points. Every role registers them in start step 6b. The registry `internal/platform/otel/metrics` of M1-68 is not merged, so they live in `internal/sync/adapters/otelmetrics` with the meter of the global provider; `sync` already had the `otel` vendor group. `bothy.consumer.name` is in `otel/attr`, the collector allowlist and signals.md 5.3. Place: `adapters/otelmetrics/consumers.go`, signals.md 4.2. | The plan row asks for `head_seq` minus `last_seq`, but `sync.event_retention` is a time window, so the alarm needs the age too. A stopped or missing worker must still show a growing lag, so the api role exports it as well; telemetry never blocks (17 rule 4). |
| D-M2-01-6 | 2026-10-12 | Alarm `BothyEventConsumerLag`, severity `page`, `max by (bothy_consumer_name) (bothy_event_consumer_pending_age_seconds{job="bothy/bothy-server"}) > 900` for `5m`, on the new key `consumer.pending_alert_after` (place Server, duration, default `15m`, at least `1s`). The start check `consumer_alert_below_retention` requires it strictly below `sync.event_retention` and names both keys and no value. The rule file holds the number; `alert-thresholds` keeps it equal to the default. Place: `bothy-alerts.yaml`, alerting.md 3.2 and 4, 02 13 item 4 and 22, `configkeys_consumer.go`, `configchecks.go`. | A normal worker restart takes at most `shutdown.total_timeout` (`60s`) plus a few `consumer.poll_interval` (`1s`), far below `15m`, and the `for` of the neighbouring lag alarm adds five minutes; `15m` is far below the `30d` retention, so the operator has weeks before any event is lost. `page`, because a standing consumer stops pushes and outgoing webhooks, which people notice at once (alerting.md 1). |
| D-M2-01-7 | 2026-10-12 | One `LISTEN event_log` connection per process for all worker consumers, opened with `pg.Pool.DialDedicated(ctx, "bothy-consumer-wake")`, apart from the tail's connection; the listening loop of the M1-16 tail moved into the shared type `listener` in `adapters/postgres`, which both use. A notification wakes every consumer through a channel of capacity one with a non-blocking send; the payload is not read. The connection is reconnected with exponential backoff from `consumer.poll_interval` up to `database.listen_reconnect_backoff_cap`, and every consumer also polls every `consumer.poll_interval`. Without a registered consumer the runner starts nothing and opens no connection. Place: `adapters/postgres/listen.go`, `waker.go`, 02 11.5 item 9. | In role `all` the ordered loop of the tail must never depend on the consumers; polling covers a lost connection and nothing is lost because the cursor lives in the database (02 14.4). The production list is empty in M2-01, and an idle connection per worker would serve nobody. |
| D-M2-01-8 | 2026-10-12 | After a full batch (`consumer.batch_size` events) the next round of that consumer starts at once (drain); after a short batch, an idle round, a locked row or a failed round the consumer waits for the next wake or poll. Place: `app/consumer_runner.go`. | A backlog drains without waiting a poll per batch, while `consumer.batch_size` keeps each round, and with it the `ACCESS SHARE` lock that a detach of the partition maintenance waits for, short. |
| D-M2-01-9 | 2026-10-12 | The stop step `event_consumers` lies in `lifecycle.PhaseConsumers` (before River's phase) with budget `0`, the rest of `shutdown.total_timeout`: the runner starts no new round and waits for the running ones; when the budget runs out the round's context is cancelled and the round rolls back. An unexpected end of the runner (the waker ends early, a panic outside a handler) goes through `Failed()` to `lifecycle.Runner.FailOn`, and the process exits `1` like after an unexpected stop of the River client. Place: `wire/sync.go`, `wire/serve.go`, 02 14.5, 15.2 and 15.3. | 02 15.3 gives this step no own budget; either way no job is lost or doubled, because cursor and jobs commit together. A process whose consumers ended would look healthy while pushes and webhooks stop. |
| D-M2-01-10 | 2026-10-12 | Branch `feat/M2-01-worker-event-consumers` in its own worktree from the head of `int/m1-wave6` (`785916c`); this session does not merge it locally and does not remove the worktree, the PR lane ports it. New text in the German chapters (02, alerting.md, signals.md, ADR 0051, threat model) is written in English. The tests take two hooks of `wire.Deps`: `ConsumerHandlers` (replaces the empty production list in worker and all) and `ConsumerGauges` (replaces the registration on the global meter provider), because `otel.Setup` installs one global provider per process and parallel serve tests would overwrite it. | The launcher's task overrides the merge step of the brief ("Definition of done") and requires English output. The hooks follow the pattern of `Deps.Registrations` and let the serve tests prove that worker and all run a registered consumer and that role api exports the gauges without a runner. |

## M3-02 S3 driver against SeaweedFS

The brief names these rows D-M3-02-1 to D-M3-02-13; they follow the numbering of this log (M3-02/1 to M3-02/13), and M3-02/14 onwards are rulings of the build.

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M3-02/1 | 2026-10-12 | One egress client per store: purpose `objectstore`, a host policy that allows exactly the scheme, host and port of every request (the endpoint with path style, `<bucket>.<endpoint host>` with virtual-hosted style, the port of the endpoint or the default port of its scheme), `RedirectNever`, the time limit `http.transfer_timeout` and the body limit `file.max_upload_bytes`. | 19 SEC-NET-2: a consumer names its own targets. 02 14.2 lets a chapter name the limit of an outside call, and these bytes serve uploads and downloads, whose limit `http.transfer_timeout` is. No stored object is larger than `file.max_upload_bytes` (03 `files.size_bytes`), so a body is neither cut nor unbounded (SEC-NET-5). An S3 redirect means a wrong endpoint or region. |
| M3-02/2 | 2026-10-12 | The SDK client is built by hand with `s3.New(s3.Options{...})`: region, the credentials of M3-02/6, the HTTP client of the egress client, the checksum mode of M3-02/10, a no-op logger, no tracer or meter, `BaseEndpoint` from `file.s3.endpoint`, `UsePathStyle`, S3 Express session auth off. `github.com/aws/aws-sdk-go-v2/config` and `LoadDefaultConfig` are never used, and `file.s3.endpoint` is required. The brief named `aws.Config` plus `s3.NewFromConfig`; `NewFromConfig` looks up `AWS_ENDPOINT_URL` and related variables of the environment, `s3.New` takes only the options. | The default configuration reads the environment and shared files and asks the instance metadata service, whose address SEC-NET-3 always blocks and whose credentials 12 4.3 does not allow. Without the endpoint resolution of the SDK the host policy names exactly one host. SDK spans and metrics have no names in `signals.md` (02 5 rule 5). The SDK still reads `AWS_EXECUTION_ENV` and the Lambda trace variables for the user agent and the recursion header; neither changes a target or a credential. |
| M3-02/3 | 2026-10-12 | No SDK retries: `aws.NopRetryer{}`. A failed call is one request and a storage failure. | 02 14.3: no retry of external calls in the request path; jobs retry through River. `TestNoRetries` holds one request for a `503 SlowDown`. |
| M3-02/4 | 2026-10-12 | `Put` and `UploadPart` sign their body as `UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD` (`v4.SwapComputePayloadSHA256ForUnsignedPayloadMiddleware`, present in `aws-sdk-go-v2` v1.47.3) and stream it with `ContentLength` set, never buffered. | The caller's reader is not seekable and the endpoint may be plain `http` in a private network, where the SDK cannot hash the payload before sending. Integrity comes from the part digest (12 3.2) and the file SHA-256 of the read pass (12 3.4), which 12 4.3 names as the replacement of SDK checksums. |
| M3-02/5 | 2026-10-12 | Every error of the driver is one type with the text `files/s3: <operation> <key>: <detail>`; detail is the port sentinel, the S3 code with the HTTP status, a status, a fixed text for a known cause (egress refusal with its reason, response too large, context cancelled or deadline, credentials with the key and the secret error code) or `transport`. `Unwrap` returns the port sentinel and the cause. Mapping: `NoSuchKey`, `NotFound` or 404 on `Get` and `Head` is `ErrNotFound` (`NoSuchBucket` is a storage failure); `InvalidRange` or 416 on `Get` is `ErrInvalidRange`; `NoSuchUpload` on `UploadPart` and `CompleteMultipart` is `ErrUploadNotFound` and on `AbortMultipart` nil; `InvalidPart`, `InvalidPartOrder` and `EntityTooSmall` on `CompleteMultipart` are `ErrInvalidPart`; everything else is a storage failure. The brief wrote the prefix as `s3 <operation>`; the driver uses `files/s3:` like the file system driver's `files/fs:`. | net/http errors carry the full request URL, and callers that wrap a port error into `kernel/errs` put its text into the log (SEC-LOG-3, SEC-SEC-3). The conformance suite gives the same results on both drivers. A HEAD answer has no body, so `Head` on a missing bucket reads as `ErrNotFound`; `Get`, `Put` and `Delete` report the bucket as a storage failure. |
| M3-02/6 | 2026-10-12 | The credentials provider resolves both references through the secret provider on every `Retrieve` and returns credentials that expire at once, so the SDK credential cache asks again for the next request; the driver keeps no copy. `New` resolves both once and stops with an error that names the key. `bothy config check --secrets` resolves the two references only with `file.storage.driver = s3` and prints `skipped` otherwise. | 16 5.2 and 02 13 item 6: a rotation works without restart once the provider re-reads the file after `secrets.cache_ttl`; a failed re-read keeps the old value inside the provider (M1-09). 02 14.4 row Secret-Provider: fail fast at start. 12 18: the references are read only with the driver `s3`. |
| M3-02/7 | 2026-10-12 | The object name is `<file.s3.prefix>/<key>` with a prefix, else the key; key and prefix both pass `domain.ValidateKey` before any request, and a refused key or prefix is never repeated in the error. | M3-01/4: one grammar for all drivers, which refuses empty segments and a leading or trailing `/`, so a path-style URL never collapses two keys into one object (`FuzzObjectName`); the driver adds nothing else to a key (SEC-UP-8). |
| M3-02/8 | 2026-10-12 | A body of another length than `size` gives `ErrSizeMismatch` and stores nothing: the body wrapper hands out at most `size - 1` bytes until the source has delivered the last byte and reported its end. A longer source fails before the last byte leaves, a shorter one fails before `ContentLength` is reached; a size of 0 proves the reader empty before any request. | M3-01 note 3. S3 never receives a complete request, so neither `PutObject` nor `UploadPart` can store a body of the wrong length (`casePutSizeMismatch`, `TestS3UploadPartSizeMismatch` against SeaweedFS). |
| M3-02/9 | 2026-10-12 | The port operation is `PresignUploadPart(ctx, key, uploadID, n, size, ttl)`. The S3 driver presigns `UploadPart` with `ContentLength = size` and the expiry `ttl`, and returns `ExpiresAt` = the time taken before signing plus `ttl`. `n < 1` (or above 10000) gives `ErrInvalidPart`, `size < 1` `ErrSizeMismatch` on both drivers; a `ttl` that is not a positive whole number of seconds and an empty upload ID are refused before signing. 12 4.1 is updated (chapter 12 is not core contract). Result of `TestPresignedPartBindsLength` (12 3.3): SeaweedFS at the pinned version accepts a plain `PUT` of exactly the signed length and refuses one byte more, one byte less, chunked transfer encoding and a `PUT` after the validity, each without a part. | 12 3.3, R-FI-02, RK-42: no driver can bind a length it does not know, and `X-Amz-Expires` carries whole seconds. The mode `presigned` stays off (`file.s3.presigned_upload` is not registered); client reachability of the storage stays open. |
| M3-02/10 | 2026-10-12 | `file.s3.checksum_mode` has the values `when_required` (default) and `when_supported`; they map to `aws.RequestChecksumCalculationWhenRequired` with `aws.ResponseChecksumValidationWhenRequired` and to the two `WhenSupported` values. `when_supported` is refused at load with an `http` endpoint (cross-check `file_s3`). | 12 18 names the default but not the value set; the two values are the two settings of the SDK, the same for request and response as 12 4.3 says. With `when_supported` the SDK sends the checksum of a streamed, unseekable body as a trailer and refuses that without TLS, so every `Put` and `UploadPart` over `http` would fail (found in review; `TestChecksumWhenSupportedOverHTTPS` holds the https path). |
| M3-02/11 | 2026-10-12 | Cross-check `file_s3` over `file.storage.driver`, the six non-secret `file.s3.*` keys and `egress.allowed_hosts`, only with driver `s3`, through `s3.Config.Validate`: endpoint set and an absolute `http` or `https` URL with a host and without user info, query, fragment or a path other than `/`; bucket set and in the S3 naming rules for general purpose buckets (reserved prefixes and suffixes such as `--x-s3` refused); region set; prefix empty or the key grammar; an IP endpoint host only with path style; an `http` endpoint only with an `egress.allowed_hosts` entry with `allow_private` for the request host and port. `file.s3.endpoint` is a string key. The check fills `s3.Config` from the six keys; it cannot bind the struct, because a check sees only the keys it names. `New` runs the same `Validate`. | 02 13 item 4, every message names keys, never values. 19 SEC-NET-2 allows `http` only with such an entry; without it the server would start and fail at the first request. A directory bucket would make the SDK open a session with `CreateSession`, which is not core S3. The schema allows an empty default only for string and list keys. |
| M3-02/12 | 2026-10-12 | Architecture rule: vendor group `awssdk` (`github.com/aws/aws-sdk-go-v2`, `github.com/aws/smithy-go` and their subpackages) and `adapter_vendors: [awssdk]` for module `files`; depguard rule `aws-sdk-only-in-s3-driver` denies `github.com/aws/` in every file except `**/internal/files/adapters/s3/**`, production and test files alike; forbidigo forbids `s3.Client.DeleteObjects` and `s3.PresignClient.PresignGetObject`. A temporary SDK import in `adapters/fs` and temporary calls in the s3 package failed `golangci-lint` once and were reverted. | 12 15, ADR 0010 and ADR 0048 Confirmation. go-arch-lint grants vendors to the component `internal/files/adapters/**` as a whole, so only depguard (ADR 0008, rules per file pattern) narrows the SDK to one adapter. |
| M3-02/13 | 2026-10-12 | The SeaweedFS test container runs `chrislusf/seaweedfs:4.48` pinned by the digest of its multi-architecture index, the repository and version of the Compose service `seaweedfs` (`TestSeaweedImagePinned`; Renovate moves it in the group Compose-Images through its own regex manager). It starts with the command of the Compose service (`weed server -dir=/data -s3 -ip.bind=0.0.0.0`, keys as `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` and `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`) plus `-master.volumeSizeLimitMB=32 -volume.max=1000`. Test code builds a full SDK client to create buckets and inspect state (`ListParts`, `HeadObject`); the driver never does. `infra/compose/compose.yaml` stays unchanged. | The test proves the version the stack runs. SeaweedFS keeps every bucket in its own volumes, and with the default volume size of 30 GB the free disk of a test runtime held one bucket only (`No writable volumes and no free volumes left`), while every test creates its own bucket; the stack has one bucket. `TestS3WrongCredentialsRefused` shows that SeaweedFS with this command refuses a wrong secret key, so the Compose service needs no identity configuration. |
| M3-02/14 | 2026-10-12 | `wire.FilesObjectStore(ctx, values, wire.FilesDeps{Secrets, Egress})`: driver `fs` unchanged; driver `s3` binds `s3.Config`, builds `wire.EgressConfig` and calls `s3.New` with `http.transfer_timeout` and `file.max_upload_bytes`. A construction error is an error of `FilesObjectStore` that starts with `config:` and names the key. No role opens the store yet. | M3-01/13: M3-03 opens the store in the roles together with the image directory. The S3 branch needs a context, the secret provider and the egress dependencies, which the old signature did not carry; no other egress consumer is wired yet, so `FilesDeps.Egress` is where the logger and metrics of the process go. |
| M3-02/15 | 2026-10-12 | The ticket ends on the branch `feat/M3-02-object-storage-s3` from `int/m1-wave6` and is not merged locally; the worktree stays. | The launcher of this build overrides the brief's "merge locally into the base branch, then `git worktree remove`": the integration of the wave merges the ticket branches. |

## M2-32 Recording Hermes Agent before the build

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-32-1 | 2026-10-12 | The live recording is part of the component's default test run: `make test` and every `make check` that selects the gate component `plugins` repeat it; without network it fails, it never skips. A Hermes surface is public when the pinned version documents it for platform plugins (`ADDING_A_PLATFORM.md`, the developer and user guide) or when it is a name without leading underscore on `BasePlatformAdapter`, on the plugin context of `register(ctx)` or on `PlatformConfig`; an underscore method the pinned base class documents as an override point is public for overriding only; everything else is internal. | 09 17.3 asks for a recording test, RK-12 needs the check continuously; a recording of the build day alone would be a stored value whose rule nobody checks again (R-QA-02). |
| D-M2-32-2 | 2026-10-12 | `plugins/hermes-adapter` is a Python project with the harness under `tests/hermes_harness` (typed strict, measured, documented like product code); the probe plugin is outside mypy and coverage. The base had no M2-23, so this ticket fixed exactly the two Makefile defects (mypy runs inside each project; bandit excludes `<project>/.venv` and `<project>/generated` besides `tests`) and added `quality.coverage.python_min_percent` (23 11, `test_config_values.py` checks every tracked project under `sdk/` and `plugins/`, gates.md row `coverage`). A parallel M2-23 resolves to one implementation at the merge. | The harness is test support the component never ships; the probe only runs inside the Hermes process, where the recording proves it. |
| D-M2-32-3 | 2026-10-12 | Pin rule: the newest release tag of the public repository older than `deps.min_release_age` on the recording day, resolved with `git ls-remote` (here `tests/hermes_pin.toml`). `renovate.json` has a regex manager for the file (`github-tags`, `currentValue` from `tag`, `currentDigest` from `commit`) and a package rule whose note asks to record again, review the fixture diff and update 09 17.10; the recording test fails until then, on purpose. | Same cooldown as every dependency (ADR 0041); the failing test is the reminder, like the rule "release binaries". |
| D-M2-32-4 | 2026-10-12 | Install from a `git archive` export, never from the checkout; the checkout is read-only. The pinned commit has a `uv.lock`, so `uv sync --locked --no-dev` installs from it (no `hermes_requirements.txt`). The pinned version refuses wheel builds outside its Nix build (`setup.py` guard), so the export is installed editable; the installed digest covers every `RECORD` of the venv and the source tree an editable install runs from. | Build backends write into the source tree; the editable target is the throwaway export, so the checkout stays untouched and a change of the running code is still found. |
| D-M2-32-5 | 2026-10-12 | Isolation set: throwaway homes, an allowlisted environment, a null keyring, refusing shims for `security`, `secret-tool`, `launchctl` and `systemctl` that record only tool and subcommand, the refusing egress proxy, `gateway run --force` (no service probe of the real account), and on macOS `sandbox-exec` denying outbound network except the loopback ports of the harness' own servers (fake model, control channel, egress recorder) and the root's own Unix sockets, signals and process information outside the sandbox, file contents and writes below the real home except the root, and the execution of `launchctl` and `security`. The throwaway configuration disables lazy installs, tirith, the update check, the model catalogue refresh, tool search, and points `models_dev.url` at the fake endpoint. The sandbox does not block the macOS keychain API; the keychain stays out of reach through the throwaway `HOME` (no default keychain), the `security` shim and the exec denial of its binary. The fake endpoint judges every credential header: every value of every credential header and credential query parameter counts, and a foreign value in any of them or two different credentials in one request is `unexpected` and fails the run; which header carries a fake credential is not judged, because the pinned model client sends every credential, the OAuth token included, as `x-api-key` to an endpoint outside anthropic.com (`_auth_style`), while its context length probe sends the OAuth token as a bearer token. | Each part closes one way to a real credential, a real model, a local model or gateway, a service or another Hermes installation; on Linux the unreachable credentials are the guarantee. |
| D-M2-32-6 | 2026-10-12 | The probe's platform name is `recording_probe`; it is a directory plugin in `<HERMES_HOME>/plugins/recording_probe`, enabled through `plugins.enabled`. The pinned loader imports a directory plugin through its `__init__.py`, so the probe has `plugin.yaml`, a two-line `__init__.py` (no Hermes import) and `probe.py`. | The product name may appear only at the places of 02 6; the adapter's platform key belongs to M2-33. |
| D-M2-32-7 | 2026-10-12 | Fixtures hold no third-party prose (Hermes texts only as `sha256:<hex>:<length>`). The pattern covers the adapter methods only (no hooks, attribute reads or the timer calls `send_typing`/`stop_typing`), renumbers IDs within itself, collapses runs of `send_draft` and of non-final edits of one streamed message, keeps only the last `set_status_text` call as the last entry (Hermes calls it from the agent thread, so its position among the event loop's calls depends on thread scheduling), drops no-op edits and compares everything else exactly. `inputs_sha256` covers the pin, the probe, the scenarios and the harness modules; a change without recording again fails the offline tests. | Repeat counts and hook timing depend on chunk timing and threads, not on Hermes' contract; foreign text could carry machine details. |
| D-M2-32-8 | 2026-10-12 | The harness deadlines are named constants in `tests/hermes_harness/limits.py`, no configuration keys. | They bound a test, not product behaviour (precedent of M1-73's probe). |
| D-M2-32-9 | 2026-10-12 | Scenarios beyond Design note 9, each as evidence for an adaptation: `tool_without_progress`, `busy_steer_global`, `busy_queue_global`, `busy_steer_command`, `interrupt_command`, `credential_login_file_optout`, `session_resume_same_thread`, `session_resume_admin`. The harness waits for the end of the gateway's startup restore before the first step (the probe asks the internal flag `gateway_runner._startup_restore_in_progress`; it decides timing only, never a fact) and for named calls instead of quiet periods where a step has a definite end. | An adaptation needs its own evidence; inbound during the startup restore is queued and replayed later, which made patterns depend on timing. |
| D-M2-32-10 | 2026-10-12 | Adaptation `progress_hooks`: 17.3 states the observed patterns; tool steps come from `set_status_text` (text verbatim as title, closed at `None`, the next status, draft or answer, or `on_processing_complete`); without drafts the buffered `edit_message` content is the source of `/last_text_excerpt`; `format_tool_event` and `render_message_event` keep their rows with "in `v2026.9.24` nicht aufgerufen"; the installer sets `display.platforms.<platform_key>.tool_progress: off`. | Measured: no caller of `format_tool_event` in the pinned gateway; progress bubbles arrive as `send` unless `tool_progress` is off. |
| D-M2-32-11 | 2026-10-12 | Adaptation `steer`: 17.5 names the global key `display.busy_input_mode` (one Hermes profile is one agent principal); `steer` stays a supported mode, `/steer <text>` is recorded as a further way. The report tells M2-33 that AG-13 covers `steer` and `queue` through the global key. | Measured: the per-platform key is never read; the global `steer` and `queue` behave as 09 7 expects. |
| D-M2-32-12 | 2026-10-12 | Adaptation `credential_type`: 17.8 names `hermes_cli.runtime_provider.resolve_runtime_provider()` with `agent.anthropic_credentials.anthropic_route_is_oauth(...)` as the source of the compatibility layer with a contract test against the pinned version (M2-33); RK-13 and T-AI-08 keep "nicht öffentliche Oberfläche". 10 10.1 gets the precedence and the rule that in `organization` the profile's home holds no Claude Code login file and sets `auth.adopt_external_logins: false`; "je Turn" stays. | Measured: no public surface; the internal answer equals the ground truth in all eight profiles; the login file is used as OAuth, the opt-out works, a `.env` change applies at the next turn. |
| D-M2-32-13 | 2026-10-12 | `session_switch` holds, and the ways go into 17.3, 17.4 and 9.5 point 2: `/new` through `handle_message` answered via `tools.slash_confirm.resolve(..., "once")`; `/resume <id> --all` sent for the adapter's admin sender listed in `PlatformConfig.extra.group_allow_admin_from` (a plain `/resume` binds only a session of the same thread); the session ID comes from the hook `pre_llm_call`. | Measured: plain `/resume` of another thread is blocked, the admin form binds it; the hook is a documented plugin surface. |
| D-M2-32-14 | 2026-10-12 | `docs/RISKS.md` is not on the base branch of this ticket, so RK-12 and RK-13 are not edited here; the column "Gegenmaßnahme" gets this addition when the file is ported: RK-12 "Mitschnitt gegen die gepinnte Version (`plugins/hermes-adapter/tests/hermes_pin.toml`) in jedem Testlauf; er fand: kein Aufruf von `format_tool_event`, `busy_input_mode` nur global, Sitzungs-ID über den Hook `pre_llm_call`"; RK-13 "Mitschnitt in jedem Testlauf; keine öffentliche Oberfläche, die interne Auflösung von Hermes stimmt in allen Profilen; Login-Datei von Claude Code wird genutzt, Abschaltung per `auth.adopt_external_logins: false`". Ratings unchanged. T-AI-08 stays unchanged (no public surface). | Creating the file here would collide with the plan port that brings it. |
