| M1-36/4 | 2026-10-13 | A provider name is trimmed, not empty, passes the display-text check (SEC-IN-4) and has at most `principal.display_name_max_chars` characters; no new configuration key. | The brief names no length; an unbounded display text is refused everywhere else, and the display-name limit is the named key for display texts. |
| M1-36/5 | 2026-10-13 | The schemas of [08 section 2.6](spec/08-identitaet.md) are enforced with these refinements, written into 2.6: an issuer with a path segment `common`, `organizations` or `consumers` is refused (use `{tenantid}` with `allowed_tenant_ids`); `{tenantid}` appears at most once and only in the path; `allowed_tenant_ids` is refused without the placeholder, each id is `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]*$` and unique; `client_key_alg`, `client_key_id` and `client_cert_x5t_s256` belong to `private_key_jwt` only, `client_key_alg` is normalized to `PS256` when absent, the thumbprint is 32 bytes as base64url without padding; `app_redirect_uris` entries are `https` with a host or a private-use scheme in reverse domain form, without user information or fragment, unique; header names are stored in canonical form and `name_header` differs from `login_header`; secret references must parse with `platform/secrets` (`file:` or `env:`), `azkv:` and `awssm:` are refused while they have no driver. | Brief test list ("/organizations/ issuer", inline secret, invalid reference) and fail closed: a configuration that can never sign in, or that names a tenant check that never runs, is refused at write time. |
| M1-36/6 | 2026-10-13 | `deleteIdentityProvider` answers `409 conflict` with reason `provider_in_use` and hint "disable the provider instead" while an identity (also an unlinked one) or a claim group references the provider; the restrict violation (SQLSTATE 23001) is mapped without the constraint name. | 03 `groups.provider_id` is `ON DELETE RESTRICT` as well, so a provider with claim groups cannot be deleted either; one answer for both keeps the hint true. |
| M1-36/7 | 2026-10-13 | `Providers.Enabled` (and `Identities.Resolve` through it) takes a share lock on the provider row inside a unit of work, and `Resolve` a share lock on the identity row; disabling and unlinking take the row lock before they read the live sessions. | A login that resolved an identity commits its session before a concurrent disable or unlink reads the sessions it must end, and a login after the disable sees the provider disabled; without the locks a session could start through a path that was just closed (R-ID-12). |
| M1-36/8 | 2026-10-13 | `identity.read` and `identity.unlink` answer `404 not_found` for another person when the caller is no owner (authz reason `not_self`), the same as for an unknown id. | Brief "Credential overview" and "Unlink" (others `404`), as `listUserSessions` does (M1-34). |
| M1-36/9 | 2026-10-13 | `Link` stores `email_at_link` only when `EmailHint` is one plain address (`domain.CheckEmail`), `issuer` only for `oidc` (required there); the subject must be non-empty valid UTF-8 without NUL. | The schema `Identity.email_at_link` has `format: email`; a hint of another form would break the response. Issuer and subject rules follow 03 `user_identities`. |
| M1-36/10 | 2026-10-13 | An unlink of an identity that is already unlinked answers `204` and records the allow with `no_change`; a provider update without effect records `no_change` and keeps `updated_at`. Audit details: `identity.unlink` names `identity_id`, `provider_id`, `provider_kind` and `sessions_revoked`; `identity_provider.manage` names the provider as resource, `provider_kind`, `changed_fields` and on a disable `sessions_revoked`, never configuration values or the name. | 06 section 7 (DELETE) and section 2 "Ohne Wirkung" as in M1-31; SEC-LOG-4 keeps values out of the audit. |
| M1-36/11 | 2026-10-13 | `Resolve` compares the issuer only for `oidc` (08 section 3.1 step 1) and answers `unmatched` for an empty subject; it never reads `EmailHint`, `DisplayName`, `users.email` or `email_at_link` (ID-T04). | R-ID-02. |
| M1-36/12 | 2026-10-13 | The migration `00013_identity_providers.sql` adds the deferred foreign keys `auth_sessions.identity_id` (M1-34/10) and `groups.provider_id` (M1-27); the schema tests of those migrations now expect them, and the claim group fixture of `authz/app` `TestGroups` inserts its provider first. `bothy_app` gets `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE` on `identity_providers` and `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` on `user_identities`. | The schema tests hold the latest schema; a provider without identities is deleted for good (08 section 3.3), an identity ends by `unlinked_at`. |
| M1-36/13 | 2026-10-13 | `identity.unlink` locks only the person's active identities (08 section 3.3), not the methods of other kinds it counts. Every later writer that removes a counted method (device revocation and expiry, M1-45; passkey revocation, M1-40; removal of local credentials, M1-39) locks the person's active identities in ascending id before it changes that method, so an unlink cannot pass on a count that a concurrent removal has already made stale. The removal itself stays allowed to take the last method: a lost device must always be revocable (08 section 10), only `identity.unlink` refuses with `no_login_method_left`. | Red team M1-36 (Minor 2 and its re-review): none of these writers exists on this base, so the race cannot occur yet; the rule belongs to the tickets that add them, and it orders the two writers without adding a last-method check to revocation. |
| M1-36/14 | 2026-10-13 | Secret references in provider configurations are checked for syntax only (brief, 19 section 10); any `file:` or `env:` reference is accepted. Before the OIDC driver (M1-41) resolves a reference it must confine provider references to a place no server key uses: a subdirectory of `<secrets.dir>` reserved for identity providers or an `env:` prefix reserved for them (not `BOTHY_SECRET_`), and refuse a reference equal to any configured server `*_ref` value. Otherwise an owner could send the token pepper or another server secret to an issuer as client secret. | Red team M1-36 (Minor 3 and its re-review): nothing is resolved before M1-41, so the risk starts there; the server's own secrets live below `<secrets.dir>` and use `BOTHY_SECRET_` (docs/operations/configuration.md), so a plain directory or prefix rule would not protect them. |

## M1-37 First setup, enrollment codes and setup lock

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-37/1 | 2026-10-13 | Base `int/m1-wave11` at `20645565`, branch `feat/M1-37-identity-bootstrap` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step (as M1-36/1). |
| M1-37/2 | 2026-10-13 | W3 and W5 are resolved as [PLAN section 7](PLAN.md) and the spec state them: the host commands are `bothy setup bootstrap-owner` and `bothy setup issue-enrollment-code` ([02 section 3.3](spec/02-architektur.md), [08 section 4](spec/08-identitaet.md)); a code is redeemed with device keys only through `redeemEnrollmentCode` ([08 section 3.2](spec/08-identitaet.md)), and the redeeming device becomes `active`. | Brief "Scope" (spec first); the implementation follows it. |
| M1-37/3 | 2026-10-13 | The setup gate (`httpserver.SetupGate`) wraps the mux of the public listener in step 4 of the chain (`Chain.UseGate`), after the correlation ID and the client IP, before authentication and rate limiting; the internal listener is untouched. It lets through only the operations whose action in the generated route table is `instance.discovery.read` (resolved with `(*http.ServeMux).Handler`, never a hand list); everything else, unknown paths and `/api/v1/gateway` included, gets `503 setup_required` without `Retry-After`. The first `true` of the setup state is cached in an `atomic.Bool` for the process; a read error is `503 unavailable` (fail closed). | Brief "Setup gate", [08 section 4](spec/08-identitaet.md), R-ID-07, T-ID-04: a token must not change the answer, and setup never reverts, so the cache cannot go stale; the bootstrap takes effect at the next request without a restart. |
| M1-37/4 | 2026-10-13 | A code is 20 bytes of `crypto/rand` as 32 Crockford Base32 characters, shown as `en_` plus eight groups of four joined by `-`. Input is trimmed, an `en_` prefix in any case dropped, `-` and spaces removed, upper-cased, `O` read as `0` and `I`, `L` as `1`; anything else than 32 characters of the alphabet is no code and is never looked up. Stored is only `HMAC-SHA-256(pepper, "enrollment-code/v1" 0x00 canonical)` with the token pepper. | Brief "Enrollment code format and storage", [08 section 3.2](spec/08-identitaet.md), 03 Grundsatz 9, ID-T27. |
| M1-37/5 | 2026-10-13 | `CompleteLogin` takes the existing port types: `LoginCompletion.Device` is `identity.RegisterDevice` (its `UserID`, `State` and `ApprovedBy` are set by the use case) and `LoginResult` holds `identity.Session`, `identity.Device` and `identity.TokenSet`; the use cases live on `app.Service`, which also implements the new incoming port `identity.Enrollment` for the two operations. A device confirmed by a code records the person as approver (`approved_by`), because the M1-33 rule requires an approver for an active device and the code is the person's own confirmation. | The brief names `DeviceRegistration`, `SessionView`, `DeviceView` and `TokenPair`, which M1-33 and M1-34 built as these types; a second set of names would only convert. |
| M1-37/6 | 2026-10-13 | The allow entry of `auth.login.complete` names the person who logged in as actor, with the new session and device, and the session as resource; the details are `{auth_method, device_id, session_id, new_device, device_state}`. A rejected code is one aggregated denial (`unauthenticated`, cause and the matched code id, never the code); a rejected login with a matching code is a denial with cause `login_rejected` after `RecordFailedAttempt`. Only rejections of the request (400, 401, 409, 422) count as failed attempts, never a server failure. | The request itself is anonymous, but a login is the person's action; the generic per-action audit writes no allow entry, so `CompleteLogin` writes exactly one (test). A database outage must not burn a person's code. |
| M1-37/7 | 2026-10-13 | `RecordFailedAttempt` refuses to run inside a unit of work (kind internal) and opens its own short one; `Match` and `MarkUsed` require the ambient unit. `Issue` locks the person's principal row before it ends the other codes, so two concurrent issues leave one valid code. | The redeeming unit holds the row lock; counting inside it would deadlock or roll back with it (brief "Failed attempts"). |
| M1-37/8 | 2026-10-13 | The info log line of a setup command carries the action and, when the telemetry pseudonym key is readable, `bothy.principal.pseudo_id`, but not the handle; the expiry goes to stderr as a text line, because the log allowlist has no time key. Neither carries the code. | 19 SEC-LOG-4 (people only as pseudonym) is the core rule; the brief's "handle" in the log line would break it. |
| M1-37/9 | 2026-10-13 | `bothy setup` assembles the server parts it needs in `wire.Setup` (pool of `bothy_app`, audit, event log with its partitions, Authorizer with the rights hooks, signing keys, identity) and runs the partition maintenance of the event log once before the bootstrap. Configuration, secret and database errors exit `1`, invalid arguments (also a taken or invalid handle) `2`, a completed setup `3`, a pending setup `4` for `issue-enrollment-code`, a handle that is no active owner `2`. | 16 section 7.1 (`bothy_app`), ADR 0066 (`Keys.EnsureActive` before the transaction); the bootstrap appends `access.changed`, whose partition exists when `serve` ran, and the command must not depend on that. |
| M1-37/10 | 2026-10-13 | The instance port gets `LockForSetup` and `CompleteSetup` (both inside the unit of work), and the migration grants `bothy_app` the column right `UPDATE (setup_completed_at)` on `instance`; every other column stays read only. `createEnrollmentCode` documents its `409` (reason `user_not_active`) in `openapi.yaml`. `auth_sessions.last_ip` is written at login from the client IP. | 08 section 4 steps 1 and 6 run as `bothy_app`; a `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` needs an update right, and the column grant is the least one. The brief defines the `409`, which the contract lacked (proven wrong, spec first). |
| M1-37/11 | 2026-10-13 | A person whose code is rejected because the person is not `active` gets the same `401` as any rejected code and counts no attempt; `TestCreateEnrollmentCodeRights` checks an agent through the use case, which answers `401` until M1-44 makes agent tokens valid (then `403`). | 08 "Schutz der Anmeldung"; API tokens do not exist on this base. |
| M1-37/12 | 2026-10-13 | A disabled `local` provider closes the code path of a person with a local identity: `redeemEnrollmentCode` share-locks the provider and rejects the code with the same `401` as every rejected code (cause `local_provider_disabled`, no attempt counted), and `createEnrollmentCode` answers `409 local_provider_disabled` instead of issuing a code that could not be redeemed. A `recovery` code issued on the host (no creator) still logs an owner in, without the closed identity (`auth_sessions.identity_id` NULL). A code redeemed by a known `pending` device of the same person with the same key leaves the device `pending` (brief "CompleteLogin": state unchanged); M1-38 approves it. The plain code stays a `string` in `IssuedEnrollmentCode` (fixed by the brief) and is wrapped in the redacting `Token` at the port. | Red team M1-37 (Major): 08 section 3.3 and R-ID-12, disabling a driver prevents new logins, and M1-36/7 orders the login before the disable. Host access is the root of trust (16 section 4.3), so an owner must not be locked out by the disable. |

## M1-46 Instance settings, branding and discovery

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-46/1 | 2026-10-13 | Branding keys change only through `updateBranding` (and the logo operations of M3-10); `putInstanceSetting` rejects them with `400 validation_failed`, field `#path/key`, code `use_branding_endpoint`. | One action per write path and one audit action per change. |
| M1-46/2 | 2026-10-13 | The branding version and its ETag are the sum of the row versions of `branding.product_name`, `branding.accent_color` and `branding.logo_file_id` (absent row 0). An absent `accent_color` in `updateBranding` writes the default `spruce` as a value; no writer deletes a settings row (`bothy_app` has no `DELETE`). | Every write raises one of the three versions by one, so the sum rises strictly and serves as the optimistic lock (06 section 8 item 1); a deleted row would lower it. |
| M1-46/3 | 2026-10-13 | A registered key without a row has its registry default, `version = 0` and neither `updated_by` nor `updated_at`; `If-Match: "0"` matches it. Writing the value it already has (also the default of a key without a row) writes nothing, appends no event and answers `200` with the current setting; the audit entry carries `no_change`. | The stored row wins (03 `instance_settings`); a no-op must not raise a version or tell clients that something changed (05 section 6). `audit: always` of the action catalogue still records the call. |
| M1-46/4 | 2026-10-13 | The accent keys are the explicit list of ADR 0044 in `kernel/designkeys/accent.go`, not derived from the tokens; a test holds every accent among the generated category colours. | `granite` is the neutral palette of all surfaces, and a new category colour becomes an accent only after the contrast gate (ADR 0044). |
| M1-46/5 | 2026-10-13 | `kernel/designkeys` is a kernel-core package (02 4.5). | `chat` (M1-52, M1-53) needs the same keys and may not import `instance` (02 4.4). |
| M1-46/6 | 2026-10-13 | `local_password_enabled` in discovery is true when `auth.local_password_enabled` allows it and an enabled provider of kind `local` exists. | The password with TOTP needs a local account provider (08 section 2.6); offering it without one would show a login that cannot succeed. |
| M1-46/7 | 2026-10-13 | `listInstanceSettings` omits a registered key that has neither a row nor a default (`branding.logo_file_id` before a logo is set). | `InstanceSetting.value` is required and the API never sends `null` (ADR 0053); the key has no value to report. |
| M1-46/8 | 2026-10-13 | A platform with only `client.recommended_version.<p>` set appears in `min_client_versions` with `min_version: "0.0.0"`. | The contract requires `min_version`, and `0.0.0` admits every app version, which is what an unset minimum means. |
| M1-46/9 | 2026-10-13 | `updateBranding` reports both field errors (`/product_name`, `/accent_color`) of one request together; a concurrent first write of a branding key answers `412 version_conflict` with the branding read after the rollback. | One round trip shows every problem of the form; the concurrent writer committed a newer branding, so the caller's version is outdated. |
| M1-46/10 | 2026-10-13 | The instance settings are built in their own start step `settings` between `authz` and `identity` (`wire.NewInstanceSettings`); identity gets the read port `instance.Settings` through `IdentityWrites.Settings`, and discovery the sign-in ways through `identity/adapters/loginoptions` over the provider store. | `createUser` reads `locale.default` (PLAN row M1-46), so the settings must exist before identity; the provider store needs no use case of identity, so the source has no start order of its own. |
| M1-46/11 | 2026-10-13 | The integration tests of the six operations live in `identity/adapters/httpapi` beside the session world of M1-34 and M1-35. | They need the real authentication and step-up, whose helpers are test code of that package; the settings are wired with the same `wire` function as the server. |
| M1-46/12 | 2026-10-13 | `tools/ci/tests/test_product_name_places.py` allows the working name in code and configuration only in the forms that 02 6 now lists: module path, whole path segment (lower case), prefix (`bothy-`, `bothy_`, `bothy.`, `BOTHY_`), alert names `Bothy<Name>` defined in the alert rules (and the examples of the alert tooling), binary before a subcommand (in `cmd/bothy` also the command word and identifiers), a whole value outside the client code (`apps/`, `packages/`), display names in the tool files of 02 6, and the fixed advisory lock keys. It skips `docs/`, Markdown, lock files and code generated from `docs/`. The few prose uses in comments, alert summaries and Terraform descriptions were reworded, and the SQL dollar-quote tag in `platform/jobs/migrate.go` became `$schema$`. | The kinds of 02 6 did not cover the tool and operations ids, the default database name and the tool display names the repository has; the implementation proved the list incomplete (brief scope item 1), and these forms keep a change of the name a mechanical replace. |
| M1-46/13 | 2026-10-13 | Branch `feat/M1-46-instance-settings` from `int/m1-wave11` (`2064556`) in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, and the worktree stays for the PR lane. The migration is `00014_instance_settings.sql`. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step (as M1-36/1). |
| M1-46/14 | 2026-10-13 | Every writer of `instance_settings` first takes the transaction-scoped advisory lock `hashtext('bothy.instance_settings')` (`LockSettings`), then locks the rows of its keys. | Row locks alone let two `updateBranding` calls with the same `If-Match` change different keys without a row and both win (red team M1-46, reproduced by `TestUpdateBranding_ConcurrentWritersOneWins`); settings writes are rare owner actions, so one lock for all keys costs nothing. |

## M1-47 Feature flags

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-47/1 | 2026-10-14 | The registry in code owns key, kind, default, module and description; `FeatureFlagPut` has no `description` and requires `reason`. `feature_flags` has no `description` column, so an orphaned row is listed without a description (the brief's "description from the row" and "description copied from the registry" have no column to read or write). | ADR 0064 and 03 `feature_flags`: one owner of the registration; the API schema marks the description absent for orphaned keys. |
| M1-47/2 | 2026-10-14 | A registered flag without a row has its default and `version = 0`; `If-Match: "0"` matches it, and writing the default with an empty rollout writes nothing, appends no event and answers `200` (audit allow with `no_change`, as M1-46/3). | Same rule as the instance settings (03); a no-op must not raise a version or notify clients. |
| M1-47/3 | 2026-10-14 | Flag evaluation reaches groups and people through the source ports `FlagGroupSource` (authz/adapters/flagsource, `Membership.InEffect` of M1-27) and `FlagUserSource` (identity/adapters/flagsource), declared by `instance`. `ExistingGroupIDs` reads each group by its key (no new authz query), because a rollout is entered by hand and stays short. | `instance` may not depend on `authz` or `identity` (02 4.4); the source implementations own the validity rules. |
| M1-47/4 | 2026-10-14 | No flag cache; errors of the flag store or the group source are returned, never turned into "off". | One indexed read per call keeps a change in another process effective at once; a database fault shows as `5xx` instead of silently disabling a feature. |
| M1-47/5 | 2026-10-14 | `Admin` of `instance/app` implements `instance.Flags` and the new port `instance.FlagAdmin` (embedded in `instance.Admin`), with `AdminDeps.Flags`, `FlagGroups` and `FlagUsers` required; the use case is `PutFlag(ctx, actor, PutFlagCmd)` like the other use cases of the port, not `PutFlag(ctx, cmd)`. | One wiring point (`wire.NewInstanceSettings`) and the reuse of `instance.updated` and the step-up path of M1-46. |
| M1-47/6 | 2026-10-14 | The allow audit of `feature_flag.update` carries `key`, `enabled`, `rollout_user_ids`, `rollout_group_ids` and `reason`, instead of a nested `rollout` object. | The audit value kinds have lists but no nested objects (`kernel/auditing`); the content is the same, IDs only. |
| M1-47/7 | 2026-10-14 | `putFeatureFlag` checks in this order: authorization, `404` for a key that is not registered (orphaned too), `400` for the body (reason blank or with a forbidden character at `/reason`; every unknown rollout ID with the index as sent), then under the row lock `412`. A concurrent first insert of the same key makes the second writer read the new row: with `If-Match` it answers `412`, without it it writes over the new row. | A body error needs no lock; the rollout pointers name what the caller sent, before normalization. |
| M1-47/8 | 2026-10-14 | `wire.IdentityWrites.PersonalTokens` and `app.PersonalTokensDefault` are removed; `IdentityWrites.Flags` (required) binds `PersonalTokenSwitch` to `identity/adapters/instanceflags`. The switch evaluates for the actor of the request when it is the caller, else as a person. The integration tests set the flag through `feature_flags` instead of a fake switch. | Brief scope item 8: no stand-in remains. |
| M1-47/9 | 2026-10-14 | `make flag-check` also fails on a registered flag missing from configuration.md 7 and on a row there without registration; the table must list exactly the registered flags. It is repository-wide like `register-check` and skips only when `services/server/go.mod` is not tracked. | The brief's "differs from the registry in key" in both directions; COMPONENTS selects test runs, not the registry rules. |
| M1-47/10 | 2026-10-14 | Branch `feat/M1-47-feature-flags` from `int/m1-wave13` (`82693e5`) in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed; the worktree stays for the PR lane. The migration is `00018_feature_flags.sql`. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step (as M1-46/13). |

## Integration int/m1-wave11

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| INT-W11/1 | 2026-10-13 | M1-36 enters as one squash onto `5cd89cd` (`int/m1-wave10`); the ticket builds on that base, so there is no conflict and the tree equals the ticket head. On that tree the `coverage` gate fails: `services/server` reaches 79.0 % against the floor of 80 %, because the provider and identity code of `identity/app` and `identity/adapters/postgres` is exercised only by the HTTP tests of another package, which per-package coverage does not count. The integration adds tests in those two packages (`adapters/postgres/providers_test.go`, `app/manage_test.go`, more cases in `app/identities_test.go`) that run every store method and the provider and identity use cases against PostgreSQL; production code is unchanged. | Blocker of the integration: the gate also runs on GitHub CI. The ticket's own `make -k check` skipped `coverage` because its `test` target failed on an unrelated timing flake. Test-only fix, no change of ticket behaviour. |

## M1-44 API tokens with prefix and checksum

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-44/1 | 2026-10-13 | Base `int/m1-wave10` at `5cd89cd`, branch `feat/M1-44-identity-api-tokens` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally, the integration lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also in German chapters and READMEs. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and language (as M1-35/1). |
| M1-44/2 | 2026-10-13 | A personal token exists only for the person itself: the authz guard of `token.create` (07 section 4.2 step 9) refuses a holder of kind `user` other than the caller with `forbidden not_self`; the application layer reads the holder's kind from `PrincipalSource` into `domain.TokenFacts`. An unknown holder passes the guard, so the use case answers `404`. | The brief's ruling: a personal token is the person's own credential; an owner minting one for someone else could act as that person. |
| M1-44/3 | 2026-10-13 | The requirement of `token.create` is `Any(Owner, Self)`, `token.list` and `token.revoke` keep `Any(Self, Owner)`. When the requirement of `token.create` fails for a holder that is a person other than the caller, the reason is `not_self` (`domain.notSelfForPersonalToken`); for an agent or app holder it is the reason of the first atom, `owner_only` (also for the owner's personal token, SR5). | The brief requires `not_self` for a personal token of another person (08 section 12) and `owner_only` for `TestPersonalTokenOfOwnerHasNoOwnerRights`; `Any` reports the reason of its first alternative (M1-28). Red team round 1, minor 1. |
| M1-44/4 | 2026-10-13 | Personal tokens carry no scopes (`/scopes` must be empty for `pat` as for `agt`). | The brief's ruling: personal tokens act with the person's grants; 07 knows scopes only for apps. |
| M1-44/5 | 2026-10-13 | A found but revoked or expired token is not refused by the Authenticator: it returns the actor of the token's principal with the token id and `CredentialInactive` (new field of `kernel/actor.Actor`), and `authorize` refuses it in `check_active` with `401 unauthenticated` and the audit reason `token_revoked`. The Idempotency-Key stage (`platform/httpserver`), which runs before `authorize`, neither claims nor replays for such an actor. No DPoP proof is checked for an inactive token and `last_used_at` is not written. A missing token, a kind mismatch and every format failure are refused by the Authenticator (`401 unauthenticated`, no audit). | The brief asks for the audit reason `token_revoked`; only authorize knows the action of the request and audits a denial (07 section 4.2), as M1-34 does for sessions. Red team round 1 (major): without the flag the stage replayed a stored answer to a revoked or expired token, and to a revoked DPoP-bound token without its key. The two files outside the brief's scope (`kernel/actor`, `platform/httpserver`) change by one field and one condition. |
| M1-44/6 | 2026-10-13 | Revoking a token is a rights change: `revokeToken`, `Tokens.Revoke` outside a unit and every caller of `RevokeAllForPrincipal` raise `permission_version`; `Revoke` inside a unit that is no rights change and `RevokeAllForPrincipal` outside one fail with `internal_error`. `access.changed` is appended only where the M1-29 path computes a difference; a token changes no grant, so a revocation appends none. | authz caches token liveness with the rights snapshot per version (M1-28/12); without the new version a revoked token would stay live in the cache of every process. 05 marks `token.revoke` as a rights change; the brief says "as the path computes it". |
| M1-44/7 | 2026-10-13 | `revokeToken` authorizes in a first unit of work and revokes in a second one declared as the rights change; an already revoked token ends after the first with `204` and no new entry. | A refused or empty call must not raise the rights version; the second unit locks the row, so two concurrent revocations write one entry. |
| M1-44/8 | 2026-10-13 | `PersonalTokenSwitch` is bound to `app.PersonalTokensDefault` (always off) in `internal/wire` until M1-47 registers `tokens.personal_enabled` and rebinds it over `instance.Flags`; `wire.IdentityWrites.PersonalTokens` is the seam. Tests use a fake switch for both values. | The brief's binding; 16 section 6.2: an unregistered flag is off. |
| M1-44/9 | 2026-10-13 | `createToken` for an app answers `409 conflict app_not_installed` before the body is checked. | Apps cannot exist in M1; M2-24 replaces the answer with the installation check (brief). |
| M1-44/10 | 2026-10-13 | Besides the port `Tokens` of 02 section 4.3 the root package declares `TokenAdmin`, the use cases of the three operations (they authorize, `Tokens` does not). | As `People` beside `Directory` (M1-31): other modules use `Tokens` without an authorization of their own action, the handlers use `TokenAdmin`. The port name of 02 is unchanged. |
| M1-44/11 | 2026-10-13 | `auth.dpop_required_for` is a string list with the default `["agent"]` (the JSON form, so the chapter cell and the schema agree) and the cross-check `dpop_required_for`, which refuses values outside `agent`, `app` and `personal` and names only the key. | The brief asks to register the allowed values; the configuration kinds have no enum list. |
| M1-44/12 | 2026-10-13 | `Parse` accepts the kinds `agt`, `app`, `pat` and `whi` only; `hpc` (host pairing code, ADR 0061 addendum, 24 section 3.2) stays with the gitleaks rule and its own ticket, and `whi` is never accepted by the Authenticator. | Brief M1-44 lists four kinds; pairing codes are no API tokens of `createToken`. |
| M1-44/13 | 2026-10-13 | The tests of ID-T23 and the operations run in the session world of M1-34 (`sessions_env_test.go` with the token handlers and a fake `PersonalTokenSwitch`); step-up comes from the M1-35 flow. The cases run as named subtests of three groups (`TestAPITokens_Issue`, `TestAPITokens_Authenticate`, `TestAPITokens_Lifecycle`) with one world per group: a world costs seconds to build and costs only tenths per case, and with one world per case the package passed the default 10 minute test timeout of `make test` on a loaded machine. The code of the earlier session existed before these tests; mutation runs (format check after the lookup, no `Bearer` refusal of bound tokens, revocation without the rights change) turned `TestIDT23_WrongChecksumRejectedWithoutLookup`, `TestIDT23_DPoPBoundAgentTokenWithoutProof` and `TestRevokeToken_Immediate` red. | Test first could not be shown for code written before the session was lost; the mutations prove the tests catch the behaviour. |
| M1-44/14 | 2026-10-13 | The token examples of `docs/api/openapi.yaml` (`listTokens`, `createToken`, `ApiToken`, `ApiTokenCreated`) use a valid 60 character token with the marker `EXAMPLE` (the first checksum vector), the 6 character prefix `bothy_agt_EXAMPL`, `scopes: []`, `dpop_jkt` and `expires_at`; the server stub and the TypeScript client are regenerated. | Red team round 1, minor 3: the old examples were no valid tokens and showed scopes an agent token cannot have; the brief allows changing openapi.yaml where the implementation proves it wrong. |
| M1-44/15 | 2026-10-13 | Not changed after red team round 1: the table grant `UPDATE` of `bothy_app` on `api_tokens` (as every comparable table, 16 allows DML), `apitoken.Parsed` without redaction (never logged; it holds what the caller already has), no cross-check `auth.personal_token_max_ttl <= auth.api_token_max_ttl` (08 does not require it), no second authorization in the revoking unit of `revokeToken` (the window only decides whether one allow entry is written). The expiry is now cut to microseconds before the check (nit 5). | Nits of red team round 1 with their reason. |
| M1-44/16 | 2026-10-13 | An actor with `CredentialInactive` is keyed by the client IP in `ratelimit.RequestKey` (as the anonymous actor) and settles the failed-credentials precheck as `401 unauthenticated`, so its `rl.public_auth` token stays spent. This replaces the acceptance of the rate limiter point in M1-44/15. | Red team round 2 (major, reproduced): a revoked or expired token, also a revoked DPoP-bound token without its key, emptied the buckets of its principal with 429 for every live credential, and an expired row never goes away. The two stages run before authorize; the change is one condition each. |
| M1-44/17 | 2026-10-13 | The API token store of `adapters/postgres` and the port `identity.Tokens` get their own tests in their packages (`TestAPITokens_StoreRoundTrip`, `TestTokens_Port`); a `token_hash` collision on insert stays `internal_error` like a reused primary key, and the doc comment of `InsertAPIToken` says so. | `make coverage` counts each package by its own tests only, and the HTTP tests of `adapters/httpapi` left the server module under its threshold; the new store test showed that the comment promised a conflict the error mapping never gave. |

## M1-50 Audit verification, checkpoints and queries

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-50/1 | 2026-10-13 | Base `int/m1-wave10` at `5cd89cd`, branch `feat/M1-50-audit-verify` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and the worktree stays, the PR lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also in German chapters and READMEs (their German parts stay). | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and the brief's language (as M1-35/1). |
| M1-50/2 | 2026-10-13 | A break that `audit.chain_verify` detects ends the run without error and without a checkpoint: one more `bothy.audit.verify.failures`, an `audit.verify` entry with decision `error`, no actor and `{first_broken_seq, failure_reason}`, and the log line `audit chain verification failed`; River does not retry it. Storage, signing and sink errors fail the run, and River retries. See [02 section 12.3](spec/02-architektur.md) and [19 SEC-AUD-7](spec/19-sicherheit.md). | A retry cannot repair a broken chain and would only repeat the entry; the alert `BothyAuditChainBroken` on the counter takes over (brief "Job" item 2). |
| M1-50/3 | 2026-10-13 | The file sink writes `<path>/<instance_id>/<seq with 20 digits>.jws` with directory mode `0700` and file mode `0600` through a temporary file in the same directory (`O_EXCL`, synced), then a hard link to the name, then `fsync` of the directory; 19 SEC-AUD-7 said "rename" and now says link. `<path>` must exist; the sink never creates it. The same content again is accepted, other content fails and the file stays. See [19 SEC-AUD-7](spec/19-sicherheit.md). | A rename replaces an existing name (POSIX), so two writers of one seq could overwrite each other; `link` fails when the name exists (`TestSinkConcurrentPut`). A missing mount must not silently put the checkpoints on the database volume. |
| M1-50/4 | 2026-10-13 | `exportAuditEvents` streams the contiguous `seq` span from the smallest to the largest `seq` whose `occurred_at` lies in [`from`, `to`), including a row inside the span whose `occurred_at` lies outside it; the description in openapi.yaml says so. An error after the first line aborts the response (`http.ErrAbortHandler`). | An aggregated denial keeps the time of its first denial (05 "2.4"), so a strict time filter could drop a row from the middle and break the `prev_hash` link that makes the export verifiable offline (19 SEC-AUD-4); an aborted transfer is visible, a short complete-looking stream would not be. |
| M1-50/5 | 2026-10-13 | `verifyAuditChain` reports the failure with the smallest `seq` among the row failures of the walk, the first invalid sink checkpoint (at any `seq`, also outside the range) and a trusted sink checkpoint whose row is gone (below the first retained row when the range starts there, above the head when it ends there; `checkpoint_mismatch`). `from_seq` and `to_seq` below 1 and `from_seq` above `to_seq` (also after capping at the head) are `400 validation_failed`; a `from_seq` below the first retained row is raised to it. `last_checkpoint_seq` is the newest trusted sink checkpoint. | The brief's "first failure wins" needs one order; `seq` is the order of the chain. Deleting the newest or oldest rows must not pass as a shorter intact chain while the sink holds a checkpoint over them. |
| M1-50/6 | 2026-10-13 | The job walks only the rows after the last checkpoint of `audit_checkpoints`, after checking that checkpoint: its JWS verifies with a key of purpose `audit_checkpoint`, its payload names this instance and the stored `seq` and `hash`, and its row still has that hash. Without a sink checkpoints are still signed into the database and go to the sink once one is configured. | SEC-AUD-2 recomputes "den Abschnitt seit dem letzten Prüfpunkt"; the checks keep a changed database checkpoint from becoming the trusted start. Older rows are compared by `verifyAuditChain` against the sink (README "Limits of the verification"). |
| M1-50/7 | 2026-10-13 | The insert of a checkpoint takes an advisory transaction lock and reads the last checkpoint again; when it changed since the walk, the run inserts nothing. | Two processes with the role `all` could otherwise sign two checkpoints that link the same predecessor, a fork that the sink would report as `checkpoint_invalid`. |
| M1-50/8 | 2026-10-13 | The route table gets `Download` (a 2xx response with a media type that is neither `application/json` nor a `+json` type); `platform/httpserver` gives such a route the deadline `http.transfer_timeout` for the context and the write, like an upload. Of the enabled operations only `exportAuditEvents` is one. | The brief puts the export into "the route class of downloads, deadline `http.transfer_timeout`" (02 14.2), and the class was so far derived from the request body only. |
| M1-50/9 | 2026-10-13 | `instance.Keys.Verify(ctx, purpose, compactJWS)` is added to the port; it wraps every failure about the JWS in `instance.ErrJWSInvalid` and leaves the check of `typ` to the caller (the audit module requires `audit-checkpoint+json`). | The brief adds the method when the base lacks it; a database error must stay `503`/`500`, not become `checkpoint_invalid`. |
| M1-50/10 | 2026-10-13 | A stored row whose `details` is no JSON object or whose `prev_hash` or `hash` has another size reads with nil details or a zero hash instead of failing the read, so the walk reports `hash_mismatch` or `link_mismatch` at that row. | Such a row can only come from a change outside the server; an error would turn a detected tampering into a `500`. |
| M1-50/11 | 2026-10-13 | Audit details of the operations: `listAuditEvents` names the filters that are set (`actor_id`, `action`, `decision`, `resource_kind`, `resource_id`, `from`, `to`), `exportAuditEvents` `{from, to}`, `verifyAuditChain` `{from_seq, to_seq, ok}`; the recorder adds `ip_hmac` and `ip_key_id` as for every entry with a client IP. The filters `action` and `resource_kind` must be codes (`400 validation_failed`). The entry of a read is written in a unit of work of its own before the page or the stream is returned. | SEC-AUD-5 allows IDs, codes and times only; a filter that is no code cannot match a stored row anyway. A read whose entry cannot be written is not served. |
| M1-50/12 | 2026-10-13 | The response validator of `httpservertest` checks an `application/x-ndjson` body line by line against the schema of the media type. | The library reads a response body as one JSON value; every export would otherwise fail the contract check of the handler tests. |
| M1-50/13 | 2026-10-13 | The log line of a detected break carries `error.type` `audit_chain_broken` and `bothy.reason` with the failure reason, not the `seq`. | The attribute catalogue has no key for a sequence number (as M1-24); the `audit.verify` entry of the run names it. |
| M1-50/14 | 2026-10-13 | The port types are `audit.Query`, `audit.Page` and `audit.EventWriter`, not the brief's `AuditQuery`, `AuditPage` and `AuditEventWriter`. | The lint rule `exported` of revive refuses names that stutter with the package name (`audit.AuditQuery`); the meaning is the brief's. |
| M1-50/15 | 2026-10-13 | Red team round 1 (`NEIN`), all fixed with a test each: `listAuditEvents` writes every item as `EventJSON` (canonical row plus `hash`, three fractional digits), `TestListItemsAreCanonical`; until the anchors of M7 every walk that starts at the first retained row starts at row 1, so deleted oldest rows are `seq_gap` at 1 (`TestSECT16_DeletedOldestRowsAreDetected`; this replaces the trust in the first retained row of M1-50/5); the walk reads the stored hash of a row through `Rows`, so a hash of another size is a detected break, not a storage error (`TestWrongSizeHashIsABreak`); `verifyAuditChain` returns its result and the job ends without retry when the audit entry cannot be written, which is reported as `audit entry of the chain verification not written` (`TestDeletedNewestRowsStillAnswer`); the `to` filter has a test (`TestListToFilter`); the use cases have tests in their own package (`internal/audit/app/log_test.go`), which keep the Go coverage gate above its threshold. | A deleted newest row breaks the audit hook of M1-24 for every new entry; hiding the verification result from the owner then would hide the break itself. |
| M1-50/16 | 2026-10-13 | With a sink, every job run first reads the sink and reports `checkpoint_mismatch` at a sink checkpoint newer than the last checkpoint of `audit_checkpoints` (lost database checkpoints); every unexported checkpoint is checked like the last one (signature, payload, row) before it goes to the sink, else `checkpoint_invalid`; a sink that holds other content under the seq (`app.ErrCheckpointConflict`) is `checkpoint_mismatch`. All three are breaks (no checkpoint, counter, entry, no retry). A sink that cannot be read fails the run before it signs. | Red team round 1, minors: a deleted database checkpoint let the job fork or freeze the sink without a count, and a forged row would have been exported. |
| M1-50/17 | 2026-10-13 | The file sink refuses a link in place of the directory of the instance or of a checkpoint file, narrows a wider mode of the directory to `0700` and syncs the root after creating the directory; `ParseSink` takes exactly `file://` with an absolute path and refuses percent escapes and control characters. | Red team round 1, minors (`TestSinkRefusesLinksAndWideModes`, `TestParseSink`). |
| M1-50/18 | 2026-10-13 | Red team round 2 (`NEIN`), both fixed with a test each: the job reads the sink before the last checkpoint of `audit_checkpoints`, and the export reads the head after every batch of unexported checkpoints, so a run that overlaps the signing or the export of another run is no break (`TestLog_OverlappingRunsAreNoBreak`, `TestLog_OverlappingExportIsNoBreak`, the latter from the re-review of round 2); a row with a `seq` below 1 is `seq_gap` at that `seq` in the verification and the job (`TestLog_RowBelowOneIsABreak`). No `CHECK (seq >= 1)` on `audit_events`: the detection does not depend on it, and a role that can insert rows can append a correctly linked forged row at the head anyway. | A checkpoint reaches the sink only after its row committed, so a database read after the sink listing sees every listed checkpoint unless it was deleted; the walk starts at row 1, so a row below 1 was never checked and must not pass as checked. |

## M1-40 Passkeys

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-40/1 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave13` at `82693e5`, branch `feat/M1-40-identity-passkeys` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step (as M1-36/1 and M1-37/1). |
| M1-40/2 | 2026-10-14 | The RP display name is the RP ID. | The product name lives in the instance settings (M1-46); the display name is only a label, and a fixed value keeps the options independent of a setting that changes. |
| M1-40/3 | 2026-10-14 | Sign counter: when the stored and the received counter are both above zero and the received one is not greater, the ceremony continues, the higher value is kept and one audit entry is written with the action of the ceremony (`auth.login.complete` or `auth.step_up`), decision `allow`, resource `passkey` and details `{passkey_id, sign_count_regression: true, stored, received}`. Counters that stay zero (synced passkeys) are no regression. The passkey row is locked before the rule is applied, so two parallel uses keep the higher value. | [03 `passkey_credentials`](spec/03-datenmodell.md), [08 "2.5"](spec/08-identitaet.md) and [R-DM-31](spec/03-datenmodell.md): a regression is reported, not refused, because synced and cloned authenticators cannot be told apart. |
| M1-40/4 | 2026-10-14 | The decoy credential ID for a login name without passkeys is the first 32 bytes of `HMAC-SHA-256(token pepper, "passkey-decoy/v1" || 0x00 || lower case name)`. Residual risk: for a known name the number of real passkeys is visible, and a decoy and a real list differ in their transports. | [08 "2.5"](spec/08-identitaet.md): the answer must not reveal accounts; the context string separates this use of the pepper from codes and tokens, and the HMAC keeps the decoy stable for a name. |
| M1-40/5 | 2026-10-14 | When `auth.webauthn_rp_id` is empty and the host of `server.public_url` is an IP address, or when both passkey keys are empty and `server.public_url` is `http` on a host other than `localhost`, or when `auth.webauthn_rp_id` is empty and the host of `server.public_url` is no valid RP ID (a single label such as `bothy`), the instance has no passkeys: the start does not fail, the passkey operations answer `403 feature_disabled` (reason `passkeys_disabled`) and `createStepUpChallenge` has no `passkey_options`. An explicit RP ID or origin that breaks the rules of 08 "2.5" fails the start (cross-check `webauthn_relying_party`). | An IP address is never a valid RP ID (WebAuthn Level 3, "RP ID"), and an http origin outside localhost is no secure context, so browsers offer no passkeys there; installations reached by IP or plain http (tests, a private network) must keep starting; passkeys serve the web console and instances with a domain (08 "6"). Spec 08 "2.5" names the rule. |
| M1-40/6 | 2026-10-14 | Every completion that opened an issued ceremony row and then failed (refused response, rejected device, server failure) sets the row to `failed` in an own short unit of work after the rollback. Only a registration whose challenge belongs to another session leaves it untouched. | A row must work once (03 `auth_challenges` "Einlösen"); a rollback alone would leave it `issued`, and the same assertion could be sent again until `auth.login_flow_ttl` ends. |
| M1-40/7 | 2026-10-14 | A refused registration response (unknown, used, expired or foreign challenge, wrong challenge, rejected attestation, algorithm outside ES256, EdDSA and RS256) answers `400 validation_failed` with pointer `/credential` and writes no deny entry; a duplicate credential ID answers `409 conflict` (pointer `/credential`). | The brief fixes `400` for registration; a refused response is a bad request, not a denial of [07 "4.1"](spec/07-rechte.md), whose closed list of reasons has none for it. |
| M1-40/8 | 2026-10-14 | `completeStepUpWithPasskey` counts like a wrong signature (`failed`, toward `auth.step_up_fail_limit`) every response that does not confirm the challenge: another challenge in `clientDataJSON`, a passkey of another person, a revoked or unknown credential, a refused assertion, and a passkey completion of a challenge that was issued without `passkey_options`. An unknown, foreign, finished or expired challenge does not count, as in M1-35. | [08 "6.2"](spec/08-identitaet.md): a wrong confirmation counts, a challenge the caller could not have used does not. |
| M1-40/9 | 2026-10-14 | `passkey.register` is authorized at begin and again at complete; the conditional step-up is decided in `authz` through `stepUpOnlyWithExistingCredential` and `PrincipalSource.HasActivePasskey`, so a person who registered the first passkey between begin and complete needs a step-up at complete. | [07 "4.2" step 8](spec/07-rechte.md): rights logic stays in `authz` (B7); the second check closes the window between the two calls. |
| M1-40/10 | 2026-10-14 | An active passkey counts as a remaining sign-in method when an identity is unlinked (`PasskeySource`) only while the person keeps an active identity at the enabled `local` provider other than the one being unlinked; the credential overview lists the active passkeys oldest first. | [08 "3.3"](spec/08-identitaet.md) names the passkey as a way in, but a passkey logs in only with a local identity (08 "2.5"); counting it without one would let a person unlink the last usable identity (review finding). |
| M1-40/11 | 2026-10-14 | `user.name` of the creation options is the subject of the person's local identity, else the handle; `user.id` is the 16 bytes of the principal ID; a person who is not `active` gets no options (`401` for an enrollment, `400` for a registration). | Brief M1-40 "Enrollment with passkey"; the user handle carries no personal data. |
| M1-40/12 | 2026-10-14 | go-webauthn is pinned at `v0.18.2` (the Go proxy serves the tag); it is the only package that imports the library (`identity/adapters/webauthn`, vendor group `webauthn` in the module register). The adapter rebuilds the session data from the checked challenge, the RP ID of the configuration and required user verification; the allow list of the stored `webauthn_session.v1` and the person are enforced by the use case through the credential lookup. | Brief M1-40 fixes the port with JSON in and out and no ceremony state in the adapter. |

## Integration int/m1-wave12

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| INT-W12/1 | 2026-10-13 | M1-44 and M1-50 enter as one squash each onto `2064556` (`int/m1-wave11`), in this order, each with only its own commits (`5cd89cd..8d66478` and `5cd89cd..69299a3`). Append-only files (DECISIONS, the server CHANGELOG, `migrations.sha256`, the privileges table of the migrations test, `api/operations.yaml`, the enabled table of `api/routes/real_test.go`, `wire.Handlers`, the `Identity` struct, the handlers of the session world in `sessions_env_test.go`, the imports of `internal/wire/configchecks.go`) hold both sides; the identity README lists the M1-36 and M1-44 sections and names `Providers`, `Identities`, `Tokens` and `TokenAdmin` in one port line; the `platform/httpserver` README keeps the `CredentialInactive` note of M1-44 and the transfer class of M1-50. Generated files (sqlc models, `api/rest/rest.gen.go`, `api/routes/routes.gen.go`, `.go-arch-lint.yml`, `config-schema.json`, the configuration reference, the catalogue copies, the SDK clients) equal a fresh generation on the head. | The tickets were built and red teamed separately on `int/m1-wave10`; a union keeps each ticket's behaviour, and generated files are only evidence while they equal a fresh generation. |
| INT-W12/2 | 2026-10-13 | M1-36 (`00013_identity_providers.sql`, already on the base), M1-44 (`00013_identity_api_tokens.sql`) and M1-50 (`00013_audit_checkpoints.sql`) all added migration version 13. M1-36 keeps 13; M1-44 becomes `00014_identity_api_tokens.sql` and M1-50 `00015_audit_checkpoints.sql`, both with unchanged content and checksum. `migrations.sha256`, the identity and audit READMEs and the server CHANGELOG name the new files; the rulings of M1-44 and M1-50 keep the name the ticket wrote, as a record of the ticket. | goose refuses a set with a duplicate version; ADR 0007 rule 4 has the branch merged later renumber, and the ticket order of the wave decides. None of the three migrations is released. |
| INT-W12/3 | 2026-10-13 | `(*server).handlers` in `internal/wire/serve.go` fills the identity handlers through `(*Identity).fillHandlers` and the generic `fill`, which sets a nil handler to the built one; the result is unchanged (an injected handler stays, nothing is filled before its module started), held by `TestHandlers_FillsBuiltAndKeepsInjected`. | Semantic conflict found by `make lint` on the merged tree: with the `Tokens` branch of M1-44 and the `Log` branch of M1-50 next to the M1-36 branches the function reached cognitive complexity 18 (gocognit, limit 15), although each ticket passed alone. Moving the repeated nil checks into one helper is the smallest change that keeps the behaviour. |
