Audit details: `pairing_code` with `from_state`, `to_state`, `old_fingerprint`, `key_generation`, `sessions_ended`, `reason`; `confirm` with `result`, `fingerprint`, `key_generation`, `attempts_remaining`, `llm_credentials`, `credential_holder_id`, `declared_server_host`, `reason`; `host.pair` with `fingerprint`, `old_fingerprint`, `key_generation`, `platform`. Fingerprints as compact lower-case codes; the host is the resource, not a detail; `from_state` and `to_state` follow M2-39 instead of the brief's `state_before` and `state_after`. | Devices pin keys by generation (24 5.5); one vocabulary for state changes in the audit. |

## Integration int/m1-wave15

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| INT-W15/1 | 2026-10-14 | M1-42, M1-49, M1-52 and M2-39 enter as one squash each onto `fd504d9` (`int/m1-wave14`), in this order, each with only its own commits. Append-only files keep both sides (this file, the server changelog, `api/operations.yaml` sorted, the authz README status line, the test files of the fake store); the `Admin` port holds the access use cases of M1-49 and embeds `ClaimSync` of M1-42. Generated files are regenerated with the generators. | Instructions of the integration; the tickets were built on the same base in parallel. |
| INT-W15/2 | 2026-10-14 | Semantic conflict: M1-49 added `SpaceDetails` and `ChannelDetails` to `authz.StructureSource` and implemented them on the empty structure of `internal/wire`, which M1-52 replaced by `chatStructure` (the spaces of chat joined with `emptyChannels`). The chat source `authzsource.Source` now implements `SpaceDetails` from the chat store (unknown ids absent, archived spaces included), `emptyChannels` implements `ChannelDetails` (no channel until M1-53), and the chat HTTP test world passes `ChannelDetails` to its channel fake. Test code only: the authz HTTP test world of M1-49 writes the `spaces` row that the foreign key of M1-52 needs beside the space details. Test `TestSourceSpaceDetails`. | Without the change the server did not compile; M1-49 itself says the space details come with M1-52. |
| INT-W15/3 | 2026-10-14 | Migration 23 was used twice. M1-52 keeps `00023_chat_spaces.sql` and `00024_grants_space_fk_validate.sql`; M2-39 becomes `00025_terminal_hosts.sql`, content and checksum unchanged. `terminalVersion` of its migration test is 25; the changelog, the authz README and the grants constraint test name the new file. | Migration numbers are sequential and locked (ADR 0007); M1-52 enters first. |
| INT-W15/4 | 2026-10-14 | Semantic conflict in the access view: M1-52 made the spaces of `AccessViewOf` paths with `Archived` and added `SpaceLevels` (a level change of a space is `changed`), M2-39 added `hosts` and `AccessView.Hosts` with the helper `diffSet`. The merged `AccessViewOf(s, kind, principal, channels, spaces []Path, hosts []ids.ID)` fills all four maps; `diffSpaces` uses `diffSet` for added and removed spaces and then adds the level changes; `structureNow` reads the archived flag per space (M1-52) and the host ids (M2-39). Test code only: the call sites in `diff_test.go` pass both arguments, and the M2-39 scope test sets "view as" as `&actor.ViewAs{UserID, StartedAt}` of M1-49. | Both rules hold: archiving a space reports its level, a host is added or removed with `terminal`. |
| INT-W15/5 | 2026-10-14 | Start steps: 6f chat (M1-52), 6g terminal (M2-39); `moduleRegistrations` ends with `chat.Register, terminal.Register`; the Authorizer, the rights hook and the Admin use cases get `Structure: NewStructureSource(pool)` (M1-52) and `Hosts: NewHostSource(pool)` (M2-39); the server holds both modules and fills `Spaces` and `Hosts` of the handlers. The operation list, the route table and the stub server of `real_test.go`, the configuration keys and their tests, the table privileges and `sqlc.yaml` hold both sides. ## M1-55 Threads

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-55/0 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave16` at `1e260ad`, branch `feat/M1-55-chat-threads` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally, the integration lane ports it. The migration takes the next free number of the base, `00028`. Text this ticket adds is English. The methods of `chat.Threads` take the caller as `a actor.Actor` like `chat.Structure` (M1-52/5, M1-53/0); the brief's signatures leave it out. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and language; one convention for every chat port. |
| M1-55/1 | 2026-10-14 | `listChannelThreads` (D6): a `tag_id` that is no tag of the channel, unknown or deleted, gives an empty page, not an error. The list authorizes `thread.read` on the channel as container (a thread resource without an id and with the channel as path hint), then each row in filter mode, in batches of the page size plus one. The cursor is the keyset `(sort time, id)` bound to caller, channel, state, tag and sort; another caller, filter or sort gives `400 cursor_invalid`. The codec is `httpserver.EncodeCursor` behind the port `app.Cursors`, as in agents. | The brief's ruling D6: an error for an unknown tag would let a caller probe the tags of a forum. |
| M1-55/2 | 2026-10-14 | `thread.placeholder_titles` defaults to `["Neuer Chat", "New chat", "Chat", "Ohne Titel", "Untitled", "Test"]` without the empty entry of 09; chapter 09 says so. The empty title is a placeholder through the rule itself, and a configured empty entry still matches only the empty title. | A list element of the configuration is never empty (`platform/config`); 09 section 12 point 2 already makes an empty title `auto`. |
| M1-55/3 | 2026-10-14 | `setThreadTitle`: a person sets the source `manual`; an agent or app keeps the source and gets `409 title_manual` on a `manual` title, not only the operator agent. Who may call at all stays with `authorize` (write and creator, or manage, or the operator agent). | 09 section 12 point 1 and brief requirement 12: a manual title is never overwritten automatically; agents and apps are the automatic writers. |
| M1-55/4 | 2026-10-14 | D8 finding in authz: `Path.ThreadOperator` fell back to the channel's effective operator for a thread of kind `thread` without a fixed operator, so a later channel operator counted as the operator of such a thread (`thread.title.update`, `agent.invoke`). It now returns the fixed operator, zero for none; main threads and channels keep the channel's operator. The level rule is unchanged: the channel operator keeps `write` on every thread of the channel through the channel. One fixture of `TestAgentInvoke_MentionRule` gets the fixed operator its thread would have (R-DM-12). | 09 section 3.1 and R-DM-12: the operator of a thread is `threads.operator_agent_id`, fixed at creation; "Ohne Bediener antwortet kein Agent". |
| M1-55/5 | 2026-10-14 | Thread actions are not audited (05 column Audit "nein"); a call without effect appends no event. `setThreadTags` also sends `ETag`, which `openapi.yaml` declares. `AppendToThread` sets `last_activity_at` from the server clock instead of `now()`, so the tests control the order. Close, reopen, title and tags lock the thread row after `authorize`; `AppendToThread` locks first and authorizes `thread.reopen` only for an implicit reopen. | 05 section 4.5; the contract of `setThreadTags`; the same value inside one transaction. |
| M1-55/6 | 2026-10-14 | Test first held for the domain rules, the migration and the authz fix (red runs in the report); the HTTP and port tests over PostgreSQL were written after the use cases and checked against mutations of the tag deletion and of the operator fallback. | Honest record of the order of work. |

## M1-39 Local accounts with password and TOTP

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-39/1 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave13` at `82693e5`, branch `feat/M1-39-identity-local-accounts` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. If M1-40 enters first, both entries of `stepUpOnlyWithExistingCredential` and both `PrincipalSource` methods stay. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step (as M1-37/1). |
| M1-39/2 | 2026-10-14 | TOTP is enrolled in two steps as the spec states (08 section 2.5, `LocalCredentialSet` with `password`, `totp_enroll`, `totp_code`): `totp_enroll` seals a new 20-byte secret, sets `totp_enabled_at` and `totp_last_step` to NULL and returns the otpauth URI once; a valid `totp_code` sets `totp_enabled_at` (if NULL) and the step. The members of one request apply in the order `password`, `totp_enroll`, `totp_code`, and any rejected member rolls the whole request back; a re-enrollment of an established path makes it pending again until confirmed. The URI is marked with `idempotency.MarkOneTimeSecret`. | Brief "SetLocalCredential"; the spec counts the path as established only with a confirmed secret, so a new, unconfirmed secret cannot keep it established. |
| M1-39/3 | 2026-10-14 | `authz` learns "credentials exist" from `PrincipalSource.HasEstablishedLocalCredential(ctx, userID)` (a row of an active identity of the person with `totp_enabled_at IS NOT NULL`). The map `stepUpOnlyWithExistingCredential` lives in the new file `authz/app/stepup.go`: step 8 itself is in the pure `authz/domain/decide.go`, which may not call a port, so `gather` asks the source for the person of the resource (its id for a `user` resource, else its owner) and sets `Input.NoCredentialYet`; `decidePrincipalRules` then skips only the freshness check of a `bedingt` action, never the check "human with a session". `passkey.register` stays "ja" until M1-40 adds its entry. | 07 section 4.2 step 8 and B7 (the rule stays in `authz`); the brief fixes the map name and signature for a clean merge with M1-40. |
| M1-39/4 | 2026-10-14 | The otpauth issuer is the host of `server.public_url`; label `issuer:login name`, every part URL-escaped. | Brief "TOTP": the display name lives in the instance settings (M1-46), which this ticket must not depend on. |
| M1-39/5 | 2026-10-14 | AEAD scheme of the TOTP secret: AES-256-GCM, key = the 32 bytes behind `auth.totp_key_ref` as hex (the form `bothy secrets init` writes for `totp_key`, generator `key`), 12-byte random nonce, AAD `"totp/v1" || 0x00 || identity_id`, stored `0x01 || nonce || ciphertext and tag`, the sealing ref in `totp_key_ref`. The start resolves the current key once in the identity step (`wire.TOTPBox`); a missing key or another length stops it. `Open` of a row with another ref resolves that ref through the secret provider (bounded by 5 seconds, its port has no context). | Brief "Sealing", 19 secrets table: a new key applies to new rows while old rows stay readable; the identity in the AAD keeps a copied ciphertext from opening for another person. |
| M1-39/6 | 2026-10-14 | Common passwords: SecLists `10k-most-common.txt` at `3a5f62f` (MIT), stored only as the first 16 hex characters of `SHA-256(lowercase(NFC(password)))`, sorted and unique (adapter README). The NFC normalisation lives in `adapters/passwordpolicy` (vendor group `xtext` now also allows `golang.org/x/text/unicode/norm`); `domain.CheckPassword` gets the NFC form, and app gets the extra outbound port `PasswordNormalizer` beside the three ports the brief fixes. | The domain and app layers may not import vendors (02 "Schichten im Modul"); no word list in the repository (white-label scan, size). |
| M1-39/7 | 2026-10-14 | Failure window: the column `local_credentials.fail_window_started_at` (03) starts with the first failure; a failure at or after its end starts a new window; reaching `auth.login_fail_limit` sets `locked_until`, clears count and window and writes a deny entry on the person with `{lock: true, identity_id}` in the same unit of work as the counter. A wrong TOTP code with the right password, a person that is not active and a path without confirmed TOTP count as failures of an existing row; a locked row counts nothing. Every rejection is one aggregated denial `{cause, identity_id}` (no identity for an unknown name), never the login name, the password or the code. | 08 section 13 (failures per account: password and TOTP), brief step 6; the login name could be a mistyped password. |
| M1-39/8 | 2026-10-14 | Exactly one Argon2id computation per rejected request: `Verify` for an existing row, also while locked, else `VerifyDummy` against a hash computed at start with the current parameters. Only a successful login with changed parameters computes a second hash (the rehash) after the verification. `TestIDT21_UnknownNameTimingEqualsWrongPassword` takes the samples in pairs back to back and compares the median of the pair differences, which cancels the drift of a loaded machine; without the race detector it measures the real Argon2id, under it (as in `make check`) Argon2id is instrumented to hundreds of milliseconds with a spread far above `authz.not_found_timing_tolerance`, so the test swaps in a hasher of constant cost and measures the database work, where an unknown name could still differ. `TestDummyUsesConfiguredParameters`, `TestStaleParametersCostLikeUnknownNames` and the case `TestLockedAccountStillHashes` hold the one computation of equal cost. The integration cases run as subtests of `TestLocalAccounts` on one world, as M1-44/13, and the timing test runs in parallel with the package. | 08 section 13, ID-T21; a rehash only happens after the password was proven, so it reveals nothing about unknown names. |
| M1-39/9 | 2026-10-14 | The Argon2id floors are the minimums of the configuration keys (`auth.argon2id_memory_kib` 19456, `auth.argon2id_iterations` 2, `auth.argon2id_parallelism` 1), so the start and `bothy config check` stop with a problem that names the key (ID-T24); the hasher refuses them too. Upper bounds keep a configuration from exhausting the server: memory 4194304 KiB, 1000 passes, parallelism 255 (the limit of the library), `auth.password_min_length` 1024. | Brief "Argon2id"; the configuration loader already reports every key problem before the start. |
| M1-39/10 | 2026-10-14 | `wire.(*Identity).BindLocal(sealer, hasher)` builds the local accounts after `BindSessions` in the start step `sessions`; `nil` builds the Argon2id hasher of the configuration, tests pass a counting one. `bothy setup` does not build them. The allow entry of `local_credential.set` carries `{identity_id, password_changed, totp_enroll_started, totp_confirmed}`. | The login completion of `BindSessions` is a collaborator; the host commands never log in with a password. |
| M1-39/11 | 2026-10-14 | The login verifies the password on a read of identity, person and row without locks and only then opens the unit of work, which reads them again with the row `FOR UPDATE` and the provider and identity share-locked, and decides there (lock, counter, TOTP step, login). A row whose hash changed in between is rejected without counting (cause `credential_changed`); an unknown name runs the same lookups in its unit of work. This deviates from step 3 of the brief, which locks the row before the hash. | With the lock held during Argon2id, parallel attempts on one known name queued behind each other while those on an unknown name did not, so the time revealed accounts and an attacker could hold the row of a person; the counter stays exact because it is decided under the row lock (code review M1-39). |
| M1-39/12 | 2026-10-14 | Every verification of `passwordhash` lasts at least the running average cost (weight 1/4) of the most expensive parameter set it has seen; the average of the configured parameters is learnt at construction. | Code review M1-39 (Major): after a change of `auth.argon2id_*` the rows keep their old parameters until their next login rehashes them (08 section 2.5), so without padding a wrong password on such a row took another time than an unknown name, which verifies with the configured parameters. |

## M4-03 Accent variants

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-03-0 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave17` at `e2f9eb4`, branch `feat/M4-03-accent-variants` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also inside the German chapters and generated pages. The base has no section 10 in this file, so the rows stand in a section per ticket. The tool's ruff configuration selects no `D` rules; the new code carries docstrings anyway, no lint rule is added. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and language; merged layout wins over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-03-1 | 2026-10-14 | The rules member `accent_variants` of `design/design-rules.json` names the default (`spruce`), the seven accents of 01 section 12 and the eight swapped roles. `tokenkit/accents.py` validates it (default in the accents, every accent a palette scale, every role an opaque colour in both themes) and raises a `RuleError` naming the member. A role is swapped in a theme only where it aliases `palette.spruce.<step>` directly; otherwise it is kept and shown as "not swapped". | One place for the list the server, the apps and the gate share; a role that does not follow the brand scale (dark `color.focus.ring` on `gorse`) must not change with the accent. |
| D-M4-03-2 | 2026-10-14 | Step search: a variant starts on the same step of the chosen scale (nearest step by number if missing, a tie goes darker in light and lighter in dark) and, while one of its pairings fails, moves one step towards more contrast (darker when the role is the darker colour of the failing pair, else lighter) until all pass; a scale end, a flat scale or a revisited step fails the build with `accent <a>, theme <t>: no step of <scale> meets <pairing>`. `moved_from` records the start step and `contrast.md` shows it. With today's palette no variant moves. | A fixed step on a lighter scale cannot always meet AA, while 01 section 12 promises that every listed accent works; the moved steps stay visible. |
| D-M4-03-3 | 2026-10-14 | `DsAccent` is a separate `ThemeExtension` with one constant per theme and accent (`lightSpruce` ... `darkMoss`) and `DsAccent.of(key, brightness)`, an unknown key giving the default; it is not a fourth resolver modifier. `DsColors` keeps its fields. In CSS the light/dark default theme block of an accent also covers an element without `data-theme`, and a `prefers-color-scheme` block covers the other theme, as for the base roles; `data-accent` sits on the same element as `data-theme`. | The tool allows a token to depend on one modifier only, and an accent role always combines with the theme; existing code keeps compiling. |
| D-M4-03-4 | 2026-10-14 | Guard scope: the palette guard "Marke und Akzent" keeps checking the base roles only; accent variants of an instance are not checked against it. The Discord guard covers every scale. Recorded in the "Verbotsliste" of the design README. | 01 section 12 allows `heather`, `rowan` and `amber` deliberately as the instance owner's choice from the checked palette, while the product brand stays spruce; contrast applies to the variants in full. |
| D-M4-03-5 | 2026-10-14 | An accent without a passing step is reported as a failed gate (exit 1, nothing written, the other gate messages still listed); an invalid `accent_variants` member stays an error of the rules file (exit 2), as for other rule errors. | The brief's test asks for exit 1 when a variant falls below AA; an invalid rules file is a different failure. |

## M7-19 API reference in the docs site

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M7-19/0 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave17` at `e2f9eb4`, branch `feat/M7-19-api-reference-docs` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the integration lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English. Rulings use the row form of this file (`M7-19/<n>` under a ticket heading) instead of the brief's `D-M7-19-<n>` in a section 10, which this file does not have on the base. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged names win (D-W-100). |
| M7-19/1 | 2026-10-14 | Generator (Design note 1): an in-repository MkDocs hook, `tools/docs/mkdocs_api_reference.py`, renders both documents to static Markdown with the pinned `pyyaml` and `mkdocs`; no new dependency. Compared with the pinned Redocly CLI 2.62.1 `build-docs` (plus the AsyncAPI HTML template for the gateway). Evidence: `redocly build-docs docs/api/openapi.yaml` rendered the OpenAPI 3.1 document without errors, but its default page loads `redoc.standalone.js` from `cdn.redocly.com` and a Google Fonts stylesheet, is one 6.9 MB HTML file outside the site's navigation and search, and renders no AsyncAPI; the gateway would need `@asyncapi/cli` and its template as a further npm tree (not in `tools/node`, not tried). The hook renders the 304 operations, 35 webhook events, 560 schemas and 93 gateway messages offline, with no script, in the site's theme, search and strict link and anchor validation; Apache-2.0 as the repository; maintained here. This replaces the suggestion `redocly build-docs` of ADR 0045. | Requirement 9 and 13 (no CDN, no external asset) and requirement 12 (both references in every build); the static hook meets them without bundling a JavaScript application. |
| M7-19/2 | 2026-10-14 | Generated pages live only in the build: `api/reference/rest.md`, `api/reference/schemas.md` and `api/reference/gateway.md` are added as generated files in `on_files` and listed in the nav under API; nothing is committed. A committed file at one of these paths, a missing document, an unresolved local `$ref` or two equal anchors fail the build. Anchors are `op-<operationid lower case>`, `schema-<Name>`, `response-<Name>`, `header-<Name>`, `security-<name>`, `channel-<key>`, `operation-<key>` and `message-<key>` (characters other than letters, digits, `_` and `-` become `-`). Text of the documents is used as Markdown with `<` escaped outside code spans; a code span wrapped over two lines is joined. `docs/api/README.md` names the site paths in code, not as links, because the pages do not exist for `make linkcheck`. | Requirement 12: a stale generated file cannot be committed; the strict build validates every generated link and anchor. |
| M7-19/3 | 2026-10-14 | External asset rule: `tools/docs/external_assets_check.py site` runs in `make docs` after the build and fails on a `<script src>`, a `<link href>` with rel `stylesheet`, `preload`, `modulepreload` or `prefetch`, or a CSS `url(...)` or `@import` (in `<style>`, `style` attributes and `.css` files) whose URL names a host. Anchors and links that load nothing (`rel="canonical"`) pass, `data:` URLs pass, an empty site fails. Requests a bundled script makes at run time are out of its reach; the known one is Material's Mermaid loader (CDN, accepted in ADR 0045 "Consequences"), which the reference pages do not use. | Requirement 13 in the form the brief gives (a check over the built HTML); the run-time loader is an existing, recorded decision and outside this ticket's scope. |
| M7-19/4 | 2026-10-14 | Test first held: `test_external_assets_check.py` and `test_mkdocs_api_reference.py` were written and run red (module not found) before the check and the hook existed. | Honest record of the order of work. |

## M2-24 App manifest, installation and scopes

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-24-1 | 2026-10-13 | The module `apps` may use `authz` (new edge in 02 4.4, `architecture/modules.yaml` and the C4 component diagram): uninstall revokes the grants of the app through `authz.Admin.RevokeSubjectGrants`, and the subscription rule asks `authz.AppScopes`. `identity` declares `AppInstallations`, `apps` implements it in `adapters/identitysource`, `internal/wire` binds it. | 11 4.3 asks uninstall to revoke grants, and the scope table of 05 2.6 lives only in `authz` (05 rule 1.1); `authz` is a lower layer and `identity` never imports `apps`, so the graph stays acyclic. |
| D-M2-24-2 | 2026-10-13 | `AppManifest` in `openapi.yaml` follows 11 3.1 field for field (required `schema_version`, `slug`, `name`, `description`, `publisher`, `version`, `min_api_version`, `scopes.required` and `scopes.optional`; optional `homepage_url`, `events`, `delivery`, `commands`, `components`, `requested_grants`, `incoming_webhooks`, `data_egress`), with inline objects and `additionalProperties: false` everywhere; `requested_grants` uses the shared enums `TargetKind`, `Level` and `Capability` so `trigger`, `manage`, `export`, `approve_actions` and `host` reach the rule check. Every example that shows a manifest uses the new form. | The YAML lacked members that 11 3.1, 3.2 and 03 `apps.manifest` require; the change that finds the contradiction aligns the YAML with the chapter (SPEC 13 point 5), and the rules can only name a refused value they receive. |
| D-M2-24-3 | 2026-10-13 | Validation pipeline: the request validation checks the schema first (`unknown_member`, `invalid_enum`, ... as `400 validation_failed`, also for `validateManifest`); then the handler hands the manifest bytes as received to the pure `domain.ValidateManifest` with an `Env` of catalogue facts and configuration. `validateManifest` answers `200` with `valid` and every rule error relative to the manifest root, stores nothing and writes no audit entry; `installApp` and `upgradeAppManifest` answer `400 validation_failed` with the pointers under `/manifest`, or `400 webhook_host_not_allowed` when every error is a host error. Codes come from the M1-18 vocabulary (`required`, `invalid`, `too_large`, `too_long`, `too_many_items`), plus `webhook_host_not_allowed` and `command_name_reserved` where 06 and 09 name the reason; the detail names the rule, never the value. | SEC-IN-1 checks the shape before any rule; one rule function serves the dry run and the install, so both report the same errors. |
| D-M2-24-4 | 2026-10-13 | Subscribable events: a durable type of the catalogue without audience `self` whose one receive action is in the scope table (with a scope or none), or `interaction.created` and `command.invoked` (receivers `agent_or_app`, scope `events:subscribe`); every other type is refused (`access.changed`, `draft.*`, `agent.invocation`, `grant.changed`, `host.updated`, ...). The scope of the receive action must be requested, and `events:subscribe` whenever `events` is not empty. `delivery` is required with events, commands or components; `events_url` required for `webhook` and forbidden otherwise; `interactions_url` required for commands or components without the gateway; `data_egress` required with events or `interactions_url`. Manifest commands: at most `command.max_per_principal`, names unique without regard to case and not reserved, description at most `command.description_max_chars`, option names unique, no target (`scope_kind` absent or `instance`, no `scope_id`). | 11 3.1 and 3.2 rules 1, 2 and 5; 05 2.6 row "Ereignis-Abos"; 11 4.1: a manifest never names a target. |
| D-M2-24-5 | 2026-10-13 | Delivery addresses are absolute `https` URLs with a host, without user information and fragment, whose host (lower case, without port) equals an entry of `webhook.allowed_hosts` without regard to case and without wildcards; otherwise the manifest is refused with `webhook_host_not_allowed`. `homepage_url` must be absolute `https`, its host is not compared because the server never loads it. 11 3.2 rule 3 now says that the operator adds a missing host to the process configuration. | `webhook.allowed_hosts` is process configuration that changes only at start (configuration.md 1, 03 9); the consent cannot add a host "in derselben bestätigten Aktion" (spec gap, sentence changed in 11). |
| D-M2-24-6 | 2026-10-13 | `installApp`: authorize `app.install` on the instance before anything is loaded; handle = `slug`, display name = `name`; every required scope must be granted, any optional scope may be; absent `event_subscriptions` means none; one unit of work declared as a rights change (permission_version first) creates principal, `apps` and the `active` installation and writes the allow entry. An app whose installations are all uninstalled is reinstalled: same principal, status back to `active`, display name and manifest columns replaced, a new installation; its revoked tokens and grants stay revoked. A handle taken by another principal and a concurrent install of the same slug answer `409 conflict`. | 11 4.1 and 03 6.3; reusing the principal keeps the authorship of the app's messages (11 4.3) and the unique `lower(handle)`. |
| D-M2-24-7 | 2026-10-13 | `upgradeAppManifest` (`app.install`): same `slug`; changed content needs another `version` (a lower one is allowed, rollback); an identical manifest with identical lists is without effect (`200`, no rights version, audit `no_change`); granted scopes and subscriptions are subsets of the current ones (new ones need `setAppScopes`), so what the new manifest drops acts at once; the upgrade never adds a grant; it is itself the consent for new delivery hosts (owner, step-up), which must be in `webhook.allowed_hosts` and are named in `delivery_hosts_added`. | 11 4.2 (changed: "Neue Scopes und Ereignisse"); `manifest_version` names the content in the console and the audit; a host change is consented by the step-up action that uploads it. |
| D-M2-24-8 | 2026-10-13 | Installation, grants and tokens are separate steps: `app.install` creates no token and no grant; grants come per target with `grant.create` (M1-29 `Admin.CreateGrant`, HTTP with M1-48), tokens with `createToken`. `requested_grants` only inform the consent. 11 4.1 says so below the diagram. | `AppInstall` has no member for targets and `App` no secret; the sequence diagram of 11 4.1 shows the whole consent flow, not one transaction. |
| D-M2-24-9 | 2026-10-13 | Calls without effect are decided before the rights change: `upgradeAppManifest`, `setAppScopes`, `suspendApp`, `resumeApp` and `uninstallApp` first load in a unit without rights change and, when nothing changes, write the allow entry with `no_change: true` there; otherwise the rights change locks `apps` and the installation and checks again (a concurrent equal change ends as no change). `suspendApp` and `resumeApp` of an uninstalled app answer `409 invalid_state_transition`; `setAppScopes` and `upgradeAppManifest` of an uninstalled app `409 conflict`. | 02 8.1: phase 1 of a rights change always raises `permission_version`, and R-API-27 forbids a rights version for a no-op (the M1-29 pattern). |
| D-M2-24-10 | 2026-10-13 | App tokens and the scope check: `createToken` for an app locks the active installation FOR SHARE (none, suspended or uninstalled: `409 conflict` `app_not_installed`); scopes absent or empty take the granted scopes at this moment, present ones must be granted (`400` at `/scopes/<i>`). The authenticator reads the installation of an app token on every request; an app request needs the action's scope in `GrantedScopes` and in the token scopes (`actor.Scopes`); without a token (webhook delivery) the installation alone decides. A removed scope acts on the next request; a scope granted later needs a new token. | Decides the open points of 08 12; replaces the part of M1-28 D6 that token scopes are not evaluated. The share lock serializes with an uninstall that holds the installation FOR UPDATE, so no live token is created for an uninstalled app. |
| D-M2-24-11 | 2026-10-13 | The installation is part of the credential: the API token path (REST and the gateway `identify`) applies the installation check, `Revalidate` fails with `app_not_installed` when the installation is no longer active or no longer grants a scope the actor carries, and the rights snapshot of an app without an active installation is empty (`PrincipalFacts.AppInstallationActive`), so suspend and uninstall write `access.changed` for the app's targets and resume adds them back. Known gap of the core contract (04 8.2): a change of the granted scopes alone changes no visible target, writes no `access.changed`, and an open gateway connection learns it only at its next `identify`, `resume` or `Revalidate`; REST and webhook deliveries see it at once. `TestAppAccessChanged_SuspendResumeUninstall` asserts the gap so a later fix shows up. | Answers "wie lange Verbindungen den Zustand der Installation neu prüfen" (08 12); the gateway (M1-64, M1-65) is not on the base, so its test waits for those tickets. |
| D-M2-24-12 | 2026-10-13 | Ports and wiring: `apps/app` uses consumer interfaces of its own (`Principals` with `IdentityPrincipals` over `identity.Directory` and the new `identity.PrincipalWrites`, `Tokens`, `Grants`, `Rights`, `Scopes`, `Cursors`); `internal/wire` builds the `authz.AdminService` for the grant revocation over the pool and binds the PrincipalSource of identity with the installations of apps everywhere (Authorizer, rights hook, expiry, admin). The new start step `apps` (6e) runs after `identity`. | The domain imports only the kernel core (02 5); the app layer turns facts and configuration into `domain.Env`. |
| D-M2-24-13 | 2026-10-13 | `listApps` lists apps whose latest installation is `active` or `suspended`, ordered by `lower(slug)` then principal id, keyset cursor bound to the caller; `getApp` returns any app, uninstalled included, with its latest installation. `listApps` and `validateManifest` authorize `app.read` on the collection of apps (resource kind `app` without id): authz treats that collection as visible like the instance, so a non-owner gets `403 owner_only`; one app stays invisible to a non-owner (`404`, AZ-P4). | 06 4 point 2 (container check); the catalogue gives `app.read` only the kind `app`, so the collection is that kind without an id (`TestAppCollection_OwnerOnly`). |
| D-M2-24-14 | 2026-10-13 | Audit details (flat, ids, codes, integers, booleans and lists of codes; never names, descriptions, addresses, `data_egress` or the manifest): install `app_id`, `installation_id`, `slug`, `manifest_sha256` (lower-case hex), `granted_scopes`, `event_subscriptions`, `delivery_hosts`, `reinstall`, `reason`; upgrade adds `previous_manifest_sha256` and the added and removed manifest scopes, events and commands, `delivery_hosts_added`, `granted_scopes_removed`, `event_subscriptions_removed`; scope change the added and removed scopes and subscriptions; suspend and resume `app_id`, `installation_id`; uninstall also `tokens_revoked`, `grants_revoked`, `reason`; `no_change: true` for a call without effect. `manifest_sha256` is the SHA-256 of the manifest bytes as received, `apps.manifest` keeps the parsed content as jsonb. A delivery host that is no audit code (an address literal, longer than a code or outside its characters) stands in `delivery_hosts` and `delivery_hosts_added` as `sha256:` and the first 32 hex digits of its SHA-256. | SEC-AUD-5; the hash names exactly what the owner uploaded; an allowed host must never make the audit entry and with it the install fail, and an address never reaches the audit (20 4.5). |
| D-M2-24-15 | 2026-10-13 | ADR 0022 confirmation: a `depguard` rule `no-plugin-in-server` in `services/server/.golangci.yml` forbids the standard-library package `plugin` in every file of the server module; the red run on a throwaway file in a scratch worktree is quoted in the ticket report and the file was never committed. | The vendor allow list of `modules.yaml` already refuses a WebAssembly or other runtime in `make arch`; `plugin` is standard library and not covered there. |
| D-M2-24-16 | 2026-10-13 | `bothy_app` gets `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` on `apps` and `app_installations` and no `DELETE`; retention classes `account` and `config` (03). The trigger `apps_principal_kind_check` (SQLSTATE `23514`) refuses an `apps` row whose principal is not of kind `app`. | Rows are never deleted (03 1 point 7, M1-12 D4); same pattern as `identity_users_kind_check`. |
| D-M2-24-17 | 2026-10-13 | `platform/httpserver` keeps the JSON body that `StageBody` checked in the request context (`httpserver.RawJSONBody`); the app handlers hash and store the manifest member exactly as received from it. | The generated strict server hands a handler only the decoded value, and a re-encoding would change the bytes the hash must cover (11 3.1). |
| D-M2-24-18 | 2026-10-13 | The base lacks `routes.SpecVersion`; this branch carries the M1-46 generator commits that add it (`feat(api): spec version, gateway protocol versions and problem catalogue from the contract`, `style(api): gofmt of the generator meta test`) unchanged, so `min_api_version` is compared with the real spec version and the later merge with M1-46 brings identical content. | The brief asks to extend the generator "exactly as M1-46 Scope item 10 describes"; taking M1-46's own commits is the exact form and avoids a second, diverging implementation. |
| D-M2-24-19 | 2026-10-13 | The ticket is built on `feat/M2-24-apps-install` from `int/m1-wave12` (`da08f58`) in its own worktree and is not merged locally; the PR lane ports it. Rulings stand in this section in the table format of the base. M1 tickets outside the closure on the base: M1-31 (yes); M1-19 (yes); M1-46, M1-48, M1-51, M1-53 to M1-57, M1-64, M1-65 (no). The AZ cases and the `access.changed` assertions run in tier 1 (real installation, token, authenticator, Authorizer and rights hook, grants from `authz.Admin.CreateGrant`, structure from the fake source); tier 2 and the gateway test wait for M1-56, M1-57, M1-64 and M1-65. | The launcher's task takes precedence over the brief's merge step; the brief allows only merged tickets (design note 17). |
| D-M2-24-20 | 2026-10-13 | Names that differ from the brief: the token scopes of the actor are the existing field `actor.Actor.Scopes` (no new `TokenScopes`), read through the new `authorize.Principal.AppTokenScopes`; the principal port methods are `identity.PrincipalWrites` (`CreatePrincipal`, `SetPrincipalStatus`, `UpdateDisplayName`); `audit.reason_max_chars` is registered in `configkeys_audit.go` (its area), the other keys in `configkeys_apps.go`; the scope table port also lists the scopes (`AppScopes.Scopes`). | The brief says to add `TokenScopes` only if missing and to follow the merged names; M1-51 may reuse `PrincipalWrites` for agents. |
| D-M2-24-21 | 2026-10-13 | A manifest whose JSON holds the escape of NUL (`\u0000`) or of a surrogate that is not part of a pair is invalid at the root pointer (`invalid`); values taken from a manifest (delivery hosts, command names, the slug) enter the audit through one helper that writes a value that is no audit code as `sha256:` and 32 hex digits. | PostgreSQL refuses both escapes in `jsonb`, and the audit refuses codes longer than 64 bytes or address literals; without the rule and the helper a manifest that passes every check made install or upgrade answer 500 (red team M2-24, SEC-IN-4, SEC-T06). One rule and one helper fix the class instead of each value. |
| D-M2-24-22 | 2026-10-13 | A keyed call (`Idempotency-Key`) of `suspendApp`, `resumeApp`, `uninstallApp`, `setAppScopes` or `upgradeAppManifest` that loses a race decides the call without effect inside its rights change: the plan runs again under the locks and writes the allow entry with `no_change`, and the rights version rises once. | The idempotency stage runs the handler in one unit, so the rights change is a late one whose error fails the whole unit even when swallowed; the losing caller got 500. Design note 9 g accepts the one extra version step for this rare case; unkeyed calls still roll back and plan again without it. |
| D-M2-24-23 | 2026-10-13 | The integration world of the module apps lives in `services/server/test/appsworld`; its tests are split over `internal/apps/app`, `internal/apps/adapters/httpapi` and `internal/apps/adapters/postgres`. | `make coverage` measures each package only by its own tests, so the use cases and the repository counted as uncovered while the HTTP tests ran through them, and the server total fell below the threshold. Code under `test/` is test code (02 Code-Layout) and outside the measurement. |

## M1-56 Sending messages

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-56/0 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave17` at `e2f9eb4`, branch `feat/M1-56-messaging-send` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally, the integration lane ports it. The migrations take the next free numbers of the base, `00029` and `00030`. Text this ticket adds is English. The methods of `messaging.Messages` take the caller as `a actor.Actor` like the chat ports, and `MessagePosted` uses `ids.ID` where the brief writes `uuid.UUID`. The start check D0 ran but gave no verdict: the shared Go build cache of the host lost files during the run, and the repeat raced with the first edits of the worktree; the final `make check` of the ticket covers the base. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and language; `kernel/ids` is the ID type of every port. |
| M1-56/1 | 2026-10-14 | The foreign key `threads.parent_message_id -> messages(id)` (`DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED`) is added in `00029` `NOT VALID` and validated by `00030`, as `00026` and `00027` did for `grants.target_channel_id`. Test code of chat that created threads from invented parent ids now inserts a parent message first, and the schema test of `00026` lists the new key. | upgrade.md rule M5: a new constraint on an existing table starts `NOT VALID`; before M1-56 no thread could name a parent, so the validation finds nothing. |
| M1-56/2 | 2026-10-14 | The mention scanner is code of `messaging/domain`, no Markdown library: `CodeMask` follows the block structure of CommonMark 0.31.2 (block quotes, list items, lazy continuation, headings, thematic breaks, fenced and indented code) and the inline rules of backslash escapes, autolinks and code spans, and its tests hold every code-span and code-block example of the specification (107 to 147, 328 to 349). Raw HTML is not part of content format 1, so an HTML tag is text and its backticks can open a code span; example 344 is the one case that differs from CommonMark. | Brief D5 allows a scanner that passes the examples; no new dependency, license or vendor group. SEC-OUT-1 removes raw HTML from the dialect. |
| M1-56/3 | 2026-10-14 | The port `internal/messaging` depends on no other module (02 section 7.2, `make arch`): `ThreadCreated` names the thread by id, and the handler of `createThread` reads the thread through `chat.Threads.Get` after the unit of work, so the answer shows `last_message_seq`, `message_count` and `version` after the first message. `messaging.Message` has no attachments, reactions, components or pins yet; the REST form and the events send them empty and `pinned: false`. | go-arch-lint allows a module port only kernel ports; the adapters may use the chat port. |
| M1-56/4 | 2026-10-14 | Mentions (07 section 4.6): a person is mentioned when the author may read the person (`user.read`) or the person may read the thread; the second check runs for every author, as 07 states it, and is decisive only for authors without `user.read`. The person is checked as a principal without a session, and authz gives the owner role only in a session, so an active owner (from the directory) counts as a reader of every thread. The checks run in filter mode; no audit entry for a token that stays text. | 07 section 2.1: owners read everything; without the owner rule an agent could not mention an owner who holds no grant. |
| M1-56/5 | 2026-10-14 | Body rules beyond the brief: `content_md` with U+0000 is `400` on `/content_md` with code `invalid`; an empty `attachment_file_ids` and an empty `components` are accepted, only non-empty ones are `not_enabled`; an explicit `kind: "text"` and an absent kind give the same request hash. | PostgreSQL text and jsonb cannot hold U+0000, which would otherwise be a `500`; brief D2 refuses only non-empty lists. |
| M1-56/6 | 2026-10-14 | A lost race on `(author, client_msg_id)` answers from a second unit of work like a repetition. When the request already runs inside an outer unit (only `createThread` with both `Idempotency-Key` and `first_message`), the transaction is lost with the unique violation, so the answer is `409 conflict` and the client repeats. | 04 section 5.1 point 4; a nested unit cannot start a new transaction. |
| M1-56/7 | 2026-10-14 | `message.created` is encoded without HTML escaping, so the payload size follows the content. A payload of at least `sync.event_payload_max_bytes` gets the longest valid UTF-8 prefix of `content_md` that fits (binary search over character boundaries), `content_truncated: true` and the envelope flag `truncated`; the system line of a reopen is a `message.created` without the `client_msg_id` echo. | 04 payload rule 2 (the appender refuses, the producer cuts); brief D7. |
| M1-56/8 | 2026-10-14 | The HTTP world of the tests is `services/server/test/messagingworld` (like `test/terminalworld`), so the tests of `app`, `adapters/httpapi` and `adapters/postgres` each measure their own package. The test subscriber of `MessagePosted` is a method of a type in each test package, because the txn registry derives the module of a handler from its package. | Coverage is per package (`make coverage`, SonarQube); the registry refuses handlers outside `internal/<module>`. |
| M1-56/9 | 2026-10-14 | Test first held for the identity lookup and the owner mention rule (red runs in the report); the domain tests, the migration test and the HTTP tests over PostgreSQL were written before or together with their code and run red on test defects first, not on missing code. | Honest record of the order of work. |
| M1-56/10 | 2026-10-14 | Code review: the scanner kept the container stack in a form where a blank line walked every open list item, a line of bullets rechecked the thematic break over the rest of the line per marker and a block quote searched the innermost item backwards, so a message of `message.max_content_bytes` with thousands of nested containers took seconds of CPU. The parser now keeps the first container a blank line stops at, the last non-break byte of each line and the indexes of the open items, so every step is constant time; a test bounds six such inputs. The message after the thread lock takes channel and space from the locked thread. | A send request must not occupy a core; no nesting limit is needed, so CommonMark stays unchanged. |
| M1-56/11 | 2026-10-14 | Red team: the `createThread` answer reads the thread through the new `chat.Threads.View`, which does not authorize, instead of `Get` (`thread.read`); a replay of the first message authorizes `message.create` on its thread before it answers. The scanner treats a line that is blank after its block quote markers like a blank line for the containers inside the quote (CommonMark 0.31.2 example 262), keeps the stops of a blank line in a sorted list and scans the indent of a container only as far as it needs. | 05 section 2.6 asks only `messages:write` for `thread.create`; a 403 after the commit hid a stored post. The code mask must match CommonMark and stay linear (M1-56/10). |

The messaging world (`test/messagingworld`) binds an installation per app to its principal source and gives the app token the granted scopes, as M2-24 requires for app rights. `TestAuthenticateAPIToken_AppInstallation` sets `DevicePendingTTL`. | The tickets' tests were built on older bases; each world now meets the rules of the other ticket and the base without changing what the tests assert. |
| INT-W19/9 | 2026-10-15 | Semantic conflict found by the red team: since M1-48 every grant write appends `grant.changed` in its unit of work, and M2-24's `RevokeSubjectGrants` (built before M1-48) revoked without it. It now appends one `grant.changed` with `change: revoked` per revoked grant (action `app.uninstall`), as the group delete cascade does; `TestRevokeSubjectGrants` checks the events (red before). This corrects the "behaviour unchanged" of INT-W19/5 and INT-W19/7, which compared with the ticket and not with the base rule. | Managers who follow grants through the event log must see the grants of an uninstalled app end (05, M1-48). |

## M7-01 Image bothy-postgres with pgBackRest

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M7-01-0 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave17` at `e2f9eb4`, branch `feat/M7-01-postgres-image` in its own worktree; this session neither merges locally nor pushes, the PR lane ports the branch. The base names the chapters in German (`16-betrieb.md`, `18-release.md`, `02-architektur.md`, `0058-sicherung-mit-pgbackrest-...`) and has no section 10 in this file, so the rows stand in a section of the ticket and text added to German chapters is English. D-W-100 and D-W-125 of `origin/main` are rulings about writing briefs, not contract the build relies on, so they are not ported. | The launcher's instructions take precedence over the brief's landing step; merged names win (D-W-100 as the brief states it). |
| D-M7-01-1 | 2026-10-14 | The image lives in `services/server/images/bothy-postgres/` (Dockerfile, `Dockerfile.dockerignore`, `entrypoint.sh`, `check-pgbackrest-version.sh`, README), not in `images/bothy-postgres` of the plan. | The image is an artifact of the component `server` (18 2); there the component path filter of `tools/ci/components.py` sees it. |
| D-M7-01-2 | 2026-10-14 | pgBackRest comes as the package `pgbackrest=2.59.3-1.pgdg13+1` of `apt.postgresql.org` (suite `trixie-pgdg`, the Debian release of the pinned `postgres:18.6-trixie` digest), checked through the repository key of the base image, whose fingerprint is pinned in the Dockerfile. The package stage runs on the build platform, downloads `pgbackrest` and `libssh2-1t64` for the target architecture and unpacks them; the final stage only copies, plus package records under `/var/lib/dpkg/status.d/` for image scanners. `libssh2-1t64` takes the version of the signed Debian archive of that release. The version guard checks the pinned package version in the build and the installed binary on every start. | 18 "Server-Images" rule 2 forbids emulation; with no step on the target platform one host builds both platforms natively, so `make postgres-image` writes both into one archive. A package exists for both architectures, so no source build is needed (brief design note 1). An exact pin of the Debian library would break the build at every Debian point release, while its integrity is already guaranteed by the signed archive. |
| D-M7-01-3 | 2026-10-14 | `bothy-backupd` is a second `main` package `services/server/cmd/bothy-backupd` with the server version from `internal/platform/buildinfo`; in M7-01 it supervises `pgbackrest server`, creates the stanza `bothy` (retried every `--retry` until it succeeds), stops the child on `SIGTERM` or `SIGINT` (the base image's stop signal), and offers `healthcheck` and `version`. | Brief design note 2: the server binary with its API role has no business in the database image; one commit versions both images. |
| D-M7-01-4 | 2026-10-14 | `pgbackrest_tls/` holds `ca.crt` and per host `postgres` and `backup` a certificate and an ECDSA P-256 PKCS#8 key. The CA is self-signed with path length zero; its key is never written, so a rotation regenerates the directory. Host certificates carry the host name as common name and DNS name and both server and client use, and all three are valid for `stack.PGBackRestTLSValidity`. The whole directory goes to `postgres` and `backup`, as the catalogue row names it. | Both hosts are TLS servers and clients (D-M7-01-5); `tls-server-auth` matches the common name; a stored CA key would let any service holding a copy mint certificates. |
| D-M7-01-5 | 2026-10-14 | Besides PostgreSQL, the container `postgres` runs `pgbackrest server`; the repository host reaches it as `pg1-host` over TLS (`pg1-host-type=tls`) for `stanza-create`, `backup` and `check`. | A repository host can only back up a database whose host it reaches through pgBackRest; without it the full backup "from inside backup" of design note 3 is impossible. It is an inbound connection on the internal network, so R-OP-08 holds. |
| D-M7-01-6 | 2026-10-14 | Both roles run as the user `postgres` of the base image (`USER 999:999`); the entry point never needs root. `secrets-init` gets `--owner postgres=999:999 --owner backup=999:999` (16 5.3 item 4 updated), and the repository and configuration directories belong to that user. The new variable `BOTHY_PGBACKREST_PG_HOST` names the database host for `backup`; `BOTHY_PG_ARCHIVE_TIMEOUT` and both host variables are required. The cipher pass goes only into the configuration file of `backup` (mode `0600`), because pgBackRest reads it from no file of its own. | pgBackRest refuses keys that another user can read (`0444` copies of root failed in the first run); a root image fails the Trivy rule of a non-root user. Host names and timeouts come from named variables (rule 9). |
| D-M7-01-7 | 2026-10-14 | The CI job `postgres-image` is public-only (the guard of `tools/ci/check_runners.py`) with a matrix of `ubuntu-24.04` and `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, a docker-container buildx builder and no QEMU; it runs when the plan lists `server` or `all`, and `ci-ok` accepts success or skipped. `check_runners.py` accepts `runs-on: ${{ matrix.<key> }}` when every value of that key is a GitHub-hosted label, under the same rules as a written-out label. | B12 and 18 rule 2 need a native arm64 run; the arm64 runner is GitHub-hosted and free for public repositories (D-W-125 rule of no cost); the switch expression of ADR 0039 cannot name a runner per matrix entry. |
| D-M7-01-8 | 2026-10-14 | The two-container test fills the secret volumes of `postgres` and `backup` with a helper container that copies the output of `stack.Init` and sets owner `999:999`, directories `0500` and files `0400`, exactly as `secrets-init` with `--owner` leaves them. The point-in-time target lies at least a second after the end of the full backup. | A test running without root cannot chown files to `999`; pgBackRest selects the backup set by its stop time in whole seconds. |

## M8-01 Discord read client

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M8-01-0 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave17` at `e2f9eb4`, branch `feat/M8-01-discord-read-client` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally, the PR lane ports it. The base has no section 10 in this file, so the rows stand in a section per ticket like the earlier tickets. The brief relies on no core rule and no contract change; D-W-51, D-W-100, D-W-120 and D-W-124 are planning rulings on `main` and are not copied to the base. | The task names the base and the lane; precedent D-M3-17-8 and M1-23/3. |
| D-M8-01-1 | 2026-10-14 | The tool code lives under `services/server/cmd/bothy/importtool/` (D-W-124 (1)); the client is `cmd/bothy/importtool/discord`. `TestDiscordClientImportsNoServerModule` holds that `go list -deps` of the package contains no package under `internal/` except `internal/platform/egress` and `internal/kernel/...`. | `bothy import` is a subcommand of the one binary (component `cmd`), which may use every package; the test keeps the tool on the public API (22 2, R-IM-01). |
| D-M8-01-2 | 2026-10-14 | Rate-limit guard: the route key is the path template plus the guild or channel ID; the bucket is the `X-RateLimit-Bucket` Discord last named for the route, else the path template, again plus that ID. A missing or unparsable wait (`retry_after`, `Retry-After`, and also `X-RateLimit-Reset-After` with `Remaining: 0`) counts as `import.discord.max_rate_wait`; any wait above it ends the call before sleeping with `ErrRateWaitTooLong`, also the wait for an exhausted bucket. After a waited-out `429` the bucket's refill time is cleared, so the retry does not wait twice. The retries after the last allowed `429` end with the new sentinel `ErrRateLimited` (status `429`). `5xx` (`ErrServer`) and transport errors are never retried in the client. | 22 4.3 items 1 to 4: fail towards waiting, never towards hammering (RK-76); the mirror's backoff is M8-05, an import run ends with exit `1` (22 10.3). |
| D-M8-01-3 | 2026-10-14 | `TestClientHasOnlyGet` type-checks the non-test files of the package from source against export data (the approach of `internal/wire/counters_static_test.go`): exactly one call of `http.NewRequest` or `http.NewRequestWithContext`, whose method argument is the object `net/http.MethodGet`; no `http.Request` literal and no use of the field `Method` of a request; no exported name, method or field that matches `post`, `put`, `patch` or `delete` (case-insensitive); no exported function with a parameter named like a method or verb. `TestFakeSeesOnlyGet` drives every exported method against the fake, which answers every other method `405`; `TestNoTestPackageInBinary` lists the dependencies of `cmd/bothy`. | 22 16 "Discord client without a write method", ADR 0043 ruling 1; the literal and field checks close the other ways to send another verb with the one construction. |
| D-M8-01-4 | 2026-10-14 | `MessagesAfter`, `MessagesBefore` and `MessagesAround` return messages in ascending snowflake order, sorted by the numeric value, whatever order Discord used. | Discord returns newest first; the import appends strictly in Discord order (22 6.2 `thread_seq`), so no caller may see another order. |
| D-M8-01-5 | 2026-10-14 | Additions to the names of the brief (D-W-100): `Config.Egress` carries the `egress.*` values of the tool (allowed hosts, timeout, response size limit), because only an operator rule with `allow_private` may open the loopback fake and a consumer policy never can; the host policy of the client names exactly the host and port of `import.discord.api_base` over `https`. The `User-Agent` URL is the constant `https://github.com/OWNER/bothy` (the placeholder of `repo_url` in `mkdocs.yml`), the version comes from `Config.UserAgentVersion`. Further sentinels beside the brief's: `ErrRateLimited`, `ErrUnexpectedStatus` (any other status, a redirect included), `ErrDecode` (a body that is not the expected JSON, without its content), `ErrInvalidID`, `ErrTokenHostMismatch`; `CallError` has a `Hint` (the missing `MANAGE_THREADS`). `Attachment.URL` and `Embed.URL` are of type `SignedURL`, whose print forms show scheme, host and path only and whose `Raw` returns the full link (M8-02). `Deps.Sleep` is a `Sleeper` function. The fixture holds archive pages explicitly; a request beyond the last page gets the last page again. Fixture and test IDs are invented 8-digit strings, because the white-label rule of `tools/denylist/generic.rules` flags 17 to 20 digits as real Discord snowflakes; the client accepts 1 to 20 digits that fit an unsigned 64-bit number. | The brief allows following the code where names differ; each addition keeps a rule of the brief (no proxy, token only to the API base, no secret in output) provable. |
| D-M8-01-6 | 2026-10-14 | `TestTokenOnlyToAPIBase` checks the unexported header setter directly with another host, plain `http` and another port (refused, no header), and a redirect of the fake to a second host name, which the client does not follow (`ErrUnexpectedStatus`, one request, all on the API host). | Every method builds its URL from the API base, so only the setter itself and a redirect can aim a request elsewhere. |

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-01-0 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave17` at `e2f9eb4`, branch `feat/M4-01-workspace-arch` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally, the integration lane ports it (the brief's landing through a pull request is the lane's step).
