Names that differ on this base and are followed: the chapters are `docs/spec/15-client-architektur.md` and `23-qualitaet.md`; `ci-ok` combines the job results with `needs` and a shell script (there is no `tools/ci/ci_ok.py` here), so the two app jobs join its `needs` and its checks instead of `RULES`. | Brief rule D-W-100 and D-W-114: follow the merged code where it names things differently; the task names the base and forbids the local merge. |
| D-M4-01-1 | 2026-10-14 | The Flutter pin lives in `environment.flutter` of the root `pubspec.yaml` (`3.47.7`, Dart `^3.13.5`), CI reads it there (`flutter-version-file: pubspec.yaml` in `gates`, `sonar`, `apps-macos`, `apps-windows` and the Pages build), and every member repeats the same exact `environment` lines; `test_flutter_pins_agree` holds the fifteen manifests equal. Replaces D-M2-38-12. | One known Flutter for every job and member; the workspace root is the one manifest every member shares. |
| D-M4-01-2 | 2026-10-14 | `packages/bothy_noise` joins the workspace (`resolution: workspace`), its `pubspec.lock` is deleted and its code and tests stay unchanged; its OSV-Scanner overrides move with the lock file to the root `osv-scanner.toml`, with a new checked entry for every package or version the one resolution adds. The dev dependency `test` is `^1.31.1` in every pure Dart member, because `flutter_test` of Flutter 3.47.7 pins `test_api` 0.7.12 and `test` 1.33 needs 0.7.15; one resolution cannot hold both. Replaces D-M2-38-13; D-M2-38-20 applies to the root file. | Brief note 1; OSV-Scanner reads the override file next to the lock file it scans. |
| D-M4-01-3 | 2026-10-14 | The reach of the diagram of 15 3.1 is an allow list: `tools/arch-check/rules.yaml` holds the arrows, and a member may depend on and import exactly the members they reach (arrows and transitive closure), checked in `make arch`. A new arrow changes 15 3.1 and `rules.yaml` in the same change; `test_rules_edges_equal_the_spec_diagram` holds both equal. 15 3.1 gains rule 8 (the reach) and rule 9 (every manifest a listed member), and the check details of rules 1, 4 and 7. Findings on a manifest name its line 1. | A deny list would let every new edge in silently (brief note 2). |
| D-M4-01-4 | 2026-10-14 | Generated Dart code (`*.g.dart`, `*.freezed.dart`, `*.drift.dart`, `lib/src/generated/`, `build/`) is out of the doc lint and the style lint (analyzer `exclude` in the root `analysis_options.yaml`), the format check (`DART_GENERATED` in the root `Makefile`) and coverage (`tools/ci/lcov_filter.py`). It stays in the compile check: the members that import it are analyzed, and `flutter test` compiles it. An excluded file is not analyzed itself, so an analyzer error inside it shows only through its users and the compiler. Each member includes its base set and then the root file through a list under `include` (Dart 3.6 and later). | Generators format and document their own output (M4-02, M4-12); the same split as M2-23 note 8 for the other languages. |
| D-M4-01-5 | 2026-10-14 | `make lint` runs `flutter pub get --enforce-lockfile` and `flutter analyze --fatal-infos` once at the workspace root, which analyzes every member, and `dart run custom_lint` in each member that declares it; `make test` resolves once at the root and runs `flutter test --coverage` per member, after checking that every member has `test/`. | One resolution and one analysis for the workspace (brief note 4); a stale lock file still fails instead of being rewritten (D-M2-38-21). |
| D-M4-01-6 | 2026-10-14 | The Dart API pages are generated for every build and never tracked: `tools/docs/dart_api_docs.py` runs `dart doc` per package into the ignored folder `docs/api/dart/`, and a warning or an error fails it. `make docs` runs it whenever a Dart member is tracked, for every component selection, not only with `apps`, because `docs/reference/dart-api.md` links the pages and `mkdocs build --strict` fails on a missing target; `make linkcheck` builds them first when the folder is missing, for the same reason (lychee checks the links offline). No validation setting changes. | Generated pages cannot drift from the code (brief note 5); the strict site build and the link check stay as they are. |
| D-M4-01-7 | 2026-10-14 | Platform tests are outside `gates`: `make apps-platform` (`tools/ci/apps_platform.py`) runs the argument lists of each member's `platform-tests.json` for the host system, and the CI jobs `apps-macos` (`macos-15`) and `apps-windows` (`windows-2025`) run it when the `apps` component is selected. On this base `tools/ci/check_runners.py` allows a GitHub-hosted runner outside the private/public switch only for a public-only job, and the switch has no Windows label, so both jobs run in the public phase only; `ci-ok` waits for both and accepts `success` or `skipped`. | They need a desktop session (brief note 6, ADR 0039); without a runner in the private phase a skipped job must not block. |
| D-M4-01-8 | 2026-10-14 | The root files `pubspec.yaml`, `pubspec.lock` and `analysis_options.yaml` and the prefix `tools/arch-check/` select the gate component `apps` (`tools/ci/components.py`). | A change there must run the Dart gates (brief note 7); before, the root files selected `all`. |
| D-M4-01-9 | 2026-10-14 | The packages of the Flutter SDK in the root lock file (`flutter`, `flutter_test`, `sky_engine`; source `sdk`, version 0.0.0) get license overrides in `osv-scanner.toml`: BSD-3-Clause, the license of the Flutter repository. The LICENSE file of `sky_engine` also collects the notices of the third-party code compiled into the engine (Apache-2.0, MIT, BSD, Zlib, Unicode, IJG and FreeType texts among them); its review is the ScanCode step before a release (docs/quality/licenses.md item 4). | deps.dev does not know SDK packages, so the license gate reports them as UNKNOWN; every Flutter app ships the engine (ADR 0027). |
| D-M4-01-10 | 2026-10-14 | Skeletons: each package library is a `library;` directive with its doc comment and no other public symbol, so each first test imports it with `// ignore: unused_import` (the import is what it checks). `device_key` is created from `flutter create --template=plugin --platforms=macos,windows` reduced to a Flutter package without native files, without the example app and without a `flutter: plugin:` section, because no platform class exists until M4-10. The apps show an empty `MaterialApp` without text and have no platform folders yet. | Brief note 8 and rule 4: no native code before its ticket. |
| D-M4-01-11 | 2026-10-14 | `packages/api_client` gets a `Makefile` whose `generate-check` passes while no generated code is tracked and fails as soon as `lib/src/generated/` or a `*.g.dart` file is tracked; M4-02 replaces the target with the generation, the compile and the comparison. `make api-clients` stays fail-closed for a manifest without the target. `tools/ci/apps_platform.py` resolves the program of each command with `shutil.which` before it runs it. | Code review M4-01: the tracked skeleton manifest made `make api-clients` (and so `make gates` and CI) fail, and without a shell Windows does not find `flutter.bat` for the job `apps-windows`. |

## M7-22 Audit log checkpoints in object storage

The brief names these rows D-M7-22-1 to D-M7-22-4; they follow the numbering of this log (M7-22/2 to M7-22/5), and M7-22/1 and M7-22/6 are rulings of the build.

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M7-22/1 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave17` at `e2f9eb4`, branch `feat/M7-22-audit-checkpoints-s3` in its own worktree; no local merge, no push and no pull request from this build, the PR lane lands it. The brief names the English chapter files of `main` (`19-security.md`, `12-files.md`, `03-data-model.md`, `20-privacy.md`); on this base they are `19-sicherheit.md`, `12-dateien.md`, `03-datenmodell.md` and `20-datenschutz.md`, and text this ticket adds there is English. Section 8 of `docs/operations/backup-restore.md`, which the brief builds on and which this base lacked, comes from `main` as its own commit. The process rulings D-W-100 and D-W-125 of `main` are not ported: they change no code. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; the brief's names follow `main` (D-W-100: the merged names win). |
| M7-22/2 | 2026-10-14 | Test service (D-M7-22-1): the SeaweedFS image the files driver pins (`chrislusf/seaweedfs:4.48` by digest, M3-02/13). Its S3 gateway supports bucket Object Lock with a default retention in mode `COMPLIANCE` (`CreateBucket` with `ObjectLockEnabledForBucket`, `PutObjectLockConfiguration`, `GetObjectLockConfiguration`; in a manual probe against the pinned image a locked version answered `DeleteObject` with its version ID with `AccessDenied`) and conditional writes (`PutObject` with `If-None-Match: *` on an existing key answers `412 PreconditionFailed`), shown against the pinned image by `TestLockCheck`, `TestConformance`, `TestObjectKeys` (retention mode of a stored object) and `TestOverwriteKeepsFirstVersion`; the SeaweedFS wiki page "Amazon S3 API" (https://github.com/seaweedfs/seaweedfs/wiki/Amazon-S3-API, retrieved 2026-10-14) lists `GetObjectLockConfiguration` and `PutObjectLockConfiguration` as supported and links "S3 Object Lock and Retention" and "S3 Conditional Operations" (`If-None-Match`). No second test service is pinned. The tests read the image from `internal/files/adapters/s3/seaweed_test.go`, so Renovate moves one pin. The tests of `adapters/httpapi` create locked buckets with `weed shell` (`s3.bucket.create -withLock`) inside the container and need no S3 client. | Design note 1: one free, pinned service with both features keeps one pin and the version the stack runs; the negative case "no lock" runs on the same service with a bucket without Object Lock. A real cloud bucket is out of scope (D-W-125, no cost). |
| M7-22/3 | 2026-10-14 | Write once and key form (D-M7-22-2, D-M7-22-3): the object is `[<prefix>/]<instance_id>/<seq with 20 digits, leading zeros>.jws`, the layout of the file sink. `Put` reads the oldest version of the key first (the same bytes count as stored, other bytes are `ErrCheckpointConflict`), then writes with `If-None-Match: *` and an SHA-256 checksum; on `412` or `409` of a concurrent writer the stored bytes decide the same way. `List` pages through `ListObjectVersions` below `[<prefix>/]<instance_id>/` (page size 1000, the tests 10), takes the oldest version of every checkpoint key, skips delete markers and other names, and returns the checkpoints in `seq` order. | The brief suggested `checkpoints/<seq>.jws`; the instance ID keeps two instances in one bucket apart, as the file sink does, and the same width keeps the key order the `seq` order. A service that ignores the header only adds a version on a locked bucket, and the oldest version is the one signed first; a bucket with a default retention requires an integrity header on uploads. |
| M7-22/4 | 2026-10-14 | New keys (D-M7-22-4): `audit.checkpoint_s3_endpoint` (empty, required with `s3://`), `audit.checkpoint_s3_region` (`us-east-1`), `audit.checkpoint_s3_use_path_style` (`true`), `audit.checkpoint_s3_access_key_ref` and `audit.checkpoint_s3_secret_key_ref` (`file:<secrets.dir>/audit_checkpoint_s3_access_key` and `..._secret_key`), all chapter 19; no time or size key of its own: the egress client of purpose `audit_checkpoint` keeps `egress.timeout` and `egress.max_response_bytes`. `retention.audit` (`365d`, at least `1d`, chapter 20) enters the schema with this first reader. The secret catalogue rows are supplied and go to the service `server` only. The cross-check `audit_checkpoint_sink` validates the endpoint, region, path style and the `allow_private` entry of an `http` endpoint at load (the rules of `file.s3.*`, copied into `s3sink.Config` across the module boundary). The SDK is allowed in `internal/audit/adapters/s3sink` (module `audit` gets `adapter_vendors: [awssdk]`, depguard and `TestS3SDKOnlyInAdapter` name the package); its interface holds only `PutObject`, `GetObject`, `ListObjectVersions` and `GetObjectLockConfiguration` (`TestAPIMethodSet`). | Own endpoint and credentials separate from file storage and backup (runbook 8 item 1). The brief also named `migrate` for the secret files "as the job runs there"; the job `audit.chain_verify` runs only in the server (roles `worker` and `all`), and `bothy migrate` never reads the keys, so the narrower list follows least privilege. |
| M7-22/5 | 2026-10-14 | Lock check: the target passes only with Object Lock enabled and a default retention in mode `COMPLIANCE` whose period (days times 24 hours plus years times 365 days) is at least `retention.audit`; `GOVERNANCE`, a lock without default retention, a bucket without Object Lock and a shorter period fail with `s3sink.ErrNotImmutable`, naming `audit.checkpoint_sink` (and `retention.audit` for the period), never the endpoint. `bothy config check` runs it for an `s3://` sink with or without `--secrets` (it needs the keys and one request; `--secrets` reports the two references as skipped unless the sink is `s3://`), and the start step `audit_log` builds the sink through the same `wire.CheckpointSink` and stops the start. | Brief item 15 and runbook 8: Governance can be lifted with `s3:BypassGovernanceRetention`; a year counted as 365 days never overstates a lock. |
| M7-22/6 | 2026-10-14 | The selection of the sink by scheme stays in `internal/wire` (`wire.CheckpointSink`, which `Audit.BindLog` now receives), not in `internal/audit/app` as the brief's scope lists it; the conformance suite is `internal/audit/app/sinktest` (only tests import it, `TestFilestestNotInBinary` also holds it out of the binary). | `app` may not import adapters (go-arch-lint, ADR 0008); the M1-50 code already selected the sink in `wire` (D-W-100: follow the merged code). |
| M7-22/7 | 2026-10-15 | `s3sink.Sink` keeps the body of every version it read in memory, by key and version ID (a version never changes; objects without a version ID are never cached), and reads the bodies a `List` has not seen with at most 16 concurrent `GetObject`s. A process reads each checkpoint once; every later run of `audit.chain_verify` costs one `ListObjectVersions` pass and one `GetObject` per new checkpoint (`TestListScalesWithManyCheckpoints`). | Red team NEIN: the job lists the sink on every run, and one sequential `GetObject` per checkpoint against a remote store at 40 ms per request passed the one-minute job timeout of River after about 1400 checkpoints, about two months at `audit.checkpoint_interval = 1h`; from then on no checkpoint was signed. The cache holds a few hundred bytes per checkpoint. |

## M4-02 Dart API client

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-02-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave20` at `ef0e37c`, branch `feat/M4-02-dart-api-client` in its own worktree; no local merge, no push and no pull request from this build, the PR lane lands it. The brief names the English chapter files of `main` (`04-sync-protocol.md`, `15-client-architecture.md`, `23-quality.md`); on this base they are `04-sync-protokoll.md`, `15-client-architektur.md` and `23-qualitaet.md`, and text this ticket adds there is English. No contract change of `main` is needed: the `openapi.yaml` of this base has every schema the ticket uses (`Event` with its discriminator, `EventPage`, `Problem`, `TriggerState`, `MessageCreate`, `MessageUpdate`, `InteractionCreate`, `UserSettingPut`). D-W-115 (the apps carry `client.outbox_retry_base` and `client.outbox_retry_cap` in `AppConfig`) is not ported: it changes no code here, the hand layer takes both values from its caller. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; D-W-100 and D-W-114: the merged names win. |
| D-M4-02-1 | 2026-10-15 | The generator runs from `openapitools/openapi-generator-cli:v7.26.0@sha256:a304ddf1e2e5f24f68fa3153568d6174cea4959d09aa8e3db6d526fd0782326d` (the digest of the multi-architecture index, `docker manifest inspect` on 2026-10-15; `docker buildx` is not installed on the build host), named in `packages/api_client/Makefile` (`GENERATOR_IMAGE`) and `docs/quality/toolchain.md`, run with `--rm --network none` (`GENERATOR_DOCKER_FLAGS`), `--user` of the caller, the repository mounted read-only and only a fresh `mktemp -d` folder writable. Temporary folders come from `$TMPDIR` (default `/tmp`), which Docker must be able to mount. | The generator needs a Java runtime the repository does not pin otherwise; the digest makes the run reproducible (brief Design note 2). |
| D-M4-02-2 | 2026-10-15 | Layout and copy step: the generator option `sourceFolder=src/generated` puts every generated file below `lib/src/generated/`, so the patterns of M4-01 cover them and the root `analysis_options.yaml`, `tools/ci/lcov_filter.py` and `sonar-project.properties` stay unchanged. The generator's library entry `lib/api_client.dart` becomes `lib/src/generated/generated_api.dart` (the brief named `lib/src/generated_api.dart`, which would sit outside the generated patterns; `pubLibrary` names no file and the generator writes no `library` directive); `lib/api_client.dart` is hand-written and exports it and the hand layer. `.openapi-generator-ignore` keeps the generator away from the hand-written paths and skips its docs and test stubs; the copy step takes only `lib/src/generated/` and the entry, and fails on any other file under `lib/`. `generate-check` (`tool/generate.sh check`) builds a workspace with this package as its only member and the root lock file in a temporary folder, generates, runs `build_runner` and `dart format` there, compares `lib/src/generated/` byte for byte (missing, extra and changed files by path, with the hint `make -C packages/api_client generate`) and analyses the copy with `package:lints/recommended.yaml` only: an error in generated code or any finding in hand-written code fails. The comparison lives in the script, not in `tools/ci/codegen_check.py`, because that tool runs the generator in place and the gate must never write into the working tree. The skeleton test of D-M4-01-11 gives way to `tools/ci/tests/test_api_client_generate.py`. | Brief Design notes 1 and 2; the root rules exclude generated code from analysis, so a separate analysis run is the only place where an error inside it shows. |
| D-M4-02-3 | 2026-10-15 | Tolerant events live in the hand layer: `EventsPageDecoder.decode` and `decodeEnvelope` (the `d` member of a gateway `dispatch` frame) keep an element whose `type` is not in `Event.discriminatorMapping` as `UnknownEvent` with its raw JSON, and decode every other element with the generated serializers. `fetchEvents` reads `GET /api/v1/events` through the client and this decoder, with the security marker of the generated operation, because the generated `listEvents` throws on the first unknown type. A known type that violates its schema still fails. | The generated union cannot skip an unknown member (brief Design note 6). |
| D-M4-02-4 | 2026-10-15 | No `null` stripping: the generated serializers leave absent optional members out, and `test/null_free_test.dart` holds it for the request models of M4 with only their required fields set. A required member typed nullable (`UserSettingPut.value`, a JSON value) is sent as `null` only when the caller leaves it unset, which the server answers with `400 validation_failed`. | Brief Design note 7 asks for the plugin only if the generator emits `null` for absent fields; it does not. |
| D-M4-02-5 | 2026-10-15 | Read retry only for `GET` and `HEAD`, after a network error, a timeout, `429` or `503` except `503 setup_required`; the wait is `Retry-After` (seconds or an HTTP date) or a full-jitter backoff `uniform(0, min(cap, base * 2^n))`. `ReadRetryPolicy.fromBackoff(base, cap)` sets `maxAttempts` to one plus the number of doublings of `base` until the bound reaches `cap` (base 2 s and cap 5 min: nine attempts), so the apps pass only `client.outbox_retry_base` and `client.outbox_retry_cap` and no new key exists. `createApiClient` therefore takes a required `readRetry` parameter, which the brief's signature did not list. A retry runs through the whole interceptor chain again (a new span ID, a new proof from the auth slot). | Brief Design note 4; 15 8: the outbox retries writes. |
| D-M4-02-6 | 2026-10-15 | Every response error reaches the caller as a `DioException` whose `error` is an `ApiProblem` (`ApiProblem.of(e)`), with the problem text as its message, so no request body reaches a log through it; the auth slot sees the raw error before the mapping. The dependency `uuid` the brief listed is not added: span IDs come from `Random.secure`, and the hand layer creates no other ID. | Interceptors of `dio` can only pass a `DioException` on; an unused dependency would only grow the lock file. |
| D-M4-02-7 | 2026-10-15 | `tools/docs/dart_api_docs.py` leaves out a `dart doc` warning whose location line points into generated code (`lib/src/generated/`, `*.g.dart`, `*.freezed.dart`, `*.drift.dart`, the patterns of D-M4-01-4); every error, every warning in hand-written code and every warning without a location still fails. The pages of the generated API stay in the site (about 13,000 pages for `api_client`). | The generated doc comments name model members in brackets that `dart doc` cannot resolve (323 warnings, all in `lib/src/generated/`), so `make docs` failed although the doc lint already excludes generated code; changing generated comments would break the comparison of G4. |

## M6-10 Color vision deficiency check for charts

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M6-10-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave19` at `b380a4e`, branch `feat/M6-10-viz-cvd-check` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. The base lacks the roles `color.viz.*`, their contrast pairing and the preview `viz` with `tokens.py previews --view`, which the plan ruling D-W-13 added on `main` and on which the brief builds ("which it does since M0"). Their viz part enters first as its own commit with the values of `main` (series light `loch.600`, `amber.600`, `spruce.600`, `heather.600`, `gorse.600`, `moss.600`, `rowan.600`, `granite.500`; dark `loch.400`, `amber.400`, `spruce.400`, `heather.400`, `gorse.300`, `moss.400`, `rowan.400`, `granite.400`; axis, grid and area fill as there). The terminal roles of D-W-13 stay out, because no part of this ticket uses them. The base has no section 10 and no D-W table in this file, so the rows stand in a section per ticket, as for M4-03. Text this ticket adds is English, also inside the German chapters and generated pages. | Instructions of the launcher: a ruling on `main` that the brief relies on enters the branch as its own commit; merged layout wins over the brief (D-W-100). |
| D-M6-10-1 | 2026-10-15 | Simulation model: the matrices of Machado, Oliveira and Fernandes 2009 (IEEE TVCG 15(6), doi:10.1109/TVCG.2009.113) for severity 1.0, for protanopia, deuteranopia and tritanopia, in `tokenkit/cvd.py` with the citation. They are applied in linear sRGB: decode, multiply, clip to [0, 1], encode. Tests pin every column against the published numbers, white and greys unchanged, and two samples worked by hand from the table. | One model for all three deficiencies, in linear RGB, as browser developer tools use it; the gate needs a reproducible transformation, not a perceptual study (brief, design note 1). |
| D-M6-10-2 | 2026-10-15 | Distance: OKLab Euclidean distance of the two simulated colours (`delta_e_ok`), computed on the unrounded simulated values. `cvd_min_delta_e_ok` is 0.05 in `design/design-rules.json`, with source and reason there: 2.5 times the just noticeable difference 0.02 of deltaE OK (CSS Color 4), because chart marks are small and the noticeable difference grows as marks shrink (Szafir 2018). A row passes when the distance rounded to three places reaches the minimum, as contrast rows round to two. Measured minimum before and after the adjustment (series; traffic light): light protanopia 0.056 to 0.071; 0.069 to 0.123, deuteranopia 0.012 to 0.094; 0.008 to 0.083, tritanopia 0.076 to 0.076; 0.095 to 0.079; dark protanopia 0.054 to 0.086; 0.071 to 0.075, deuteranopia 0.012 to 0.067; 0.007 to 0.059, tritanopia 0.116 to 0.116; 0.011 to 0.099. | The measure the palette guards already use; the factor is a stated design choice, not a measured value (brief, design note 2). |
| D-M6-10-3 | 2026-10-15 | Changed values (alias steps of the same scale, so hue and series order stay): light `color.viz.series.3` `spruce.600` (`#1b8a83`) to `spruce.700` (`#136f69`), `color.viz.series.6` `moss.600` (`#1b9046`) to `moss.900` (`#063e1a`), `color.warning.fg` `amber.700` (`#8c4f10`) to `amber.800` (`#6b3b09`), `color.danger.fg` `rowan.700` (`#b21622`) to `rowan.900` (`#63080e`); dark `color.viz.series.3` `spruce.400` (`#49c0b7`) to `spruce.300` (`#7cd5cd`), `color.viz.series.7` `rowan.400` (`#fb827a`) to `rowan.500` (`#f15653`), `color.danger.fg` `rowan.300` (`#fcaaa3`) to `rowan.400` (`#fb827a`). Two series per theme is the fewest that passes (exhaustive search over the steps of each series' own scale); no palette step changes, so no other pairing moves. The traffic light failed under deuteranopia in both themes and under tritanopia in dark; the state roles changed because every contrast pairing of banners, badges and live box stays green in both themes and with every accent (`make tokens`). All previews are regenerated, because the state roles appear in the components, layout, mobile and palette sheets too. | Brief, design note 4: fewest series, lightness before hue, guards and contrast green; the state roles only with the banner and badge pairings green. |
| D-M6-10-4 | 2026-10-15 | Pairs (`cvd_pairs`): a `sequence` entry with the eight series and `wrap: true` gives 1 with 2 up to 7 with 8 and 8 with 1; a `pairs` entry gives `success.fg` with `warning.fg`, `warning.fg` with `danger.fg` and `success.fg` with `danger.fg`; all under the three deficiencies, per theme (`light`, `dark`). The accents of M4-03 swap none of these roles, so there are no per-accent CVD rows; a test fails if an accent role joins the checked roles. Rows go to `contrast.md` (section "Color vision deficiency") and to the member `cvd` of `tokens.resolved.json`. Names that differ from the brief (D-W-100): the spec chapter is `25-visualisierung.md` on this base, and the D-W rulings the brief cites are not in this base's DECISIONS. | Brief, design notes 3 and 5. |

A template content with U+0000 is `400` on `/content_md` with code `invalid`, as M1-56/5 does for messages. The `around_seq` window is chosen in Go (`domain.PickAround`, `domain.PageFlags`) from those rows; the flags then follow from whether a side returned more rows than it gave. All values stem from the one statement, so from one snapshot. | Brief notes 3 and 4; the window rule stays a pure, table-tested function instead of SQL arithmetic. |

## M3-13 Search vectors and search_tsquery

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M3-13-1 | 2026-10-15 | The language of 14 3.1 is assigned by `messaging` (`CreateMessage`, `CreateThread`, the reopen line) and `chat` (`Threads.Create`, the main thread at channel creation) inside the unit of work of the insert through `identity.Directory.SearchLanguage` and `InstanceSearchLanguage`. The map key is the first language subtag (the part before the first `-`) in ASCII lower case. A person author takes `search.locale_map[users.locale]`; an actor that is no person (agent, app) takes the instance language without reading its principal; origin `system` takes the instance language, whoever caused the line; import (M8-03) will do the same. A locale without entry gives `simple`. | One rule at one place (identity owns `users.locale`, instance owns `locale.default`); the actor kind already says when no person row exists, which saves a read on the write path (M1-56 D9). |
| D-M3-13-2 | 2026-10-15 | Main threads get the language of the creator of the channel, like every thread, although their title is empty. | 14 3.1 "Threads get the same rule via the creator" holds for every row; a later title (M1-55 forbids it for main threads) would still be indexed in a language. |
| D-M3-13-3 | 2026-10-15 | The validated pair `search.languages` and `search.locale_map` lives in the kernel package `internal/kernel/searchlang` (`kernel.core`, standard library only), with `New`, `For`, `Languages`, `Configs` and `LocaleIndex`. | identity (write side), wire (checks, start check) and later M3-14 and M3-15 (query side, file text) need the same rules; a kernel package is the only place all of them may import. `LocaleIndex` lets start errors name the entry of `search.locale_map` without the configured name. |
| D-M3-13-4 | 2026-10-15 | New configuration kind `map(string)` (`config.KindStringMap`): a JSON object of strings in defaults, the environment and a file scalar; in the file also a YAML mapping of plain scalars. Duplicate and empty keys, non-string values, nested objects, sequences, aliases and tags are problems that name the entry position, never text; the canonical text is the JSON object with sorted keys; `Values.StringMap` and `Bind` into `map[string]string` hand out copies. An empty text is no object and is refused. | `search.locale_map` is a map (14 15); the form follows the list kind (JSON in the environment, native YAML in the file), so `bothy config print` output can be pasted back. |
| D-M3-13-5 | 2026-10-15 | Checks `search_languages` and `search_locale_map`, both through `searchlang.New`: languages are unique, unqualified lower-case PostgreSQL identifiers of at most 63 bytes and never `simple`; map keys are primary language subtags (two to eight lower-case ASCII letters); map values are valid names listed in `search.languages` or equal to `simple`. Messages name the key and the entry position (map in key order), never a value. `search_locale_map` stays silent when `search.languages` itself is invalid, so that problem is reported once. | 14 3.1: the server must not start when a map value is missing from `search.languages`; `simple` is always added by `search_tsquery` (14 4.2), and a mapping to `simple` is the documented fallback. |
| D-M3-13-6 | 2026-10-15 | Start check `pg.CheckTextSearch` in step `schema` of `wire.Serve` for every role, directly after `pg.CheckSchema`: every name of `searchlang.Map.Configs()` must be a text search configuration visible on the search path (`pg_catalog.pg_ts_config`, `pg_ts_config_is_visible`), and `to_tsvector('simple', 'Ä') = to_tsvector('simple', 'ä')` must hold. On failure the start ends with exit code 1 before any public listener; the message names `search.languages entry n` or `search.locale_map entry n`, or the character classification, never a configured name. PostgreSQL 18 behaves as planned on 16: a database with `LC_CTYPE 'C'` does not fold `Ä` and fails the probe; the pgtest image (`en_US.utf8`) passes. | A missing configuration would fail every message insert with a SQL error; a database without case folding would silently never find capitalised umlaut words. Both are operator errors best seen at start (16 7.2). |
| D-M3-13-7 | 2026-10-15 | Columns and function in one transactional migration (`00035_search_vectors.sql`; numbered 00034 on the ticket branch, INT-W22/1); the two GIN indexes concurrently in a second one (`00036_search_vector_indexes.sql`, `-- +goose NO TRANSACTION`), each build preceded by `DROP INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF EXISTS` to remove an invalid leftover of an interrupted run. Index names follow the merged convention `<table>_<column>_idx`. | upgrade.md M4 and ADR 0007 rule 3; a second `bothy migrate up` after an interrupted build succeeds. |
| D-M3-13-8 | 2026-10-15 | Adding the `STORED` generated columns rewrites `messages` and `threads` once under an exclusive lock, against upgrade.md rule M3; this is accepted only while no `server/v*` tag exists (checked at the start of this ticket: none). Existing rows keep `search_language = 'simple'`; no backfill in the migration (M7), no reassignment job in this ticket. A later change of an expression is a release with a maintenance window whose duration M3-16 measures (RK-38). | 03 5 and 14 8 prescribe generated columns; before the first release no instance holds real data. |
| D-M3-13-9 | 2026-10-15 | `search_tsquery(regconfig[], text)` belongs to `bothy_owner` (it runs the migrations); `REVOKE ALL ... FROM PUBLIC` and `GRANT EXECUTE` only to `bothy_app`. This follows the merged convention of the trigger functions of 00002 and 00004 (`REVOKE ALL ... FROM PUBLIC`). The function runs as the caller (no `SECURITY DEFINER`). | Least privilege of M1-12 D4: a new function is executable by `PUBLIC` by default. |
| D-M3-13-10 | 2026-10-15 | The language goes to SQL as text and is cast there (`sqlc.arg(search_language)::text::regconfig`). The queries of `messages.sql`, `threads.sql` and `threads_main.sql` list their columns instead of `*`, `t.*` and `RETURNING *`, so no read carries `search_language`, `search_vector` or `title_search`; the adapters convert the generated per-query row types through one local row struct each. `domain.Message.SearchLanguage` and `domain.Thread.SearchLanguage` are write-only: set before the insert, never read back. Static tests (`TestMessageInsertsSetSearchLanguage`, `TestThreadInsertsSetSearchLanguage`) fail when an `INSERT INTO messages` or `INSERT INTO threads` of the module's query files does not name `search_language`, or when generated query code reads a vector. | pgx needs no codec for `regconfig` and `tsvector`; later writers (M2 agent messages, M8-03 import, V3-01 DMs) cannot forget the language. |
| D-M3-13-11 | 2026-10-15 | Fuzz property of `search_tsquery`: the result equals the OR of the same non-empty sub-queries joined with the tsquery operator `\|\|` (no text in between), and its text form is stable under a second parse. The literal property "`r::text::tsquery = r`" of the brief fails for inputs like `a or b`, because tsquery equality compares trees and the text form of a nested OR has no parentheses, so it parses back left-associated: the same query with another tree, not a loss. The function is unchanged; a fuzz run during the ticket found no counterexample to the stronger property. | Holds what 14 4.1 needs (no error, no changed query) without a false alarm on associativity. |
| D-M3-13-12 | 2026-10-15 | Branch `feat/M3-13-search-vectors` from `int/m1-wave19` (`b380a4e`) in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it (task instruction over the brief's landing step). The base holds the German chapters: `chapter14` is `"14-suche.md"`, the spec changes go into `14-suche.md`, `02-architektur.md` and `16-betrieb.md`, and text this ticket adds is English. `NewChat` takes the directory in `ChatWrites.Directory` and `NewMessaging` a `dir` parameter, both `Identity.Directory` in `Serve`; `wire.NewSearchDirectory` builds the same for worlds without identity. The schema tests of 00026 and 00032 (`TestChatChannelsMigration_Schema`, `TestMessagingMigration_Schema`) list the new columns and indexes. | The merged code names the chapters in German; the directory with the language configuration must be the one identity builds, so the wiring cannot forget it (a directory without `WithSearch` fails closed with `internal`). |

## M1-58 Editing, deleting and revisions

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-58/0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave19` at `b380a4e`, branch `feat/M1-58-messaging-edit-delete` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally, the integration lane ports it. The brief's contract (the operations, schemas and parameters of `openapi.yaml`, the actions of `actions.yaml`, 02 4.3 and 4.5 naming `eventlog.Redactor`) is the same on the base and on `main`, so no contract commit is brought in. The port reuses `messaging.Message` as the `MessageView` of the brief. `Update`, `Delete` and `Revisions` take the caller from the context (kernel `actor`), as the brief's signatures state; `Create` and `CreateThread` keep their explicit actor. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step; one message DTO per module (M1-57 reuses it too). |
| M1-58/1 | 2026-10-15 | No-op rule: an edit whose resulting content equals the stored content byte for byte and whose resolved mention set equals the stored one (order ignored), an empty body included, answers `200` with the current message and its `ETag` and writes nothing: no revision, no event, no new version. Mentions that resolve differently than before (a person left or joined) make an edit with the same content a change, which stores the unchanged content as a revision. | Brief design note 2 point 5; the resulting state is what the edit compares. |
| M1-58/2 | 2026-10-15 | Error precedence of `updateMessage`: `428` (the precondition stage of the chain, before any lookup) > `404` (missing and invisible share the path: the message is read in the visible channels of `Scope(message.read)`, and `authorize` gets an unknown thread hint when none is found) > `403` (author, `write`, `channel_read_only`) > `410` > `412` > `413` > `400`. An `If-Match` that is not a version (`"x"`, a weak tag) is `412` like a stale one, because version 0 never exists. A system line names a person in its author column but is the server's: editing it is `403 forbidden` (the contract lists no `409` for `updateMessage`, and the content CHECK of a system line would otherwise fail with `500`). | Brief design note 2; 06 "Concurrency" item 2; the response list of `updateMessage` in `openapi.yaml`. |
| M1-58/3 | 2026-10-15 | The visible channels of an edit, a deletion and a revision list come from `Authorizer.Scope` for `message.read`, the list action of reading messages; `message.update` and `message.delete` are not read-level actions, which `Scope` refuses. An app needs `messages:read` for them to find its messages, so an app with only `messages:write` gets `404` on its own message. | 07 section 7.4 rule 1; the predicate is the read scope, the decision is `authorize` with the action itself. |
| M1-58/4 | 2026-10-15 | Lock order (design note 4): the thread row through `chat.Threads.LockForMessageChange`, then the message row (`LockVisibleMessage ... FOR UPDATE`), several message rows in ascending `id`, the counters `event_seq` and `audit_seq` last through the phase hooks. An edit locks only the message row; a deletion re-reads the row under its lock and ends without a write when it was deleted meanwhile, so parallel deletions count the message down once and an edit that waited for a deletion gets `410`. | 03 section 6.3; M1-62 locks the thread first too, so a deletion and a move cannot deadlock. |
| M1-58/5 | 2026-10-15 | Redaction (design note 6): `domain.RedactPayload` is a pure function that returns the `eventlog.RedactFunc` for the event form of the tombstone; `message.created` and `message.updated` become `{"message": T}`, `reaction.added` and `reaction.removed` keep `message_id`, `thread_id` and `principal_id` with `emoji: ""` and `count: 0` and drop every other member, `pin.added` and `pin.removed` stay, any other type or a schema version other than 1 is an internal error that fails the deletion. sync writes the payloads in one statement and sets `redacted_at` only when unset; `bothy_app` gets the column right `UPDATE (payload, redacted_at)` on `event_log` in its own migration `00037` (module sync), `message_revisions` is `00038` (module messaging); numbered `00034` and `00035` on the ticket branch, INT-W22/1. | 04 section 2.2 rule 5, R-DM-18; a column grant makes a change of the envelope impossible for the server role. The migration numbers are the next free ones of the base; a parallel ticket that takes them makes the later one renumber at integration. |
| M1-58/6 | 2026-10-15 | Reactions and pins (design note 8): their tables come with M1-59, so the deletion has a marked step where M1-59 adds the two `DELETE` statements and `TestDeleteMessage_RemovesReactionsAndPin`; the redaction of their events is already defined and tested. `messages.search_vector` does not exist on the base (M3-13), so DP-T01 cannot check it yet; the tombstone's empty content makes it empty by itself when the column comes. | Out of scope of the brief; the tests hold what exists. |
| M1-58/7 | 2026-10-15 | The deletion of another principal's message writes one `allow` entry through `auditing.Recorder` in the unit of work, with resource kind `message`, the message id and only `thread_id`, `channel_id` and `author_principal_id`; an own deletion and an already deleted message write none. A refusal is recorded once by the deny path of authz (R-AZ-04). `wire.NewMessaging` takes the write ports as `MessagingWrites` (appender, redactor, recorder). | 05 `message.delete`, column Audit "yes for other people's"; SEC-LOG-4 and 20 section 4.5: no content in audit details. |
| M1-58/8 | 2026-10-15 | Test first: the tests of the redactor, the thread count, the migration, the domain rules and the HTTP use cases ran red before their code (compile failures on the missing symbols, and the migration schema test on the missing table); `TestEventLogRedactionGrant` and `TestEnvelope_RedactedFlag` were written after the grant and with an envelope mapping that already existed, so they passed on their first run. | Honest record of the order of work. |

## M1-59 Reactions and pins

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-59/0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave22` at `a042721`, branch `feat/M1-59-messaging-reactions-pins` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the integration lane ports it. The migration takes the next free number of the base, `00039`. The brief was read from `origin/main`; the base already holds every rule it needs (03 `reactions`, `pins` and the three keys of section 9, 04 section 9 item 3, 05 section 6, the `410` of `addReaction` and `pinMessage` in `openapi.yaml`), so no contract commit was brought in. `make check` runs with `SONAR_SKIP=1` as the launcher sets it. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge and SonarQube steps. |
| M1-59/1 | 2026-10-15 | `reaction.added`, `reaction.removed`, `pin.added` and `pin.removed` carry no `resource.version`; `TestReactionPinEvents_Envelope` checks that the column is NULL instead of equal to the message version. | The core contract wins over the brief's design notes 4 and 6: 04 section 9 item 3 names `reaction.*` and `pin.*` as events without `resource.version`, `actions.yaml` sets `resource_version: false` for all four, and a version the event does not raise would make clients drop later real changes. |
| M1-59/2 | 2026-10-15 | `TestAddReaction_IsNotStatus` sets and changes `trigger_state` with a fixture statement. | The port `SetTriggerState` named by the brief is not on the base (it comes with runs in M2); the test still proves that reactions leave the trigger columns and the version alone and that a trigger change writes no reaction. |
| M1-59/3 | 2026-10-15 | Symbol rule (design note 3) in `adapters/unicode`: refuse invalid UTF-8, then normalise to NFC and check the byte cap and the grapheme count on the normalised form, then refuse Cc, Cs, Zs, Zl, Zp and the Bidi controls and a symbol of only Cf and Mn. The normalised form is stored, compared and returned; every refusal is one error that names no part of the input. The rule also checks the `emoji` filter of `listReactions` (pointer `#query/emoji`). | The stored form is what the byte cap must bound and what the primary key compares, so a composed and a decomposed letter are one reaction; logs never carry the symbol (SEC-LOG-4). |
| M1-59/4 | 2026-10-15 | `github.com/rivo/uniseg` v0.4.7 (MIT) gets its own vendor group `uniseg`; `golang.org/x/text/unicode/norm` comes from the existing group `xtext`. Both are allowed for the adapters of `messaging` and imported only by `adapters/unicode`. | go-arch-lint knows one component for all adapters of a module, so a narrower allowance per adapter package does not exist; the register comment names the one user. |
| M1-59/5 | 2026-10-15 | `pinMessage` locks the thread row (`chat.Threads.LockForMessageChange`) before the message row. | The message row lock of design note 6 serialises only one message, while `pin.max_per_thread` bounds a whole thread; the thread lock makes the bound exact and keeps the lock order of deleting (thread, then message). |
| M1-59/6 | 2026-10-15 | `removeReaction` authorizes `reaction.remove` on the resource `reaction` with the message's thread and channel and the caller as owner; a read-only channel refuses it with `403 channel_read_only` like `addReaction`. The integration tests sit in `internal/messaging/app` next to those of M1-58 and run through the HTTP chain of `test/messagingworld`. | 05 table 4.7 (own reaction) and rule 1.8; the world of M1-58 already holds the delete operation the tests need. |

## M4-04 Design system with tokens, theme and fonts

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-04-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave20` at `ef0e37c`, branch `feat/M4-04-design-system-theme` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also inside German chapters. The base has no section 10 in this file, so the rows stand in a section per ticket. Names that differ on this base and are followed: the chapters are `docs/spec/01-produkt.md`, `15-client-architektur.md` and `23-qualitaet.md`; the section "Integration" of typography.md is "Einbindung", the section "Outputs" of the tool README is "Ausgaben". No core rule or ruling from main was needed. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged layout wins over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-04-1 | 2026-10-15 | `tokens.py build` writes the Dart output twice, to `design/build/dart/design_tokens.g.dart` and to `packages/design_system/lib/src/generated/design_tokens.g.dart` (same bytes), and `tokens.py check` reports either as stale when it differs; `test_check_fails_when_package_copy_differs` and `test_build_writes_the_package_copy_with_the_same_bytes` hold it. The copy is generated code (D-M4-01-4). It compiled against Flutter 3.47.7 without a change to the generator. | A package may not import a file outside its folder (15 3.1 rule 2); one generator writing both copies keeps them equal without a second tool. |
| D-M4-04-2 | 2026-10-15 | `resolveDsContext` never throws: an unknown value of `appearance.theme` or `appearance.reduce_motion` follows the platform (default `system`), an unknown `appearance.density` gives `comfortable` (also `touch` on desktop, which 01 11 does not allow there), a touch platform is always `touch`, and an unknown or absent accent gives `spruce` (`dsDefaultAccent`). A key counts as known when `DsAccent.of` returns a variant other than the default one. | The server rejects unknown values, but a newer server may add one (06 13); the app then shows the default of 01 11 instead of failing. |
| D-M4-04-3 | 2026-10-15 | Color scheme mapping as in the package README: primary `color.accent.bg`, onPrimary `color.text.on-accent`, surface `color.surface.canvas`, error `color.danger.bg`, outline `color.border.default`, the other roles from the nearest semantic token; accent roles (including the focus ring) come from `DsAccent` of the context's accent. Density maps `comfortable` to `VisualDensity.comfortable`, `compact` to `VisualDensity.compact`, `touch` to `VisualDensity.standard`. The focus ring is a `side` of the button themes and the focused border of inputs. Text styles go through `dsTextStyle`, which puts `packages/design_system/<family>` in front of the fallback list and sets the `wght` axis from the weight. | Brief note 3; an app registers the fonts of a dependency under the package-qualified name (seen in the font manifest of the test build), so the plain family of `DsType` alone would fall back to a system font inside the apps. |
| D-M4-04-4 | 2026-10-15 | Fonts from the default branches of `googlefonts/atkinson-hyperlegible-next` (commit `7925f50f649b3813257faf2f4c0b381011f434f1`) and `googlefonts/atkinson-hyperlegible-next-mono` (commit `154d50362016cc3e873eb21d242cd0772384c8f9`) on 2026-10-15; both repositories have no release tags, the font files report version 2.001. The version 5.3.0 in typography.md is the Fontsource package version of the WOFF2 files, not a font version. The files are stored as `<Family>[-Italic]-VariableFont_wght.ttf` instead of the upstream `[wght]` names, because alchemist's font loader passes the URL-encoded asset keys of names with brackets to the asset bundle, which then finds no asset. The OFL texts are stored as the hygiene hook leaves them (trailing spaces of one line removed); `fonts/MANIFEST.json` records the SHA-256 of the stored bytes, the license `OFL-1.1` and the license text of every file; `fonts_test.dart` fails on a changed, missing or extra file, another license or a font without license text. `quality/licenses/allowed.txt` stays unchanged; docs/quality/licenses.md "Bundled fonts" states the rule. | Brief note 5; D-L11 settles OFL-1.1 with the text shipped next to the files; every golden test of a later member loads the fonts through alchemist. |
| D-M4-04-5 | 2026-10-15 | Golden helpers live in the library `package:design_system/testing.dart` (`dsGoldenMatrix`), because several members need the same matrix and test folders cannot be shared between packages. It returns an alchemist `GoldenTestGroup`, so it cannot be written without alchemist, and `alchemist` is a regular dependency of `design_system`; members that import `testing.dart` keep `alchemist` as their own dev dependency for `goldenTest`. Each member has its own `test/flutter_test_config.dart`: fonts loaded, CI goldens everywhere, platform goldens only on macOS, files in `test/goldens/ci/` and `test/goldens/macos/`. The matrix renders with reduced motion and the default accent; for CI goldens it keeps Ahem for the text theme below the scope. deps.dev knows no license for `alchemist` 0.14.1 and `equatable` 3.0.0, so both get a checked `PackageOverrides` entry (MIT, from their LICENSE files) in the root `osv-scanner.toml`, as D-M4-01-2 does for the other pub packages. A side effect is that the apps bundle the Roboto files alchemist declares; removing them is left to the app packaging ticket. | Brief note 6, which allows the regular dependency when `testing.dart` needs alchemist; 15 20. |

## M4-05 Icons in the design system

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-05-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave22` at `a042721`, branch `feat/M4-05-design-icons` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it (task instruction over the brief's landing step). Text this ticket adds is English, also inside German chapters. | The task names the base and forbids the push; the brief relies on no contract change that the base lacks. |
| D-M4-05-1 | 2026-10-15 | The icons ship as `vector_graphics_compiler` assets (`assets/icons/<weight>/<glyph>.vec`), not as an icon font. Measured on 128 glyphs at 16, 20 and 24 px and ratios 1 and 2 against the `flutter_svg` render: vectors 0 % mean alpha difference everywhere, the font 2.97 % mean and up to 47.1 % in one pixel at 16 px x1 (table in docs/design/icons.md "Integration (M4-05)"); 74,608 bytes for the vectors against 34,564 for the font. | Design note 1: the clearly sharper candidate at 16 px wins, the size only decides a tie. `vector_graphics` is maintained by the Flutter team (BSD-3-Clause). |
| D-M4-05-2 | 2026-10-15 | The icon assets, their lookup file, LICENSE and MANIFEST.json are generated by the token tool (`tokens.py icons`, part of `build`); `check` rebuilds them in memory and compares byte for byte, an extra file under `assets/icons/` is stale too. The compiler runs from the workspace lock with the mask, clip and overdraw optimizers off. | docs/design/icons.md names `icon.tokens.json` as the single source; the optimizers need the native path-ops library of the host, and Phosphor glyphs have no masks or clips, so turning them off keeps the bytes the same on every host. |
| D-M4-05-3 | 2026-10-15 | The assets hold every mapped glyph in its mapped weight and in `fill`, and every space icon in `regular`; `tokens.py vendor` copies exactly these SVGs and no longer requires `--sans` and `--mono` (without them `vendor/fonts/` stays as it is). | Design note 3: the golden and `DsIcon(filled: true)` need the active form of every icon; a new icon should need only the Phosphor package. |
| D-M4-05-4 | 2026-10-15 | A spinning glyph rotates once per `motion.duration.pulse`, and only while the ambient `DsMotion` has a positive pulse, which only the full context has (the reduced one is zero); otherwise no `AnimationController` exists. | No dedicated token exists, and adding one would change the token files of M0-01's gate (design note 4). The pulse test also holds during a theme animation, when `AnimatedTheme` lerps a new `DsMotion` instance that is not identical to `DsMotion.full`. |
| D-M4-05-5 | 2026-10-15 | `DsIcon` throws an `ArgumentError` without a non-empty `label` and without `decorative: true` in debug and in release (no separate assert); an empty label counts as none. `DsIconSizeRole` is a new enum of this ticket (the brief names it, the generated code has only `DsIconSize`). `testing.dart` gains `dsPumpIcons`, so `flutter_test` (SDK) becomes a regular dependency like `alchemist`, and `DsIconPlaceholder` is public so the helper can wait for it. The lookup class is `DsIconAssets` with `lookup(glyph, {filled})` and `space(name)`, returning the asset path. | One rule in every build mode; an empty label gives no screen reader text. Vector assets load asynchronously, and every member with icons in a golden needs the same wait. |

## M4-06 Localization with ARB lint

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-06-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave20` at `ef0e37c`, branch `feat/M4-06-l10n` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. The base names the chapters in German (`01-produkt.md`, `15-client-architektur.md`), text this ticket adds there is English, and the term table of 01 section 9 gets the `language-check` markers of `main` as its own first commit, because the generator reads the table between them. The base has no language gate (`tools/docs/language_check.py`, `tools/docs/language-allow.txt`); the port to `main` adds the two lines `path: packages/l10n/lib/l10n/app_de.arb` and `path: packages/l10n/lib/src/generated/app_localizations_de.dart` to the allow list with the comment "German product strings of the `de` locale (D-W-94, M4-06)". The generated term table and the German literals of the tests stand between `language-check` markers instead. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged names win (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-06-1 | 2026-10-15 | `app_en.arb` is the template of `flutter gen-l10n`, German stays the default locale: `preferred-supported-locales: [de]` puts `de` first in `supportedLocales`, and `resolveAppLocale` falls back to `de`. `l10n.yaml` has no `synthetic-package` line: Flutter 3.47.7 rejects the option as removed (output into `lib/src/generated/` is the only mode). | Code and reviewers read English (D-W-94); 01 section 9 makes German the default. |
| D-M4-06-2 | 2026-10-15 | The generation check is part of `make codegen-check`: for each Dart member with an `l10n.yaml` (SKIP `codegen-dart` when none or when `apps` is not selected), `tools/ci/l10n_check.py` copies `pubspec.yaml`, `l10n.yaml` and the ARB directory into a temporary directory, adds an `untranslated-messages-file` to the copied `l10n.yaml` (the generator ignores command-line options while an `l10n.yaml` exists), runs `flutter gen-l10n` there, and fails when the file lists a message or when `lib/src/generated/` differs from the fresh output. The member is never written. | A missing translation breaks the build and a stale generation fails (brief note 2), without touching the working tree. |
| D-M4-06-3 | 2026-10-15 | Emoji are the code points of `Extended_Pictographic` (Unicode 13.0 `emoji-data.txt`, taken from the Unicode Character Database of Perl 5.34), the regional indicators U+1F1E6 to U+1F1FF and U+FE0F, kept in one constant `emojiRanges` of `test/support/arb_rules.dart`. The fixtures write the dash and the emoji as JSON escapes, so the fixture files pass the repository's text rules while the parsed values hold the characters. | The property reserves the unassigned code points of the emoji blocks, so later emoji fall inside the ranges; escapes keep the `text-rules` hook valid for every file. |
| D-M4-06-4 | 2026-10-15 | The term check runs from English to German: an English message with a term of the table as a whole word (case-insensitive, plural `s`) needs the German term of the row or its stem (without a final `en`, `e`, `n` or `s`) in the German message of the same key. Rows with alternatives become one pair each, a parenthesis in the German cell is a note. The generator is a Dart tool (`packages/l10n/tool/term_table.dart`) that `test/term_table_test.dart` also runs as the check, reading the chapter by walking up to the repository root; it reads `docs/spec/01-product.md` or, on the German bases, `01-produkt.md`. | The template is English (brief note 4); a Dart tool keeps the Markdown parsing next to its only consumer without a new Python tool package. |
| D-M4-06-5 | 2026-10-15 | `problemText` maps every code of `x-problem-codes` and these reasons to their own messages: `forbidden` with `capability_missing`, `level_insufficient`, `owner_only` and `view_as_read_only` (07 section 4.1), `channel_read_only` with `channel_archived` and `agent_invocation_denied` with `project_not_ready` (`docs/api/openapi.yaml`); an unknown reason keeps the text of the code, an unknown code gives `problemFallback(code)`. `not_found` reads "Not found or no access" (01 section 6.7). | Brief note 5; the reasons are the ones the specification names with a user-facing meaning. |
| D-M4-06-6 | 2026-10-15 | `L10nFormat` has two duration forms instead of the one `durationShort` of the brief: `elapsed` is the running clock of the live box (`4:12`, `1:02:03`) and `durationShort` the finished duration (`42 s`, `28 min`, `2 h 5 min`). `money` joins number and currency code with a non-breaking space in the order of 01 section 9 (`1,84 USD`, `USD 1.84`) and takes the decimals of the currency from `intl`. `relativeDay` gives "Today", "Yesterday", the weekday for the five days before and the medium date otherwise, all in the given IANA time zone (`timezone` package, BSD-2-Clause). | The screens of 01 show both forms (`Läuft 4:12`, `Lief 28 min`); one function cannot tell a running from a finished run. |
| D-M4-06-7 | 2026-10-15 | First keys: the trigger labels of components.md section 5 with the German texts of that table, the live box state words, reasons, stop button, earlier-steps link and stall banner (plurals) of section 2.1, "Not found or no access", "No access anymore", "Sending", "Sending delayed", "Offline", "Today" and "Yesterday", the problem texts and `problemFallback`. "Sending delayed" ("Senden verzögert") has no wording in 01 yet; the key follows the brief. | Brief note 8: the keys M4-07 and M4-18 need first. |

## M4-10 Device key for macOS and iOS

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-10-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave20` at `ef0e37c`, branch `feat/M4-10-device-key-apple` in its own worktree; this build neither merges locally nor pushes, the PR lane lands it. The base names the chapters `docs/spec/15-client-architektur.md` and `08-identitaet.md`; text this ticket adds there is English. Every rule the brief cites (R-CL-04, R-CL-05, R-ID-11, D-L10) is on the base, so nothing is ported from `main`. Names that differ from the brief: the plugin's Swift sources are `darwin/device_key/Sources/device_key/` (not `darwin/Classes/`) and the core package is `darwin/device_key/DeviceKeyCore/` (not `darwin/DeviceKeyCore/`, D-M4-10-4); the native tests use Swift Testing (D-M4-10-6); the generated Dart code is formatted after generation. | The launcher's instructions take precedence over the brief's landing step; merged and tool layouts win over the brief's names (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-10-1 | 2026-10-15 | `device_key` is one Flutter plugin package with a folder per platform and a Pigeon interface (`DeviceKeyHostApi`: `ensureKey`, `keyInfo`, `sign` (async), `attestation`, `deleteKeys`), not a set of federated packages. Errors cross the channel only as the codes `user_cancelled`, `key_invalidated`, `not_available` and `internal` with a fixed text; the Dart side keeps only the code, and a platform without the plugin (Pigeon's `channel-error`, or `MissingPluginException`) reports `not_available`. A missing key on `sign` or `attestation` is `not_available`; an enclave key the Secure Enclave no longer accepts is `key_invalidated`: the enclave loads any blob and rejects it only when it signs, with CryptoTokenKit `corruptedData` or `objectNotFound` (canceledByUser is `user_cancelled`), and a blob that no longer yields the registered public key counts the same (red team M4-10). Which code a real biometrics change produces is not measured on a device with Touch ID. | 15 3 names one member `device_key`; the Pigeon interface is the boundary federation asks for. Fixed texts keep key material and server addresses out of logs. |
| D-M4-10-2 | 2026-10-15 | The public JWK (`crv`, `kty`, `x`, `y`, base64url without padding) and the RFC 7638 thumbprint are computed in Dart from the coordinates the native side returns; the native side returns signatures as `R || S` (CryptoKit's `rawRepresentation`, so no DER conversion is needed), and the Dart side rejects any other length. | One implementation for every platform (M4-11, M5-01 reuse it). |
| D-M4-10-3 | 2026-10-15 | Access control: `dpop` with `[.privateKeyUsage]` and `AfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly`; `step_up` with `[.privateKeyUsage, .biometryCurrentSet]` when biometrics are enrolled, otherwise `[.privateKeyUsage, .userPresence]`, and `WhenPasscodeSetThisDeviceOnly` on iOS and macOS (the Data Protection keychain knows the same class). Without an enclave the software key's keychain item carries `.userPresence` for `step_up`, with the same accessibility classes, and `hardwareBound` is false. Biometrics count as enrolled unless the device has no sensor or reports `biometryNotEnrolled`, so a key created while the lid is closed or the sensor is locked out is still bound to them (code review M4-10). 15 9 is updated to this. | 08 6.1 requires invalidation on new biometrics, while a Mac without Touch ID must still confirm with the device password (brief note 3). |
| D-M4-10-4 | 2026-10-15 | The core is the Swift package `DeviceKeyCore` (CryptoKit, Security and LocalAuthentication for the biometrics check) inside the plugin's Swift package folder, `darwin/device_key/DeviceKeyCore`, and the plugin package depends on it by the path `DeviceKeyCore`. The plugin uses Swift Package Manager only (enabled by default in Flutter 3.47.7), without a podspec. The shared access group of the `dpop` key comes from the Info.plist key `DeviceKeyAccessGroup`, read when the plugin registers; the extensions read the same key. | Flutter links a plugin's Swift package through a symbolic link, so a path leaving its folder (`../DeviceKeyCore`) would resolve beside the link; the extensions (M5-04, M5-06) add the same core package (15 9). The pinned Flutter no longer needs CocoaPods for plugins (brief note 4). The plugin registrar takes no arguments, so the build configuration reaches the plugin through Info.plist. |
| D-M4-10-5 | 2026-10-15 | `testHardwarePathOnAppleChip` runs only where `SecureEnclave.isAvailable` and otherwise reports the skip `secure enclave not available on this host`; it signs only with the `dpop` key. `testKeychainRoundTrip` skips when the test process cannot write to the Data Protection keychain (`errSecMissingEntitlement` for an unsigned test binary). `darwin/run_ios_sim_tests.sh` prints a SKIP line and exits 0 on a host without Xcode and its simulator; with Xcode a missing iPhone simulator fails. The enclave form of the step-up key is tested through the policy the manager passes to the enclave (`KeyPolicy.policy`), because creating an enclave key with `WhenPasscodeSetThisDeviceOnly` needs an unlocked user session, which a build host reached over ssh does not have (`errSecInteractionNotAllowed`). | CI runners are virtual machines without an enclave (brief note 5); the hardware path counts as proven only on a host with one. |
| D-M4-10-6 | 2026-10-15 | The Swift tests use Swift Testing instead of XCTest, and the first entry of `platform-tests.json` is `["sh", "darwin/run_core_tests.sh"]` instead of a bare `swift test`: the script passes the Swift Testing paths of the command line tools when no Xcode is installed and runs plain `swift test` otherwise. | The command line tools ship Swift Testing but no XCTest, so a Mac with an Apple chip and without Xcode can prove the Secure Enclave path; Xcode 16 on the `macos-15` runner runs Swift Testing with `swift test` and `xcodebuild test`. |

## M2-26 Outgoing webhooks after Standard Webhooks

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-26-0 | 2026-10-15 | The ticket is built on `feat/M2-26-webhooks-outgoing` from `int/m1-wave19` (`b380a4e`) in its own worktree and is not merged locally; the PR lane ports it. The system action `webhook_out.transition` and R-AZ-27 (05 section 4.11, D-W-104), which main has and the base lacked, come over as the first commit of the branch (catalogue entry, rule row, generated tables and Go catalogue, the system row of the requirement table). The base still holds the German chapter files; this ticket writes its additions to them in English (owner rule "everything English"), so the PR lane can carry them to the English chapters of main unchanged. | Launcher task over the brief's merge step; Design note 1 a names the missing spec state, which the task allows to port. |
| D-M2-26-1 | 2026-10-15 | The consumer creates delivery rows only for `active` and `paused` subscriptions; a `disabled` subscription gets none, so events of that time are not delivered (webhooks.md 5 point 4). | A disabled target must not pile up rows; a re-enable continues with the remaining pending rows and new events. |
| D-M2-26-2 | 2026-10-15 | The delivery quota pauses on the crossing: when an insert brings an `active` subscription from below `webhook.max_pending_per_subscription` to it, the subscription becomes `paused` (cause `max_pending`); rows keep being created while paused; the open count is read once per round with a bound and tracked in the memo of the round. | No event is lost, and a resumed subscription with a large backlog is not paused again before it drained and filled up again. |
| D-M2-26-3 | 2026-10-15 | One job kind `apps.webhook_deliver` with only the subscription as argument serves every trigger (round, chain, retry, lease wake-up, owner resume, redelivery, `app.resume`); an attempt claims the due head under the subscription row lock, takes a lease of `webhook.delivery_timeout` with a wake-up job, sends outside any transaction and records in a second unit of work, discarding the result when the head is no longer pending with the claimed attempt. | Strict order per subscription with at most one attempt in flight; a crash after the send leads to a second send with the same `webhook-id` (at least once). |
| D-M2-26-4 | 2026-10-15 | `webhook_subscriptions.signing_key_kid` names the active key that signed the last accepted delivery; at creation the active key. | 03 requires the column; it then names the key a receiver needs most. |
| D-M2-26-5 | 2026-10-15 | A gap is detected at the head (its event is no longer in the log: every pending row below the smallest retained seq becomes `dead` with `event_expired`) and from the lost range of a consumer round (`sync.ConsumerLossHandler`: every live app subscription becomes `disabled`, all its pending rows `dead`); both disable with cause `gap` and name the lost range in the audit entry. | Among existing rows the head rule finds exactly the due deliveries; the loss of the consumer itself produces no rows, so only the round can see it. A re-enable never loops. |
| D-M2-26-6 | 2026-10-15 | Statuses `pending`, `succeeded`, `failed` (after `410`, terminal), `dead` (after `webhook.retry_max_age`, `not_authorized`, `not_subscribed`, `event_expired`); `last_error` from the closed list `timeout`, `connection_failed`, `tls_failed`, `address_blocked`, `host_not_allowed`, `http_status`, `throttled`, `gone`, `not_authorized`, `not_subscribed`, `event_expired`; throttles (`429`, `502`, `503`, `504`) are retried but not counted; every other status, `3xx`, timeouts and refused or failed connections count. | webhooks.md 4 separates throttle and failure; the response body is never stored (03). |
| D-M2-26-7 | 2026-10-15 | Backoff after the n-th failed or throttled attempt: uniform in [base, min(cap, base * 2^(n-1))] with an injected random source; for a throttle with a valid `Retry-After` the larger of backoff and `Retry-After`; no retry after `created_at + webhook.retry_max_age` (cut to it), and a failure at or after it makes the delivery `dead` and the subscription `disabled`; the deadline is checked only after a failed attempt. Deliveries take `created_at` from the clock of the service. | Exponential with full jitter between base and cap (webhooks.md 4 point 1); a head that waited in a paused subscription still gets its attempt. |
| D-M2-26-8 | 2026-10-15 | Entries of `webhook.allowed_hosts` have the form `host[:port]` (without a port only `443`); an address must parse, use `https`, have no user info, fragment or opaque part, and its canonical host and port must match an entry (`domain.ValidateTarget`): host or port outside the list is `400 webhook_host_not_allowed`, every other defect `400 validation_failed` at `/url`. The same rule checks manifest addresses (replacing the host-without-port comparison of D-M2-24-5) and is the host policy of the webhook egress client. | One rule for manifests, subscriptions and the egress client; a list changed after a restart blocks old subscriptions at delivery (`host_not_allowed`, counted). |
| D-M2-26-9 | 2026-10-15 | The types an app subscription may name are the keys under `webhooks:` of openapi.yaml minus the types with `receivers: agent` in actions.yaml, as a hand-written table in `domain` held equal to both files by `TestAppSubscribableMatchesContract`. | webhooks.md 7 lists the agent-only rows; a test catches a drift of either file. |
| D-M2-26-10 | 2026-10-15 | Signing set: `v1a` with every signer of `instance.Keys.Signers(webhook_out)` (active first, then retiring keys whose `not_after` lies ahead); `v1` with the key of `hmac_generation` and, inside `webhook.secret_rotation_overlap`, the previous one. Header entries separated by one space, `v1a` first in signer order, then `v1` newest generation first, standard base64 with padding. HMAC key = `HMAC-SHA256(master, "webhook-v1:" + canonical subscription id + ":" + decimal generation)`, the master key the hex-decoded value of `webhook.hmac_master_key_ref`; the secret shown once is `whsec_` + standard base64 of the key. | webhooks.md 2.1; the form the reference libraries decode. |
| D-M2-26-11 | 2026-10-15 | A published `webhook_out` key is `whpk_` + standard base64 of its DER-encoded SubjectPublicKeyInfo (RFC 8410); `getWebhookKeys` lists `next`, `active` and `retiring` keys ordered by state and kid with `not_after` only when set and the caching header of `getJwks`. | The form of the example under `getWebhookKeys`; a receiver decodes it with any standard library. |
| D-M2-26-12 | 2026-10-15 | `docs/api/webhook-test-vectors.json` is the contract of every verifier: derivation cases `{master_hex, subscription_id, generation, derived_hex}` (derived secrets only as `derived_hex`, because a derived value cannot carry the `EXAMPLE` marker) and signature cases `{name, webhook_id, timestamp, body, ed25519_seed_base64, public_key, hmac_secrets, signature_header}` with `ed25519_seed_base64` and `public_key` as lists in signer order; generated once by `TestWriteWebhookTestVectors` from fixed seeds; every `whsec_` value carries `EXAMPLE`. | G10; two `v1a` signers need lists; no gitleaks allowlist entry. |
| D-M2-26-13 | 2026-10-15 | `event_type` is not a column of `webhook_deliveries`: the delivery list reads it from the event log while the event is retained and omits it afterwards; `WebhookDelivery.event_type` left `required` in openapi.yaml. | The core contract wins over the API document (spec gap, reported). |
| D-M2-26-14 | 2026-10-15 | `redeliverWebhook` makes a `pending` row due now; a finished row becomes `pending` with `last_error` and `response_code` cleared, `attempt` kept and `created_at` now (a new retry window); the same `webhook-id`; an event no longer in the log is `409 conflict` with `reason: event_expired`; the operation gains the `409` response. | webhooks.md 4 point 4: a redelivery is a new delivery of the same event. |
| D-M2-26-15 | 2026-10-15 | `installApp` with `delivery.mode = webhook` creates one subscription (url `events_url`, the subscribable event subscriptions of the installation, no HMAC, created by the installing owner) in the install transaction after `webhook_out.manage`, with its own audit entry; upgrades never change subscriptions; uninstall disables them; a suspended installation gets rows and sends nothing; `app.resume` makes the heads of its active subscriptions due (`ResumeInstallation`). | 11 sections 4.1 to 4.3. |
| D-M2-26-16 | 2026-10-15 | Keys `webhook.max_consecutive_failures`, `webhook.retry_backoff_base`, `webhook.retry_backoff_cap` (chapter 03) and `webhook.max_pending_per_subscription`, `webhook.retry_max_age`, `webhook.secret_rotation_overlap`, `webhook.hmac_master_key_ref` (chapter 11) in `configkeys_webhook.go`; `webhook.delivery_timeout` and `webhook.allowed_hosts` stay where M1-32 and M2-24 registered them; `webhook.receiver_tolerance` is no server key. Cross-checks `webhook_backoff_order` and `webhook_retry_within_retention`. New generated secret `webhook_hmac_master_key` (generator `key`, service `server`), resolved once at the start, so a missing key ends the start. | Design note 15; a retry needs its event in the log. |
| D-M2-26-17 | 2026-10-15 | Span `webhook.deliver` (CLIENT) with `server.address`, `http.response.status_code` and `bothy.outcome` from `succeeded`, `throttled`, `gone`, `failed`, `blocked`; logs `webhook delivery failed` (info) and `webhook subscription state changed` (warn) with the new attribute `bothy.webhook_subscription.id`, `bothy.event.seq`, `bothy.event.type`, `bothy.reason` and `server.address`; no new metric. | signals.md 5.2 and 5.3; never path, query, body or secret. |
| D-M2-26-18 | 2026-10-15 | Field codes `not_subscribable`, `not_granted`, `hmac_not_enabled` (and `too_few_items`, `invalid` for an empty or duplicate list); conflict reasons `app_not_installed`, `one_time_secret` (from the Idempotency-Key stage) and `event_expired`. | Design notes 2 and 13; clients treat unknown codes like `invalid`. |
| D-M2-26-19 | 2026-10-15 | Agent subscriptions are not delivered: `webhook_out.deliver` answers `forbidden subject_kind_not_allowed` for every principal other than an app, and `listAgentWebhookSubscriptions` and `createAgentWebhookSubscription` stay under `later` without a ticket. | Agent delivery needs `agent.invoke` and a fresh invocation token per delivery through M2-08 (plan gap, reported). |
| D-M2-26-20 | 2026-10-15 | `listWebhookDeliveries` lists newest first by `(event_seq, id)` descending with a cursor bound to subscription and status filter. | The latest attempts matter most to an owner. |
| D-M2-26-21 | 2026-10-15 | Names that differ from the brief: the methods of `apps.Webhooks` take the caller `actor.Actor` like `apps.Apps`; `PublicKeys` authorizes `instance.discovery.read` like `JWKS`; the delivery decision lives in `Authorizer.authorizeDelivery` with the gate `domain.DeliveryGate` and the requirement marker `domain.Delivery`; the egress refusal reaches the pure classification as `domain.BlockedError`; signing keys and the job runtime start after the apps step and are bound later (`wire.Apps.BindKeys`, `BindJobs`). A scope removed through `setAppScopes` also removes the event subscription that needs it (M2-24), so the delivery ends `dead` with `not_subscribed` before the rights check; with the subscription kept the rights check ends it with `not_authorized` (`TestDeliveryScopeRemovedIsDead` covers both). | The merged code wins over the brief (D-W-100); 02 start order and the consent rules of M2-24 stay. |
| D-M2-26-22 | 2026-10-15 | The webhook scenarios live in `services/server/test/appsworld` (`scenarios_api.go`, `scenarios_delivery.go`) and the packages `app`, `adapters/httpapi` and `adapters/postgres` of apps call them, so each package is measured by the scenarios that run through it (the pattern of D-M2-24-23). The golangci exclusion of `gocognit` and `goconst` for test code covers `test/` as well as `*_test.go`, matching `sonar.test.inclusions`. | `make coverage` measures each package only by its own tests; code under `test/` is test code (02 Code-Layout), which the analysis already treats so. |
