## Rulings from main: D-W-115 and D-W-116

These two rows of section 9 of the ruling log on `main` are copied verbatim (only a link to a
brief that this base does not carry is written as a path), because M4-08 builds on them; the base
has no section 9.

| ID | Decision | Reason | Location |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-W-115 | The apps read eight configuration keys that steer their own behaviour but have place `Server` in the configuration reference, because no API operation delivers them: `sync.reconnect_backoff_base`, `sync.reconnect_backoff_cap`, `sync.typing_min_interval`, `sync.typing_ttl`, `sync.max_focused_threads`, `client.cache_max_age`, `client.outbox_retry_base` and `client.outbox_retry_cap`. `AppConfig` of `packages/domain` (M4-08) carries them with the default of the reference next to the keys of place `App`, and a test fails when one of them leaves the reference or changes its place. The server stays authoritative where it checks a value itself (for example a `focus` with more than `sync.max_focused_threads` threads is rejected). Limits that `getInstance` delivers in `InstanceLimits` (`file_max_upload_bytes`, `voice_max_duration_ms`, the message limits) are read from the server and never from `AppConfig`. | M4-02, M4-09, M4-12 to M4-15 need these values; generating `AppConfig` only from place `App` would leave them without a source, and a literal in code would break the rule that limits are named keys. An API field for them can replace this list later without changing the callers. | `docs/briefs/M4-08-domain.md` (on `main`), [operations/configuration.md](operations/configuration.md), D-W-114 |
| D-W-116 | The briefs of M4-11 to M4-38 change the column "Depends on" of nine M4 tickets: M4-15 also depends on M4-02, because its executors call the generated API and classify `ApiProblem`; M4-18 also depends on M4-11 (and through it M4-10), because the app shell registers the device keys of macOS and Windows; M4-24 also depends on M4-21, because its shortcuts stop runs, toggle the live box and are tested against approval cards (R-PR-04); M4-28 depends on M4-27 instead of M4-26, because the guided flow of 10 11 starts in the grant wizard; M4-30 depends on M4-25 instead of M4-18, because the notification note and "Start at login" live in the settings; M4-31 depends on M4-25 instead of M4-18, because it makes the crash report switch of the settings visible; M4-32 depends on M4-22, M4-24, M4-28, M4-29 and M4-33 instead of M4-21, so that its checklist covers the chat, settings and administration screens A1 to A6 and, through M4-33, the notification area of M4-30 (M4-36 adds the terminal rows and M4-37 the rows of screen A8, each into its section of the skeleton page `docs/quality/accessibility-checklist.md` that the brief package creates, so no build ticket has to create the page or its navigation entry); M4-33 depends on M4-20 and M4-30 instead of M4-19 and M3-18 (which M4-30 implies through M3-23), because the thread menu sits in the thread header and its test needs the notification stream and the fake's notification decision of M4-30 (and through it the settings entry and the step-up dialog of M4-25); M4-34 drops M4-21 and M4-30, which M4-24 and M4-33 now imply. `AppConfig` also carries `auth.device_pending_ttl` and, added by M4-36, `terminal.max_session_duration` with the rule of D-W-115, because the `Device` and `TerminalSession` schemas name no expiry and screens E4 and D11 show the remaining time (display only; the server decides). The cost figures of screen A5 come from `getCostStats` (M6-05) and join the agent page of `admin_ui` with the statistics views of M6; M4-28 builds the backend switch, the guided flow and the agent tokens. | A brief whose tests or screens need a ticket outside its closure could start before that ticket is merged and could only stop; The cost figures belong with the statistics of M6 (M6-05 and the views built on it), so M4-28 does not wait for them. | [briefs/README.md](briefs/README.md), [PLAN 10](PLAN.md), D-W-114 |

## M4-08 Domain package

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-08-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave20` at `ef0e37c`, branch `feat/M4-08-domain` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane lands it. The rulings D-W-115 and D-W-116 come first from `main` as their own commit (section "Rulings from main"). Text this ticket adds is English, also in the German chapter `docs/spec/15-client-architektur.md` (the brief names `15-client-architecture.md` of `main`). | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged names win over the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-08-1 | 2026-10-15 | Typed IDs are extension types over the canonical lowercase UUID text that implement `Object`; besides the twelve kinds of the brief there are `ForumTagId`, `DraftId` and `QuietHoursId`, because those entities have IDs of their own. `parse` takes the 36-character form in either case; its `FormatException` names only the length of the input. `Uuid7Generator` takes the clock and the randomness as functions; `rand_a` is a counter seeded with 11 random bits at each new millisecond, incremented within it, and on overflow the timestamp advances by one, so IDs of one generator are strictly increasing, also when the clock goes back. | Note 1 of the brief; a seed below half the range leaves at least 2048 IDs per millisecond before an overflow (RFC 9562 6.2 method 1). |
| D-M4-08-2 | 2026-10-15 | Every enum carries the wire code and a member `unknown` (code empty) that `fromCode` returns for any other text; `AgentRuntimeState.unknown` is the value of the spec itself and the fallback. `test/enums_test.dart` maps every enum of the entities to its schema or property in `docs/api/openapi.yaml` and fails when a value of the spec has no member. `SystemCode` also has `agent_session.resume_failed` of 03, which the API schema of this base does not list yet. | Note 2 and requirement 17 of the brief (06 13 item 3). |
| D-M4-08-3 | 2026-10-15 | The event catalogue is generated by `tools/catalog/event_catalog_dart.py` from the `events:` list of `docs/spec/actions.yaml` into `lib/src/rules/event_catalog.g.dart`, because `action_catalog.py` has no output hook; `make event-catalog` writes it and `make codegen-check` compares. `resourceKind` is the leading word of `resource`. `decideApplyForEvent` returns `unknownType` for a type the catalogue does not know; a type that carries `resource.version` but arrives without one is not applied (`ignoreStale`), because only a greater version may change the object. | Notes 3 and 5 of the brief, 04 9 item 3. |
| D-M4-08-4 | 2026-10-15 | Grouping: text and voice are one kind class, run anchors and cards each their own, system lines never group, and a negative time difference does not group. Forum order: descending by `lastActivityAt` (or `createdAt` for `ForumSort.created`), ties by ID descending so every device shows the same order; `ForumSort.unknown` sorts by activity. Cost labels: `CostLabel.finalAmount` stands for the brief's `final`, a reserved word in Dart; an unknown billing mode is labelled information only. | Note 4 of the brief, 01 6.1 and 6.7. |
| D-M4-08-5 | 2026-10-15 | `AppConfig` is the client's single source of the values of place `App` and of the server keys of D-W-115 and D-W-116 (`auth.device_pending_ttl` included; `terminal.max_session_duration` follows with M4-36). `tools/configdoc/app_config.py` reads the generated block of section 4 of the configuration reference by column position, derives the kind from the default (`boolean`, `duration`, `bytes`, `byteRate`, `bitRate`, `pixels`, `integer`, `decimal`, `list`), keeps the server keys as a constant with the reason, fails when one is missing or has another place, and writes `lib/src/config/app_config_keys.g.dart` with the table and one typed getter per key named after the key without its first segment (a collision fails the generation). `make app-config` writes it; `make codegen-check` compares whenever `packages/domain` is tracked, for every component selection, because the check needs only Python. | Note 6 of the brief; reading by position works for the German and the English reference alike. |
| D-M4-08-6 | 2026-10-15 | `mergeReadState(local, incoming, reset: false)`: an incoming state with an older version changes nothing; a newer one keeps `lastReadSeq` monotonic (the higher position stays) and brings the server's counters; `reset: true` marks the result of `markThreadUnread`, which with a newer version replaces the local state. `ReadState.unreadCount` is optional: the snapshot (`UnreadThread`) delivers it, `ReadState` of the API carries only `mention_count`. `UnreadCounter.resetFrom` takes the thread's stored `last_message_seq` and `unread_floor_seq` and, without a delivered unread count, computes it as 03 `read_states` defines it (`last_message_seq - GREATEST(last_read_seq, unread_floor_seq)`); messages it saw above that position stay counted on top. `MessageUpdated` and `MessageDeleted` carry the previous local copy of the message, so an edit counts only a mention that was added or removed, and a deletion takes a mention back, also one the server counted. | 04 14, 03 `read_states` and requirement 13 of the brief (code review of M4-08); a `read_state.updated` on another device carries no marker of a reset (see the report). |
| D-M4-08-7 | 2026-10-15 | The file entity is `FileMeta`, so it never clashes with `dart:io` `File` in the apps; `copyWith` of the entities cannot clear a nullable field (a new object does). The package has no runtime dependency (`meta` and `collection` are not needed); `yaml` is a dev dependency for the tests that read the specification. | Note 2 of the brief and the dependency limit of its scope. |

## M8-02 CDN fetch with host allow list

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M8-02-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave21` at `8b981ed`, branch `feat/M8-02-cdn-fetch` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. The base has no section 10 in this file, so the rows stand in a section per ticket like the earlier tickets. Brief, spec and REUSE were read from `main`; the brief relies on no core rule and no contract change, and chapter 22 already describes the behaviour (6.5, 14, 16), so no spec text changes. Additions to the names of the brief (D-W-100): `CDNConfig` also carries `MaxRetries`, `MaxRateWait` (the `429` limits of requirement 16), `UserAgentVersion` and `Egress` (as in `Config` of M8-01, so that only a test rule with `allow_private` opens the loopback fake); `AttachmentRef.URL` is a `SignedURL`, so that a printed reference never shows the signature; the one request construction of the package moved into `newGet`, which the API client and the fetcher share, because `TestClientHasOnlyGet` allows exactly one; the fetcher also returns `*CallError` (op `GET <scheme://host/path>`) and the `429` sentinels of the client. | The task names the base and the lane (precedent D-M8-01-0); the brief allows following the code where names differ, and each addition keeps a rule of the brief provable. |
| D-M8-02-1 | 2026-10-15 | One `platform/egress` client per fetcher with a host policy of exactly the entries of `import.discord.cdn_hosts` over `https`, `RedirectNever`, `Timeout` = `import.http_timeout` and `MaxResponseBytes` = `import.attachment_max_bytes` plus one, instead of one transport per allowed host (REUSE 5.2). The fetcher checks every link against the same list before the request, so `egress.allowed_hosts` adds no CDN host. | The per-request host policy gives the same guarantee: no connection is shared across a host the policy did not allow, because the pool keys connections by scheme, host and port. |
| D-M8-02-2 | 2026-10-15 | Re-read rule: on `403` or `404` the body is closed unread, `MessagesAround(channel, message, 1)` reads the message once, the attachment with the same ID (also in a forwarded message snapshot) gives the fresh link, which passes the URL check and the declared size check again, and is fetched once. A missing message or attachment, or a second `403` or `404`, is `ErrCDNGone`; `5xx` and other statuses are a `*CallError` without retry. A `429` waits like the read client (`retry_after`, else `Retry-After`; a missing value counts as `import.discord.max_rate_wait`, a longer wait ends with `ErrRateWaitTooLong`); its body is read only for that value. | 22 6.5 item 3 asks for exactly one re-read and one more try; the caller writes the jump-link line for a failed attachment (22 6.5 item 6, M8-04). |
| D-M8-02-3 | 2026-10-15 | Port rule: a link matches an entry when its lower-cased host without one trailing dot equals the entry and its port equals the entry's port, `443` when the entry names none; an explicit `:443` matches an entry without a port. Entries are DNS names with an optional `:port`, never IP literals or wildcards. | Production entries name no port; tests write the fake's port into the entry as `host:port` (Design note 1). |

## M7-24 Token output for TypeScript

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M7-24-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave21` at `8b981ed`, branch `feat/M7-24-tokens-typescript` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. The brief relies on no core rule and no D-W ruling that the base lacks (25 section 10 item 3 is on the base), so nothing is ported first. The base has no section 10 and no D-W table in this file, so the rows stand in a section per ticket, as for M6-10. Names that differ from the brief (D-W-100): the spec chapter is `25-visualisierung.md` on this base. | Instructions of the launcher; merged layout wins over the brief (D-W-100). |
| D-M7-24-1 | 2026-10-15 | Shape and name mapping of `design/build/ts/tokens.gen.ts`: `export const light = {...} as const` and `export const dark = {...} as const satisfies Theme` with every token of type colour of the theme contexts (the roles of `DsColors`; the accent variants of `DsAccent` stay out, the themes carry the default accent), nested by role below the dropped prefix `color`; a segment with `-` or `_` becomes camelCase (`bg-hover` to `bgHover`, `on-sidebar-muted` to `onSidebarMuted`, `area-fill` to `areaFill`), a numeric segment stays a numeric key (`viz.series[1]`); a role that would also be a group, a key used twice or a segment without a valid identifier fails the build (exit 2). Values are the hex strings of `tokens.resolved.json`; the translucent roles `scrim` and `shadow` are `{ hex, alpha }`, as there. `type Theme = Widen<typeof light>` widens the literals, so `dark` with a missing or extra role does not compile. `fonts` keeps the families with fallback list (`fonts.sans`, `fonts.mono`), `sizes` the font sizes in px keyed by size (`sizes[12]`), `radii` the radii in px (`radii.md`). The header line `// SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0` comes first because the license gate requires it in every `.ts` file, the next line says that the file is generated and must not be edited. The fonts and their `<family>.OFL.txt` are copied byte for byte from `vendor/fonts/`, whose checksums `vendor/MANIFEST.json` holds; `check` reports a missing, changed or extra copy as stale. | Brief, design note 1 and requirements 12 to 15; keys that need no quoting keep plugin code plain, and numeric keys keep the series order of components.md section 11. |
| D-M7-24-2 | 2026-10-15 | Compile check: the target `ts` of `tools/design-tokens/Makefile`, part of `check` and so of `make tokens`, runs `tsc --strict --noEmit --target es2022 --module es2022 --lib es2022` of TypeScript 5.9.3 from `sdk/typescript/package-lock.json` (the only TypeScript lock of the repository; `make bootstrap-ci` installs it in CI and locally). Without it the target fails with a hint instead of skipping. The unit test `CompileTest` compiles the rendered file and one whose dark theme lost a role; it skips only when that compiler is not installed, because `make tokens` runs the hard check after it. When M7-25 brings the plugin's own lock, the check may switch to that TypeScript. | Brief, requirement 13: a pinned TypeScript of a lock file of the repository; no new dependency. |

## M7-10 Server release chain with a trial release

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M7-10-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave20` at `ef0e37c`, branch `feat/M7-10-server-release-chain` in its own worktree; no local merge, no push and no pull request from this build, the PR lane lands it. The base names the chapters in German (`18-release.md`, `release.md` with sections 2 "Server" and 5) and has no section 10 here, so the rows stand in this ticket section and the added text is English. The brief's "release.md 2.1 and 5" are sections 2 (with the new 2.1 and 2.2) and 5 on this base; the job `portability` it lists does not exist on the base. `docs/RISKS.md` and `docs/OPEN-QUESTIONS.md` are missing on this base, so RK-57 is not edited here; its new mitigation is the trial release of D-M7-10-1, for the port to `main`. D-W-127 of `main` is not ported: the brief restates its rules for M7-10 and no code reads it. The jobs this ticket adds or changes in `release.yml` follow D-W-52 of `main` (every job with `environment: release` resolves the tag itself and compares; the server's release set reaches `publish` by artifact id). | Instructions of the launcher override the brief's landing step; merged names win (D-W-100 as the brief states it); the port to `main` stays a small diff. |
| D-M7-10-1 | 2026-10-15 | Trial release (Design note 1): `make release-trial COMPONENT=server` runs `tools/ci/release_trial.py`, which starts `registry:3.1.2` pinned by digest on `127.0.0.1` with a random port, creates a `docker-container` buildx builder in the host network (so BuildKit reaches the registry on `127.0.0.1`), runs `make -C services/server release-build`, pushes `bothy-server` (both platforms, one build) and `bothy-postgres` (one build per platform) by digest, joins the postgres digests, generates a cosign key pair in the temporary directory, and then calls the same `tools/ci/release_chain.py` functions as `release.yml` (`sbom`, `sign --mode private`, `verify --mode private`, `tag`). It checks every image `infra/compose/compose.yaml` names with `${BOTHY_VERSION}` against the signed digest and runs three probes that must fail (changed file against `SHA256SUMS`, unsigned child digest against `cosign verify`, a release without one SBOM). cosign and syft get an empty `DOCKER_CONFIG`, so no credential of the host enters. `TRIAL_PLATFORMS` defaults to both platforms for both images, unlike the brief's default of the host platform for `bothy-postgres`: the merged Dockerfile of M7-01 builds the foreign platform of `bothy-postgres` without emulation (D-M7-01-2). Vulnerability scan and ScanCode are not part of the trial; they are the separate targets `make trivy-image` and `make scancode`. The trial and the target refuse to run when `GITHUB_ACTIONS` is set. | D-W-127; the same code in trial and release is what makes the trial a proof; the merged code wins over the brief's default (D-W-100); a scan result depends on the vulnerability database of the day, not on the chain. |
| D-M7-10-2 | 2026-10-15 | Signing commands (Design note 2, cosign 3.1.3 pinned): private phase `cosign signing-config create --no-default-rekor --no-default-fulcio --no-default-tsa --no-default-oidc --out <file>` (checked to name no service), `cosign sign --yes --key env://COSIGN_PRIVATE_KEY --signing-config <file> --bundle <image>.sigstore.json <ref@digest>` per image index and `cosign sign-blob --yes --key env://COSIGN_PRIVATE_KEY --signing-config <file> --bundle <file>.sigstore.json <file>` per binary and for `SHA256SUMS`; every bundle is checked to carry no transparency log entry and no timestamp. Verification `cosign verify --key <pub> --insecure-ignore-tlog <ref@digest>`, `cosign verify-blob --key <pub> --insecure-ignore-tlog --bundle <bundle> <file>`, `sha256sum --check --strict SHA256SUMS`. Public phase: the same commands without `--key` and signing configuration (keyless with the OIDC token of the job), verified with `--certificate-identity-regexp '^https://github.com/<owner>/bothy/.github/workflows/release.yml@refs/tags/server/v' --certificate-oidc-issuer https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com`, plus `actions/attest` per image digest; the mode follows `github.event.repository.visibility`. `SHA256SUMS` covers every file of the release except the signature bundles. Signing steps stand only in the job `supply-chain` with `environment: release`; `check_runners.py` rule `signing-outside-release` holds that for every workflow (environment name compared without case). | ADR 0062 items 2 and 3 and its confirmation; the deprecated `--tlog-upload=false` is not used; the trial ran these commands against the pinned version. |
| D-M7-10-3 | 2026-10-15 | ScanCode gate (Design note 3): ScanCode publishes no container image, so `tools/scancode/Dockerfile` builds one from `python:3.13-slim-trixie` pinned by digest and the release archive `scancode-toolkit-v32.5.0_py3.13-linux.tar.gz` pinned by sha256 (`ADD --checksum`); the archive's native wheels are linux/amd64 only, so the release job `scancode` runs on `ubuntu-24.04` and an arm64 host runs it emulated. `make scancode` scans `git archive HEAD` with `--license --copyright` and `tools/ci/scancode_policy.py` fails on any file whose detected SPDX expression `quality/licenses/allowed.txt` does not satisfy (`OR` one side, `AND` both, `WITH` the license, `LicenseRef-*` never) and on scan errors, except paths in `quality/licenses/scancode-exceptions.txt`, each with a reason (only text that names or quotes a license). `supply-chain` needs `scancode`, so a finding stops the release before any tag; the report is a release artifact. Calibration: a run of ScanCode 32.5.0 over the tracked tree at this branch found 38 files outside the list, all text that names licenses or reviewed data (documentation, tool manifests and lock files, the OFL-1.1 fonts of docs/design/typography.md rule 5 in `design/` and `tools/design-tokens/`, the Unlicense Noise vectors of D-M2-38-15, the OSV-Scanner overrides, the SPDX header templates of the server's code generators, DCO and CLA in `CONTRIBUTING.md`); `scancode-exceptions.txt` lists these paths with their reasons, and the same report then has no finding. That run used the PyPI package `scancode-toolkit-mini` 32.5.0 natively, because the container was killed twice for memory in the shared Docker VM of the build host; the release job runs the container on an amd64 runner. | licenses.md section 4; a digest-pinned base plus a checksum-pinned archive fixes the image as rule 9 of the brief asks; a gate needs a decision rule, and documentation that lists forbidden licenses must not stop a release. |
| D-M7-10-4 | 2026-10-15 | Tag form (Design note 4): `server/vX.Y.Z` stays. 18 section 2 rule 2 now quotes the Go modules reference as written: the subdirectory prefix of tags and the `/vN` suffix from major version 2 on are stated without a condition, and the reference gives their purpose ("If a module might be depended on by other modules, these rules must be followed so that the go command can find and download the module"); the server module is a main module that no module requires (P-05), so the go command never maps its versions to tags, and its version comes from `VERSION`. | The old text attached the condition "only if loaded as a dependency" to the rule itself, which the source does not say (the plan's risk RK-57). |
| D-M7-10-5 | 2026-10-15 | The public key of the private phase lives at `quality/release/cosign-private-phase.pub` (with a README there). This ticket does not create it; until the owner commits it and stores `COSIGN_PRIVATE_KEY` and `COSIGN_PASSWORD` in the environment `release`, a server release stops at its verification step, before any tag (`release_chain.py verify` names the missing file). | Brief Design note 2 and Out of scope; a missing key must fail closed, not sign with nothing. |
| D-M7-10-6 | 2026-10-15 | `make release-prep` (Design note 5): git-cliff 2.14.2 selects and parses the commits (`--unreleased --context`, `--tag-pattern` per prefix, `--include-path <dir>/**`, for `server` also `docs/api/**`), and `tools/ci/release_prep.py` applies the rule of 18 section 3: a break gives a major from 1.0.0 on and a minor before, `feat` a minor, `fix` or `perf` a patch, other types alone nothing to release; the first release is 0.1.0. `--bumped-version` is not used: in a fixture repository it gave 1.0.0 for a `BREAKING CHANGE` footer on 0.1.0 and a patch for a `docs` commit, and with `no_increment_regex` it ignored a break marked on a `chore!` commit. `CHANNEL=beta` appends `-beta.N` (N one above the highest existing beta tag of that version); stable after betas keeps the version and gets a section that points to the beta sections. The section `Unreleased` becomes `[X.Y.Z] - <date>` under a new empty `Unreleased`; nothing is written on any error; the script never commits or tags. | ADR 0060 item 2 names the rule, and the tool did not follow it in three reproduced cases; git-cliff still does the path and tag selection and the Conventional Commits parsing ADR 0060 chose it for. |
| D-M7-10-7 | 2026-10-15 | `tools/ci/components.py release` also rejects a tag whose component `CHANGELOG.md` has no section `[X.Y.Z]`, so the job `resolve` stops before the gates and the build; `publish` keeps its own check. | Requirement 15 of the brief: a missing changelog section aborts before anything is published; before, `publish` found it after the image was tagged. |
| D-M7-10-8 | 2026-10-15 | `postgres-build` is a matrix of `ubuntu-24.04` and `ubuntu-24.04-arm` (GitHub-hosted in both phases, allowed for release workflows), each pushing its platform by digest and uploading the digest as the artifact `postgres-digest-<arch>`; matrix jobs have no output per leg, so `supply-chain` downloads them by pattern and joins them with `release_chain.py join`, which pushes the index by its own digest (computed from `imagetools create --dry-run`) and compares what the registry stores. Images are pushed by digest only until the version tags. | Brief requirement 13 and 18 section 7 rule 2; `imagetools create` needs a target and a tag would publish a name before the scan. |
| D-M7-10-9 | 2026-10-15 | `services/server/Makefile` `release-build VERSION=<v> OUT=<dir>` writes `bothy_<v>_linux_amd64` and `bothy_<v>_linux_arm64` with the flags of the Dockerfile (`CGO_ENABLED=0`, `-trimpath -buildvcs=false -ldflags "-s -w"`); the image builds stay with the root targets and `release.yml`. | Component contract of 23 section 3; the binaries cover the platforms of the images (18 section 7 rule 1). |

The brief, spec and contract were read from `origin/main`; the base holds every rule the ticket needs (03 `read_states`, `drafts`, `user_settings`, 04 section 14, 05 personal actions and events, R-AZ-21, 01 section 11, 13 section 3.3, the OpenAPI operations and schemas are the same apart from translation), so no contract commit was brought in. The test of the setting registry finds the chapters by their number (`docs/spec/01-*.md`, `13-*.md`) and their section numbers, so it reads the German chapters of the base and the English ones of `main`. `make check` runs with `SONAR_SKIP=1` as the launcher sets it. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step; chapter names differ between the base and `main`. |
| M1-61/1 | 2026-10-15 | A setting that was deleted starts again at `version = 1` on its next PUT; the `settings.updated` of the delete carries the last value with the last version plus one and `deleted: true`. | Brief note 16: clients drop the local entry on `deleted: true`, so the new entry with version 1 applies; keeping a tombstone row only for the version would store a default the server must not store (04 section 14, row Settings). |
| M1-61/2 | 2026-10-15 | `draft.deleted` carries no `resource.version`, although the brief's event table names `version: last + 1`. | The catalogue (`docs/spec/actions.yaml`, ADR 0049) records `resource_version: false` for `draft.deleted`, and the frame conformance (G9) checks every delivery against the catalogue; the core contract is not edited by this ticket. Clients apply it in `seq` order (04 section 9 point 3). The empty final state of a PUT still answers version last plus one in its body. |
| M1-61/3 | 2026-10-15 | The six personal actions of this ticket (`read_state.update`, `read_state.mark_unread`, `draft.read`, `draft.write`, `settings.read`, `settings.update`) are refused to agents and apps by `authorize` itself: the requirement table of authz has override rows `All(Human, Self)` and `All(Human, Self, Level(read))` with the unchanged 05 texts. A person with a personal token passes. An agent or app without read on the thread or channel still gets `404` (visibility comes first). | Brief note 22: permission logic only in authz; the requirement step of M1-28 let an agent naming itself pass `self`. The table already holds overrides by action id (message.create, thread.tags.set). |
| M1-61/4 | 2026-10-15 | For `clear_draft`, `OnMessagePosted` deletes the author's channel draft of `ChannelID` when `CreatedThread` is set and the author's draft of `ThreadID` otherwise; it does not read the channel kind. Only messages of people clear drafts. | Only a forum has channel drafts (`putChannelDraft` refuses other kinds and a channel never changes its kind), so a new thread of a text channel finds none, exactly as brief note 12 requires; agents and apps have no drafts. |
| M1-61/5 | 2026-10-15 | `ReadState.mention_count` counts above `last_read_seq` with `messaging.Mentions.CountUnread`, without `threads.unread_floor_seq`. | M1-60/2: imports write no mentions, so the floor hides none; the list, the answers and the events then count the same way. |
| M1-61/6 | 2026-10-15 | An over-long draft is `413 payload_too_large`, not `400 too_long`. U+0000 in a draft is `400` on `/content_md` with `invalid`; a channel draft in a channel that is not a forum is `400 validation_failed` without a field; field codes are `out_of_range`, `too_many_items`, `duplicate`, `not_uuid_v7`, `not_in_thread`, `not_allowed` (reply on a channel draft) and `invalid`; a setting value whose event would not stay below `sync.event_payload_max_bytes` is `400` on `/value` with `too_large`. | Brief note 8 asks for M1-56's answer to an over-long message (`domain.CheckContent`); PostgreSQL text cannot hold U+0000 (M1-56/5); a setting has no reduced payload, and the appender would refuse the event with `500`. |
| M1-61/7 | 2026-10-15 | The reply target of a thread draft is checked through the new port `messaging.ReplyTargets` (`InThread`, implemented by the messaging service with the rule of `createMessage`); the chat port gains `Threads.InfoForShare` and `ThreadInfo.LastMessageSeq` and `UnreadFloorSeq`; `Structure.ListSidebarStates` of M1-52 is used as it is. The kind and space of a channel draft come from `Structure.GetChannel` after `draft.write` allowed the call. | Brief note 8 asks for the messaging port, and the base port had no reply check; additive changes only. |
| M1-61/8 | 2026-10-15 | `internal/wire` builds the subscribers of sync (`syncapp.Subscribers`) with the module objects of the txn registry and binds the appender in the start step of authz (and in `bothy setup`) next to the rights hooks; an unbound subscriber fails a message with `clear_draft`. The golden file of the registrations gains `subscribe messaging.MessagePosted -> sync`. The test world of messaging takes the option `WithPersonal`, which registers the subscriber of sync before the test subscriber and adds the twelve handlers; its people authenticate through the test authenticator of httpservertest as for M1-56 and M1-60, not with DPoP proofs. | The registry is built before the appender exists (02 "In-Tx-Ereignisse": one list); the world of the base is the shared HTTP world of chat and messaging. |

## M5-01 Device key for Android

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M5-01-0 | 2026-10-15 | Base `int/m1-wave23` at `d2d32fa`, branch `feat/M5-01-device-key-android` in its own worktree; this build neither merges locally nor pushes, the PR lane lands it. Every rule the brief cites (R-CL-04, R-CL-05, R-ID-11, D-L10) is on the base, so nothing is ported from `main`. The base names the chapters `docs/spec/15-client-architektur.md` and `08-identitaet.md`; text this ticket adds there is English. Names that differ from the brief: the Kotlin packages are `devicekey.flutter` (plugin, generated `DeviceKeyApi.g.kt`) and `devicekey.core` (core module), without the product name (02 6); the plugin has its own Gradle wrapper (Gradle 9.3.1 with a pinned distribution checksum) and AGP 9.1.0, Kotlin 2.4.0 and compile SDK 36 of the pinned Flutter's plugin template; the instrumented tests run with `connectedDebugAndroidTest` and the JVM tests with `testDebugUnitTest`. | The launcher's instructions take precedence over the brief's landing step; merged and tool layouts win over the brief's names (D-W-100, D-W-120). |
| D-M5-01-1 | 2026-10-15 | Key parameters (brief note 2): alias `<prefix>.<sha256(scope)>.<purpose>` (lowercase hex, prefix `device_key`, tests `test.device_key.<uuid>`), `KeyPairGenerator` `EC` in `AndroidKeyStore` with `secp256r1`, purpose `SIGN`, digest `SHA256`. `dpop` without user authentication. `step_up` with `setUserAuthenticationRequired(true)`, from API 30 `setUserAuthenticationParameters(0, AUTH_BIOMETRIC_STRONG or AUTH_DEVICE_CREDENTIAL)`, before it `setUserAuthenticationValidityDurationSeconds(-1)`, and `setInvalidatedByBiometricEnrollment(true)`. From API 28 StrongBox is tried first and the key is generated again without it on `StrongBoxUnavailableException`; below API 28 an `SDK_INT` check skips it. A missing step-up key is created only when `BiometricManager.canAuthenticate` accepts the authenticators of the API level, otherwise `not_available`. | 08 6.1, 15 9; the alias keeps server addresses out of the Keystore, as on Apple (D-M4-10-3). |
| D-M5-01-2 | 2026-10-15 | The attestation is the key's certificate chain from `setAttestationChallenge`: the DER certificates, leaf first, concatenated (the channel member is bytes and the DER framing separates them). Android binds the challenge when it creates the key, so `attestation` creates a missing key with the challenge and returns its chain, and returns null for an existing key and where the device does not attest (a chain of one certificate); the app asks for the attestation before `ensure` when it registers. | M4-10 defined the member as bytes without an encoding; returning a chain with an old challenge would look like a fresh attestation. |
| D-M5-01-3 | 2026-10-15 | Device credential rule: from API 30 the step-up accepts strong biometrics or the device credential (the prompt then has no negative button); on API 26 to 29 strong biometrics only (the prompt shows the negative button from Dart), and a device without enrolled biometrics on those levels gets `not_available` for `step_up`. Written into 15 9 and the Android row of 08 6.1. | A per-operation key cannot be unlocked with the device credential before API 30 (brief note 3); the app explains that step-up needs biometrics there. |
| D-M5-01-4 | 2026-10-15 | Signatures: `SHA256withECDSA`, DER converted to `R || S` with strict DER parsing (minimal integers, at most 33 bytes, left padding to 32). `hardwareBound`: from API 31 `KeyInfo.securityLevel` StrongBox or trusted environment, before it `isInsideSecureHardware`. Errors leave with a fixed text and never with a cause: `KeyPermanentlyInvalidatedException` is `key_invalidated`; `UserNotAuthenticatedException` and, from API 33, `android.security.KeyStoreException` with `ERROR_USER_AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED` (what the Keystore throws at `sign` for a step-up signature without the prompt) are `not_available`; below API 33 that case is `internal`, because the class is hidden there. Prompt errors: cancel, negative button and system cancel are `user_cancelled`; no hardware, nothing enrolled, no device credential and lockout are `not_available`; others `internal`. A step-up signature without prompt texts is `internal`. | Brief note 4; the emulator at API 36 showed the `KeyStoreException` path, not `UserNotAuthenticatedException`. |
| D-M5-01-5 | 2026-10-15 | `apps_platform.py` knows the system `android`, which is no host: its commands run on any host when `adb devices` lists exactly one device in state `device`, print `SKIP ... android: no device` without one (or without `adb`) and fail with two or more; `--system android` runs only them. The CI job `apps-android` (public phase, `ubuntu-24.04`, kvm enabled, Temurin 17, `reactivecircus/android-emulator-runner` pinned by SHA, API 26 and 36, `x86_64`, `google_apis`) runs `apps_platform.py --system android` and feeds `ci-ok` like `apps-macos`. `make test` runs `./gradlew -p <module> testDebugUnitTest` for every Gradle module with a tracked `src/test/` under `packages/*/android/`; the jobs `gates` and `sonar` install Temurin 17 and the Android SDK (`android-actions/setup-android`) when such a wrapper is tracked. The run script sets a temporary PIN on an emulator without one (and clears it at the end), because the step-up key needs a secure lock screen; it never touches a physical device's lock screen. | Brief note 5 and its requirement that the JVM tests run in `make test`; an emulator can run on Linux and macOS, so a host check alone cannot select it. |
| D-M5-01-6 | 2026-10-15 | The Kotlin core is the Gradle library module `android/devicekeycore` with its own build file and tests. The plugin library compiles the module's main sources into itself (`sourceSets`), and its standalone `settings.gradle.kts` includes the module for the tests. An app (the push service of M5-05, the share activity of M5-06) uses `DeviceKeyCore.forContext` through its dependency on the plugin project `:device_key` and does not include `devicekeycore` as a second project, which would duplicate the classes. Both build files list the same dependencies (`androidx.biometric` 1.1.0); the plugin adds `kotlinx-coroutines-android` for Pigeon's `suspend` handler. | Flutter includes a plugin as one Gradle project and ignores its settings file, so a project dependency on the module would break every app build that does not include it by hand. |
| D-M5-01-7 | 2026-10-15 | M4-10 had no prompt texts on the channel, so the Pigeon source gains `PromptTextsMessage` (`title`, optional `subtitle`, `negativeButton`) as an optional last parameter of `sign`; the Swift host ignores it. The Dart API stays source compatible: `DeviceKeyClient.sign` takes an optional named `prompt` of the new type `StepUpPrompt`. Pigeon writes `analyzer: exclude: [android/**]` into the member's `analysis_options.yaml` when it generates Kotlin; the folder holds no Dart, so it stays. | Brief note 1; the texts come from `packages/l10n` (rule 8). |
