## M4-14 Sync engine: catch-up and reconciliation

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-14-0 | 2026-10-16 | Base `int/m1-wave30` at `c0ad78b`, branch `feat/M4-14-sync-catch-up` in its own worktree; no local merge, no push and no pull request from this build, the PR lane lands it. Brief and spec were read from `main`; the brief relies on no core rule or contract change that the base lacks (R-SY-14 and R-CL-03 are in the German chapters 04 and 15 of the base, D-W-115 was ported earlier, D-W-100 and D-W-114 are planning rulings), so nothing is ported. The base names the chapters `04-sync-protokoll.md` and `15-client-architektur.md`; text this ticket adds there is English. Names that differ from the brief (D-W-100, D-W-114): the sign-out notice is `SignedOutNotice` (`SignedOut` is an event of `auth`), the REST reads are the port `Fetcher` with `ApiFetcher` over the generated API, `Dispatcher.apply` takes a `DecodedEnvelope` (the raw envelope with the `DecodedEvent` of `api_client`, null when malformed) plus the stored cursor and an `AfterCommit`, `Fetcher.myReadStates` takes a channel because the generated `listMyReadStates` requires `channel_id`, the write path of drafts calls `CatchUpListener.applyDraftPutResult`, and a failed gap fill is retried by `CatchUpListener.onFocus`. The wire mappers (`wire.dart`) and the identity port (`LocalIdentity`, `AuthIdentity`) are files beyond the brief's list. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's landing step; merged names win over the brief. |
| D-M4-14-1 | 2026-10-16 | A gateway page is the run of `dispatch` frames up to the next `cursor` frame or `catchup.complete`; it is applied in one `commitPage` with the `seq` of that frame (never below the stored cursor) and the epoch of the session. In the live phase every durable `dispatch` is its own transaction with `cursor = seq`; ephemeral events apply at once without a transaction. A disconnect drops an unfinished page; its events come again from the stored cursor. | Design note 1; 04 7.1 (the server sends `cursor` at the end of every page) and 04 9 item 2. |
| D-M4-14-2 | 2026-10-16 | `ready` without a stored cursor empties the mirror and applies the whole snapshot with `cursor = head_seq` and the epoch in one transaction and goes `live` at once, because an `identify` without a cursor gets no catch-up and no `catchup.complete` (04 6.3 item 4; red team of M4-14); with a cursor only the parts that are not events apply (active run summaries by version, the counters, the agent presence, the feature flags into a stored instance row). After a whole snapshot the drafts are loaded with `listMyDrafts` when a draft reference is newer than or missing from the local drafts, the read states with `listMyReadStates` for the channels with unread threads, and the instance with `getInstance` (its flags replaced by the snapshot's). | Design note 1; without a cursor the mirror belongs to no known time line; the snapshot names drafts only by version and carries counters without a read state version. |
| D-M4-14-3 | 2026-10-16 | The outbox, the run store and the outbox start are hooks (`onEcho`, `onRunUpdated`, `onLive`), constructor parameters with no-op defaults, not dependencies. `onEcho` runs after the commit for a `message.created` with `client_msg_id` whose author is the person (or when the person is not known yet, after a `resume` in a new process); a truncated `message.created` echoes after its reload. | Design note 2; M4-17 wires them; outbox IDs are UUIDv7 of this device, so an echo of another author never matches an entry. |
| D-M4-14-4 | 2026-10-16 | `access.changed` in the middle of a page applies in its `seq` position (removal inside the page transaction, file bytes and `TargetsRemoved` after the commit) and the rest of the page applies after it. Only `4012` and `session.revoked` of this session stop a page: the page rolls back, the database is wiped (`LocalDatabase.wipe`), `SignedOutNotice(deleteLocalData: true)` is emitted and nothing is applied after that. `4012` reaches the listener as the connection state `signedOut`/`revoked` (`watchConnection`). | Design note 3; 04 8.1 and 8.3 item 2: the gateway withholds only events of removed targets, so dropping the rest would lose events that are never sent again. |
| D-M4-14-5 | 2026-10-16 | `session.revoked` wipes only when its `session_id` is this device's session (`LocalIdentity.sessionId`, the `sid` of `auth`) or the own session is unknown; a revocation of another session of the person (a logout on the phone) changes nothing here. | `session.revoked` has the audience `self` and reaches every device of the person (05 6); the revoked device itself is also closed with `4012` (04 8.5). |
| D-M4-14-6 | 2026-10-16 | Gaps: a `message.created` that continues a loaded range, or arrives in a thread with no loaded range, or lies below every loaded range joins the loaded ranges; otherwise it is stored without joining them, the gap from the end of the highest range below it up to it is recorded, and `Fetcher.range` loads it after the commit (`listThreadMessages` with `after_seq`, page by page); the fetched messages and the whole range up to the new message join the loaded ranges in one transaction. `MessagesDao.watchContiguous` therefore shows only the contiguous range ending at the newest loaded message. A failed fill keeps the gap recorded; `onFocus` retries it. | Design note 4; 04 4: a real gap is missing data, and a thread never opened here shows from its newest message without loading its history. |
| D-M4-14-7 | 2026-10-16 | Personal data: the echo of this device (`updated_by_device_id`) is ignored for drafts, settings and the sidebar; the read state is merged also for the own echo, because `mergeReadState` is monotonic and versioned and the counters come only from the server. A `draft.updated` or `draft.deleted` for a draft the person is typing on this device (`DraftEditing`) is not applied; the next local write wins. | Design note 5 and 04 14. |
| D-M4-14-8 | 2026-10-16 | A page whose transaction fails (a payload that breaks its mapping, a database error) leaves rows and cursor as they were; the listener then applies nothing until the next `ready` or `resumed`, which catches up from the stored cursor. An envelope that does not fit the schema of its type is skipped like an unknown type and the cursor passes it. | A later page must not move the cursor past events that were never applied; `SessionControl` has no close, and a reconnect repeats the page. |
| D-M4-14-9 | 2026-10-16 | When `getSnapshot` fails during a resync, the listener keeps mirror and cursor and retries the snapshot after the reconnect backoff (`sync.reconnect_backoff_base`, `sync.reconnect_backoff_cap`, full jitter) on the same connection; frames that arrive meanwhile wait in memory and apply after the snapshot with the duplicate rule. A disconnect ends the retries; the next session catches up from the stored cursor and gets `resync_required` again. `onLive` runs once per session, also when a `catchup.complete` follows a resync. | Code review of M4-14: dropping the mirror and halting left the app empty on an open connection until the next reconnect; after a restore every client asks for the snapshot at once. |
| D-M4-14-10 | 2026-10-16 | `packages/local_store` gets three methods beyond the brief's list, each with its test: `PersonalDao.deleteSettingIfNewer` and `deleteNotificationPreferenceIfNewer` (for `deleted: true`, 04 14) and `MessagesDao.reactionsOf` (reading the `me` flag of a reaction inside the page transaction, where a stream would wait for the transaction's end). | Fixed requirement 17 and Design note 5 need the deletions; no existing method removes those rows. |
| D-M4-14-11 | 2026-10-16 | `decideApply` decides per event between the version rule and the `seq` rule and drops a versioned type that arrives without `resource.version`; the comparison with the stored version is the conditional upsert of the DAOs (D-M4-12-3), one statement per row. Event payloads, the snapshot and REST answers go through one set of wire mappers from JSON to the domain types; the generated models of REST answers are serialized back to JSON first. The page size for the isolate decision is an estimate over the decoded frames (string lengths plus a few bytes per value). | One rule in one place; the gateway frames are already JSON-decoded by the connection, so their raw size is not at hand. |
| D-M4-14-12 | 2026-10-16 | REST reads that run after a commit (a truncated object, a gap fill, the newest page of a focused thread, the structure of `added` or `changed` targets, drafts and read states after a snapshot) store their result only when, inside the transaction of the write, the target is still held: content needs its channel row and neither the channel, its space nor the thread may have been removed in this process (`SyncContext.removedTargets`); a view of `changed` needs its row. An `access.changed` with `added` (for its spaces and channels and for every thread its structure load lists), a `thread.moved` and a snapshot that lists a target take it out of the removed set (follow-up review of M4-14: a moved-out thread stayed blocked after its new channel became visible). | Code review of M4-14, reproduced: a `getMessage` in flight during `access.changed` with `removed` stored the message and its search row again (04 8.3 item 3, T-DV-01). |
